**Tests.** `Helpers.spec`'s hue-gap check (≥ 30° from every player and status color) does not apply to a color without hue; it is replaced by: the accent is neutral (channel spread ≤ 12), lighter than every player and status color by at least 1.5:1 (closest: Amber 1.65:1, where the shapes also differ), its label is ≥ 7:1 and the lip is ≥ 3:1 on the stage and on a card. The "one accent-filled button per screen" audit rule is unchanged (the accent is still a distinct token from `text`). The pill, toast, Draft and lens specs now check the outlined battleground mark. 0 audit findings on all 120 shots.

## Practice bot difficulty

### D-057 — Easy, Medium, Hard and Extreme bots, all playing fair (Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "easy-medium-hard and extreme difficulty settings for bot play".

**Rule.** A practice match takes a difficulty (`{ op = "Practice", difficulty = "Easy" | "Medium" | "Hard" | "Extreme" }`; absent or anything else is Medium, never a rejection). **Medium is the Balanced bot, unchanged**, so every balance number keeps its meaning: `lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400` prints the same report before and after. Practice stays unrated. Every level decides from its own View only, the same information a human gets: no true support, no opponent plan, no hidden poll, no opponent ground game except what its own polls saw, the opponent's cash only as its Low / Medium / High bracket. **No handicap**: no level gets extra cash, AP or information.

- **Easy** misjudges. Each plan weighs every state's worth by a random factor (log-normal, `BOT_EASY_NOISE` = 0.45), so it spreads effort over the wrong states; the draft reads the map the same hazy way; and half its debate focuses and Choice stances are random (`BOT_EASY_SLIP` = 0.5; a random pick alone costs the standard bot about 20 points of win rate). It still takes every legal turn, raises money and spends it.
- **Hard** (1) predicts the opponent's plan by running the standard bot from the opponent's side of the public board, their cash guessed from their bracket (`BOT_FORESIGHT_CASH` = $2 / $7 / $13), and counts that week's predicted swing against its margins (`BOT_HARD_FORESIGHT` = 1); (2) values a state by how much a point there moves its chance of reaching 270 (a normal approximation of the EV total) instead of expected EV alone (`BOT_HARD_WIN_CHANCE` = 1): ahead, it shores up the close states it leads; behind, it reaches for the ones it trails; (3) picks its debate focus by the odds each topic is won or lost against the opponent's likely focus (`Debate.odds`; half the weight on the topic the standard bot would pick, `BOT_FOCUS_READ` = 0.5).
- **Extreme** is Hard with the win-chance weight pushed three times as far (`BOT_EXTREME_WIN_CHANCE` = 3, `BOT_EXTREME_FORESIGHT` = 1).

**Harness** (`lune run tests/harness/difficulty.luau [N]`; seats alternate, two different candidates a match, fixed seeds; ± is the 95% band):

| Pairing | 400 matches | 1,000 matches | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| Easy vs Medium | 15.5% ± 4.9 | 18.7% ± 3.1 (the reverse seating: Medium wins 79.3%, so Easy 20.7%) | 15–25% |
| Hard vs Medium | 65.0% ± 4.9 | 66.1% ± 3.1 | 60–70% |
| Extreme vs Medium | 77.8% ± 4.9 | 77.5% ± 3.1 | 75–85% |
| Medium vs Easy | 80.2% ± 4.9 | 79.3% ± 3.1 | — |
| Hard vs Easy | 80.2% ± 4.9 | 81.6% ± 3.1 | — |
| Extreme vs Easy | 86.0% ± 4.9 | 87.1% ± 3.1 | — |
| Extreme vs Hard | 61.5% ± 4.9 | 61.5% ± 3.1 | above 50% |

Robustness (400 matches each, a scratch run of `Play.match` against the harness's restricted styles): Medium / Hard / Extreme beat Sandbag 61 / 75 / 85%, AttackOnly 59 / 69 / 80%, RallyOnly 62 / 67 / 80%, NeverPoll 47 / 66 / 75%, TopIssues 47 / 63 / 76%, FundraiseOnly 92 / 93 / 93%. The order holds against every opponent, so Hard and Extreme are not just counters to the Medium bot they predict.

**What was tried.** Foresight alone is harmful (Hard without the win-chance weight: 18% against Medium; counting only the opponent's predicted rallies and fundraisers, 32%): counting the opponent's coming rallies against a state makes an expected-EV bot give up exactly the states the opponent is fighting for. The win-chance weight alone is worth about +6 (56%); the two together +15 (65%), and the stronger weight +28 (78%). Extreme variants that also skipped paid polls, valued money higher (`moneyValue` 0.6) or banked ground game earlier measured within the noise of plain Extreme and were left out. Hard and Extreme fundraise much less than Medium ($10–16 a match against $37) and rally more; that is the weight at work, not a bug.

**Server cost.** One decision (Lune, this Mac, the 1,000-match run): Medium's plan 0.25 ms mean, 0.6 ms p99; Hard and Extreme 0.5 ms mean, 0.95 ms p99, 3.4 ms worst seen (one extra standard-bot plan for the foresight). Select, draft and decision steps stay under 0.3 ms. Far under the 20 ms aim.

**Tests.** `tests/sim/BotDifficulty.spec`: names and the Medium default, valid full matches at every level from either seat, determinism per level, Easy still spends, and fair play: through a whole match, every Hard, Extreme and Easy decision is identical when true support, undecided, the opponent's ground game, the pools, the poll errors and the opponent's private polls are all changed, and when the opponent's cash changes inside its bracket. `tests/net/Lobby.spec` (the request field, the default, invalid values), `tests/net/Session.spec` (the bot seat gets its level and shows it in the seat list; same seed, same bot moves), `tests/e2e/Game.spec` (a Practice request with a level starts that bot; the button plays Medium).

## The start screen

### D-059 — A global leaderboard, bot levels, How to play, and leaving a match (no Config changes, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "add a global public leaderboard on the start screen, easy-medium-hard and extreme difficulty settings for bot play, the ability to leave matches, and a 'how to' menu on the start screen".

**Leaderboard rules.** Ranked by the saved rating (`Profile.rating`, D-028), highest first; equal ratings in a fixed order (smaller UserId first). Only a rated human-vs-human match moves a rating, so a bot match never touches the board and nobody climbs by farming the bot; a player with no rated games is never listed. Storage: an OrderedDataStore `Leaderboard_v1`, key `Player_<UserId>`, the rating as a whole number. It is written when a rated result changes a rating and when a player who already has rated games loads in (so ratings saved before the board existed appear). Reads: the server caches the top 50 and reads `GetSortedAsync(false, 50)` at most once every 60 s, only when someone asks (the Lobby asks when it opens and when "See all" opens), so one server makes at most one sorted read a minute against a budget of 5 + 2 per player. A failed read keeps the last good list; with none the board says "Leaderboard unavailable". The server re-applies its own writes of the last two minutes over a fresh read (a write can take a moment to show up). Names are usernames from `Players:GetNameFromUserIdAsync`, pcall'd and cached for the server's life; a failed lookup shows "Player <id>" and is tried again on the next read. Display names would need another web call per player, so the board shows usernames. Wire: op `Leaderboard` (no fields), answered with message kind `Leaderboard` carrying `Net/Leaderboard.Board` (status, entries, and `you`: the viewer's rank when listed, else their rating if they have rated games). The numbers (`TOP` 50, `REFRESH` 60 s, 10 and 3 rows on screen) are presentation and request budget, not gameplay, so they live in `Net/Leaderboard`, not `Sim/Config`.

**Leaving a match.** New op `Forfeit { matchId }`. The server ends the match at once as a forfeit (`Session.forfeit`), the same result a disconnect forfeit produces: winner the other seat, Profile reason "Forfeit", rated against a person exactly as before (Elo moves, a loss for the leaver), a practice loss against the bot (rating untouched). The opponent gets the notice "Your opponent left the match. You win by forfeit." and the Results screen's "Your opponent forfeited the match.". The leaver goes straight to the Lobby (the client skips that match's Results). Only a seated player of their own live match can forfeit it: a request for another match id is refused (`NotInMatch`), and one from a player not in a match (already out, a repeated request) changes nothing and just re-sends their lobby status. The control is "Leave match" at the end of the in-match **?** sheet (every match screen has the **?**), behind a confirmation: "Leave and forfeit? This counts as a loss" against a person, "Leave this practice match?" against the bot. Not on Results, whose buttons already go back to the Lobby.

**Bot levels.** The Lobby's Play vs Bot gets four chips, Easy, Medium (the default), Hard and Extreme, remembered for the session; Practice requests carry `difficulty`. What each level does, and the server's check of the field, is D-057; `tests/ui/LobbyExtras.spec` follows a tapped Hard chip to the server's bot seat, and an unknown level to Medium.

**How to play.** The Lobby's "How to play" now opens five short pages for new players (the goal, a week, the actions, debates and events, election night), each a title, a picture and at most 30 words (`Shared/UI/Words/Lobby`). Three pictures are the in-match Help's own (`Help.art`), so both always agree; the full rules stay in the in-match **?**.

**Layout.** On a wide screen (≥ 844 px) the leaderboard card sits beside the Lobby's column (top 10 when the screen is at least 560 px tall, else top 3); on a phone it is the last thing in the column (top 3, or only "You" and See all under 740 px, since the Lobby never scrolls). New UI is in new modules (`UI/Leaderboard`, `UI/HowTo`, `UI/BotLevel`, `UI/LeaveMatch`); shared files got small hooks only (`Help`: the Leave button and `Help.art`; `App.client`: mounts LeaveMatch; `Lobby`: the three new pieces).

**Tests.** `tests/net/Leaderboard.spec` (ordering, ties, bad rows, the top-50 cut, moving a player, what a viewer gets, the two new requests, `Session.forfeit`), `tests/e2e/Leaderboard.spec` (the fake engine now has an OrderedDataStore with its own failure switch and `GetNameFromUserIdAsync`: empty board, ordering and names, one read a minute, failures, name retries, backfill on join; leaving against a person and the bot, a repeated request, a request outside a match), `tests/ui/LobbyExtras.spec` (the level chips and the Practice payload, How to play, the card on phone, short phone and desktop, the sheet, both confirmations, closing when the match ends). Shots `01c`–`01f`, `02e`–`02j`, `28`–`28b`: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

### D-060 — Front-page panels: segmented controls, monograms, header race bar (no rule changes, Oct 23, 2026)

WP-2 of the front-page pass (art direction §4), on the white accent (D-058). Client only; presentation numbers live next to their screens.

**Segmented controls, not folder tabs.** The plan gave Help's tabs per-corner radii ("folder" tabs on the body card). `UICorner`'s per-corner radii are still a Studio beta ("New UI Capabilities"), so a live game can't rely on them. Help's tabs and the Lobby's bot levels are instead one segmented control each: a `field` track, the picked item raised with the 2 px white outline, the others flat with muted labels. Same 44 px targets.

**Battleground marks stay outlined.** The Draft's "B" badge was a white (accent) fill; it is now the outlined ★ tag (a dark tag with a white edge), shown on the rim off the Votes lens and on phones (where small circles carry no number), since the Votes lens already stars the number. Home is "H" filled in its owner's color; a pick's "1"/"2" is outlined like the selection ring. Nothing on the map is filled white.

**Monogram colors on Select.** Candidates have no colors of their own, so every card's monogram is in your dealt color (the face you would play) and the taken one in your opponent's; grey before colors are dealt.

**Header score.** The Campaign header uses `Kit.raceBar` (10 px, tick at 270 of 538) with the vote counts in Fredoka at the outer ends and names in `Kit.textColor`. A phone's strip shows the numbers only (both names don't fit beside the title and the buttons); left is always you, and the colors match the bar.

**Text sizes.** The Lobby keeps four: wordmark 72, heading 20 (the primary's label), body 16, caption 14. Hosting shows the code at hero 48, so its title becomes a caption eyebrow ("YOUR PRIVATE MATCH") and the leaderboard title is body-size Fredoka.

### D-061 — Color Pass colors are claimed in Select, first come, first served (supersedes D-055's tie rule, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "for the paid color choice, if two paid players pick the same color one person shouldn't be stuck with a default color. make it like the candidate selector where it's first come first serve."

**Rule (Sim, pure).** Colors work like candidates (D-048). `Match.new(config, seed, playerIds, colorPass?)` takes the Color Pass owners by player id (`{ [id]: { favorite: string? } }`; `PlayerState.colorPass`). During Select an owner claims a color with `Match.claimColor` (`MatchState.colorClaims`: color → player, public in every View). A color someone else holds is turned down with `ColorTaken`; claims of one color in the same server step are settled in a seeded order (`Rng.fork(match.rng, "colorClaims:<n>")`, a coin flip, never seat 1), and the loser keeps the pass, keeps their old color (or the one dealt to them) and picks again. A new claim releases the old color; an owner may change color any number of times until Select ends, also after locking in (the color is not part of the selection). A player without the pass is turned down with `NoColorPass`; an unknown name is `UnknownColor`; after Select, `WrongPhase`. An owner's saved favorite (the Lobby's Color sheet) is claimed for them as Select opens, like a PrePick (batch `colorClaims:0`): two owners with the same favorite are settled by the coin flip, and the loser starts without a claim and is told (`ColorTaken` notice). **This replaces D-055's tie rule**, where the loser was dealt a default color for the whole match.

**Dealing.** Everyone without a claim always holds a dealt color, so the View never has a gap: in a seeded seat order, Crimson and Cobalt first (their order a coin flip), then the other four in a seeded order (fork `"colors"`, as D-055), skipping claimed colors. The deal is recomputed after every claim batch and once more as Select ends; the root stream never advances, so a dealt color changes only when someone claims it (an owner who claims Crimson moves a non-owner from Crimson to Cobalt). Two players without the pass are still always one Crimson and one Cobalt, each seat Crimson half the time (D-001), and colors stay distinct. With no owners the colors are dealt exactly as before: `lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400` prints the same report before and after (only the run time differs). No Config number changed.

**Server.** Ownership is still only the server's check (`PassService`, D-055), taken at match start; the client is never asked. The seat spec carries `colorPass` and, for owners, the saved `color`. New op `ClaimColor { matchId, step, color }` (`Net/Schemas`: `color` must be a string, else `BadShape`) goes to `Session.claimColor`: a stale step is `StaleStep`, outside Select `NotSelect` / `NotInput`, and the match's reasons above. Accepted claims wait for the next update and settle as one batch with that step's candidate claims (`pendingColorClaims`); a loser gets the notice `ColorTaken`.

**View.** `view.colorClaims` (color → player; a player whose color is not in it was dealt theirs) and `view.canPickColor` (you own the pass and it is Select). `players[p].color` is live during Select.

**Client.** Select shows a row of six swatches under the color line when `view.canPickColor` (both steps; hidden for everyone else, who keep the line alone): each a 44 px round chip with a 36 px monogram (your candidate's initials, or your name before a pick) in that color over the color's name. Your color wears the white selection outline; the color your opponent claimed is dimmed, shows their initials and reads "Taken", and does nothing; a tap on any other sends `ClaimColor` (`Net.claimColor`) and the outline moves at once, settling on the server's answer. No buy link on Select: ownership is checked when the match starts, so a purchase mid-Select could not unlock this match's picker (the Lobby's sheet sells the pass). The `ColorTaken` notice reads "Rex took Crimson first. Pick another color." (the opponent's first name once they hold a candidate, and the color last asked for or the saved favorite; `Words.colorTaken`); the rejection text is "Your opponent has that color. Pick another." The Lobby sheet's tie line now says "Same color as your opponent? Whoever claims it first gets it." Debug: `color Teal`. Phone 390x844 still fits step 1 without scrolling; shots `03b-select-color`, `03c-select-color-change` and `04b-select-color-issues` (fixture moment `SelectColor`: you hold Violet, Rex claimed Teal) report 0 audit findings at all three sizes. Also fixed: four client type errors luau-lsp reported (two `FindFirstChild` chains, a tone cast, and `Leaderboard.Board`, which the WP-2 merge had dropped); no behavior change.

**Tests.** `Match.spec` (first come, taken colors, the same-step coin flip over 1,000 seeds, favorites and a shared favorite, non-owners, changing color, the end-of-Select deal, more than two players, determinism, no other seeded stream moved), `Session.spec` (the request, notices, a shared favorite, a timeout), `Lobby.spec` (the schema), `ColorPass.spec` (an owner claims through the real server, by request and by tapping a swatch; a non-owner is refused), `Select.spec` (the picker hidden for non-owners; outline, Taken, a tap claims, step 2, audit at three sizes; the notice wording), `DebugUI.spec` (`color`).

## Landslide (the designers' asks, Oct 23, 2026)

### D-062 — A win with more than 70% of the votes is a Landslide (presentation only, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "If someone wins with more than 70% of the EV give a 'Landslide' graphic on the final screen."

**Rule (Sim, pure).** New `Config.LANDSLIDE_EV_SHARE = 0.70`. `Election.landslide(config, election)` returns the winner when their EV is **strictly more** than that share of the EV in play (the EV of every counted state, 538 on the full map: 377 or more is a landslide, 376 is not), else nil; never on an EV tie, never without an election (a forfeit or an abandoned match). It decides nothing about who wins; it only names the moment. Both players are shown it: the winner's side reads it as theirs, the loser's as the rival's landslide.

**How often.** The balance harness now prints "Landslides (winner over 70% of EV)": 0.8% of 400 bot-vs-bot matches at the 0.12.0 numbers (the bots are close to even; human matches with a beginner will see it more). No other number moved: the helper is read only by the client and the harness.

**Screen.** `Shared/UI/Landslide.moment(config, view, result, names)` (pure) says whether Results shows it and with what words: only for a match decided on election night (`result.reason == "Election"`) whose result agrees with the count. Results puts a `LandslideBox` right under the "You won" / "You lost" banner: the eyebrow "YOU WON BY A" (the loser reads "REX WON BY A"), a hero-size **LANDSLIDE** stamp in the winner's color tilted +4° (the banner tilts −6°, so they read as two moments) with a glow in the winner's color behind it, and "You took 412 of 538 votes, over 70%." The stamp slams in 0.45 s after the banner. For the winner it is the reward: twice the confetti (100 pieces), a second hit ("debateWin") as it lands, a light sweeping across it (`Motion.shine`) and the glow breathing; for the loser it lands quietly after "defeat". Everything decorative is off under reduced motion (the stamp is simply there). Still four text sizes (hero, heading, body, caption). Fixture moments `Landslide` and `LandslideLoss` (the Over match with the loser's safest states handed over until the winner holds more than 70%) render it offline: `DebugScreen = "Landslide"` in Studio, shots `23b-results-landslide` and `23c-results-landslide-loss`, 0 audit findings at all three sizes; `23-results` stays the ordinary win with no stamp.

**Tests.** `tests/sim/Landslide.spec` (377 and 376 of 538, either seat, "strictly more" at an exact share, EV in play rather than EV awarded, a smaller board, no election, an EV tie, and agreement with 20 counted matches). `tests/ui/ResultsLandslide.spec` (the moment's words for the winner and the loser, nil after an ordinary win, a forfeit or a disagreeing result; on screen: no stamp after an ordinary win, the stamp in the winner's color right under the banner, the sounds, the loser's version in the rival's color, a strict audit at phone, iPad and desktop, reduced motion).

### D-063 — Polls cost half: $0.5 a state, $1.5 a region (tuning, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "Make polls half their current cost." Polls had weak use: in playtests the paid poll was the action people skipped, and the harness's NeverPoll bot beat the balanced bot (57% at 400 matches before this change, 53% at 2,000).

**Change.** `INTERNAL_POLL_COST` 1 → 0.5, `REGION_POLL_COST` 3 → 1.5 (a region still costs three singles, so the bot's region-vs-singles rule is unchanged). Money is shown floored to $0.1 and prices rounded up to $0.1 (`Numbers.cost`), so $0.5 and $1.5 show exactly; plan validation adds the same Config numbers (Plan.luau), so a $0.5 poll is affordable with $0.5. Every price on screen (the state card's "Poll $0.5", Help, Explain, the plan's money line) reads Config; nothing else needed changing.

**Harness** (`lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400`, before → after; and 2,000 matches with `INTERNAL_POLL_COST=1 REGION_POLL_COST=3` against the new defaults): the bots poll by count, not by price, so they buy about the same number of polls (7.1 → 7.6 single-state polls and 0.1 region polls per player a match) and spend $5.4 → $3.0 on them; the saved ~$2.4 goes to ads and ground game (GOTV spend $6.9 → $7.4; "GOTV changes the winner" 8.9% → 10.7% at 2,000, back over its 10% target). NeverPoll 57.0% → 56.0% at 400 and 53.0% → 53.2% at 2,000 (no change: a half-price poll is still a poll the bot's plan has to fit). Candidate win rates moved within noise at 400 (±10 points); at 2,000 Rex 57.4 → 54.3, Venkat 55.1 → 53.2, Begay 46.6 → 51.7, Strand (two free polls a week, so the cut halves her perk's dollar value) 48.8 → 46.4, Alvarado 43.1 → 44.4 (still flagged, as before). Comeback rate 37.5% → 40.0% (at the top of its band). Red flags 7 → 5 at 2,000 (Rex, Venkat and GOTV cleared; comeback rate at 40.0% is new). Landslides (D-062) 0.8% → 0.5% at 400.

**Fixtures.** The half-price polls change seed 18's bot match: the Over fixture turned into a loss for seat 1. The Election and Over moments now use seed 26, again a close win for seat 1 on votes with fewer states (290 to 248, 23 states to 28), so the specs and shots keep their meaning; two Election specs no longer assume the tipping call is the night's last.

### D-064 — The game is called Landslide, "Race to 270" (no rule changes, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "Change the name to Landslide with the tagline 'Race to 270'."

**What changed.** Every player-visible use of "270" as the game's name is now "Landslide"; 270 as the votes needed to win stays everywhere (the race bar's tick, "270 to win", election night's "270!" stamp, How to play, Help, the coach tips). The name and the tagline live in `Shared/UI/Words/Setup` (`GAME_NAME`, `TAGLINE`). In the client the name appeared only on the Lobby's wordmark; there is no in-game version or credits line. Developer output keeps the `[270]` log prefix (tests and the checklists grep it), and the repo, the module names and `build/270.rbxl` keep the old name. The experience's name on Roblox is set by hand (Studio Game Settings and the Creator Hub; `docs/TESTING.md`).

**The lockup** (`Kit.wordmark`, art direction §3.12, research 01 §1: a front-page logo is one bold word plus a small banner). "LANDSLIDE" in Fredoka One, white with the top light, the 3 px slate outline, the 6 px drop and the soft white glow the "270" had; at 72 px it is 428 px wide, so on a phone it scales down to fit the column (`TextScaled` with a 28–72 constraint; about 56 px at 390 wide). Under it, riding 12 px up over the name's empty foot, the tagline "RACE TO 270" on a ribbon tilted −3° and split Cobalt | Crimson (the two default player colors: the race itself), Fredoka at heading size, white with a navy outline, a navy edge and a shadow. The Lobby's old one-line pitch ("Win the race to 270 votes.") is gone: the ribbon says it. On a short screen (under 420 px tall) the eyebrow is dropped instead of the tagline. The audit treats the ribbon's text as part of the picture (attribute `Lockup`, like a monogram's initials), so the hosting screen keeps four text sizes (14, 16, 48, 72). Shots `00-kit`, `01-lobby` to `02j`: 0 audit findings at all three sizes.

### D-065 — The week's event sits under the score (no rule changes, Oct 23, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "Move the weekly events to the top under the EV count so they're more apparent." The event card was the third card of the side column: under the map on a phone (a scroll away) and under the plan on an iPad.

**Layout.** On the plan screen the event is now a strip right under the header (the score, the time bar and the now line), full width, above the map and the side column, at every screen size: 52 px, never inside the scrolling column, so it is seen without scrolling on a 390x844 phone, a 1024x768 iPad and a desktop. One tap target: a gold plate (the leaderboard's gold: news) with the event's icon (Spotlight tv, Regional megaphone, Windfall coins, Choice vote), the title "Windfall · Small-dollar surge" (bold) and one line of what it does ("Fundraisers where you trail +20%", "Roads & Transit rallies and ads +30%", "Rallies in the South Atlantic +50%"; in a Choice week the stance taken, "Stance A: Side with the staff"), then an arrow. The line says the multiplier as a signed percent so it fits one line on a phone; the full sentence ("… raise 20% more this week") is in the details. A tap opens the "This week" sheet: the full effect, the story, what the map's * marks, the stance taken in full and, for Helen Strand, next week's event. With only next week's event known (week 1 for Strand) the strip says "Next week: <name>". Both texts are one line that ends in "…" on a phone narrower than 390 px rather than pushing the map down (a spec checks every card of the deck, this week's and next, at 390 px). The strip is hidden during the reveals (B1, as before) and its gold plate pulses once when a new week's event arrives (decorative; pulsing the whole strip ran it past the screen's right edge for a moment, clipping the arrow). On a phone the map keeps its size; the side column under it starts 60 px lower. The Decision screen is unchanged: in a Choice week the stance card is the screen itself and the header names the event.

**Code.** `EventCard.strip(parent, sheetParent, model)` (new, in `Screens/CampaignParts/EventCard`); `Campaign` places it between the header and the body and moves the body down by its height; `EventCard.new` (the Collapsible card) is now used only by the Decision screen. Copy in `Shared/UI/Words/Campaign` (`eventTitle`, `eventLine`). Shots `09-campaign-plan` (Windfall), `09b-event-spotlight`, `09c-event-regional`, `09d-event-next-week`, `09e-event-sheet`, `15-campaign-choice-week` (Choice): 0 audit findings at all three sizes.

## The week's results as a running tally (client only)

### D-066 — Each move is followed by its effect; "New polls are in" lands on the real polls (no rule, payload or Config changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** "For the end of week results, it currently shows every action first, then the total change, but I want it to update the map and values after each shown move, rather than waiting until the end to tally up." The reveal listed both players' actions while the map stayed on last week's polls, then jumped to the new ones; the header's score had already jumped to the result when the reveal opened.

**What the client can know.** The server never says what one action did, and can't without leaking: the true gain is `base × multipliers × saturation(true support)`, so a published per-action delta would let a player solve for true support. The public polls come out once, at the end of the week, with fresh AR(1) noise (about ±2.1 points of margin per state per week) and the momentum drift folded in. So the per-move numbers are **estimates**, and nothing on the wire changes.

**Rule (Shared/UI/RevealSteps, pure, tested).** Each shown move's step re-runs the sim's own arithmetic (Resolve, Support, Events, Polls, read-only) on the start-of-week public poll in place of true support: issue and platform, candidate, battleground, underdog tier, frontrunner, closing weeks, the event and its cap, repeat-ad decay, the attack target's platform, backlash, the pivot's national loss, and a stance's published points (`EventResult.moves`, exact). Every action reads the snapshot of its own Resolve step (rallies the week's start, ads after the rallies, pivots after the ads, stances after the pivots), and the running tally applies the shown moves phase by phase as Resolve flushes them, so neither the reveal order nor the seat watching changes a number (D-001). Both players' moves use the public poll, also the viewer's own (an internal poll would only sharpen one side). A step is one move that changes support plus the still events around it (a flight, a fundraiser, money raised, private polls), so the pace goes to what moves the map. The last step, **"New polls are in"** (first drafted as "Polls settle"), is everything else: momentum drift, the new poll noise, and the estimates' error. It lands the map and the score on exactly the new View's polls and projected votes. Nothing is smeared across the moves.

**How good the estimates are.** With true support for the polls and no drift, the moves add up to exactly what Resolve did, every state, every week (spec, three bot matches). In real play (40 bot matches, 480 weeks): a moved state's direct effect averages 2.7 points of margin and the estimate is off by 0.18 on average; the settle moves every state 1.9 points on average and makes about 9 leader changes a week against 5 from the moves. Polls are noisy by design (D-002), and the settle step now shows that instead of hiding it inside the moves.

**Screen.** After "Both plans are in" (map and score still at the week's start), each step lands its lines, bumps its states, moves their fill and leads to the tally, badges its points (FLIP and a ring where the leader changes) and counts the header's projected votes and race bar to it (`model.revealEV`, set from what the map paints). Then "New polls are in": the real polls, rings on what they flipped, and "What changed" with the whole week's real flips and biggest moves, as before. Without the week's start (a client that joined mid-reveal) nothing is estimated: the map shows the new polls and a Choice week badges the stances' published points, as before. Pace: the server's 8 s reveal (3 s when both skip) is unchanged; steps average 8.6 a week (at most 17) against more single-event beats before, so each move gets more time. Shots `16b-resolve-step1`, `16c-resolve-mid`, `16d-resolve-settle`, `17-resolve-end` at phone, iPad and desktop size show the map and the score together, 0 audit findings. `MapInterface` is unchanged (the map is driven only through `setView` and `setMarks`).

**Follow-up (user feedback, same day): make each move's effect obvious, and the last step read as news.** "I want it to be obvious what effects the moves are having." Each move step now badges **every** state it touched with its signed points toward you (`SwingMarks.step`: "+1.5" in your color when it helps you, "−1.5" in orange when it hurts you; no "biggest 3" cut), a leader change says FLIP in the new leader's color, rings, and bumps in that color, and the header's line carries it at a glance ("Rex: Rally · NY → Rex +28", `Words.leaderArrow`) while the projected votes count to the new total. The last step is renamed **"New polls are in"** (it read as moves being undone) and has its own look: a raised stamp in the panel, "Polls shifted 4 states" under it (`Words.pollShift`: the leader changes the new polls made on top of the moves), a pulse sweeping the map west to east (0.6 s, decorative), and the header's line in the round display font, popped in. The estimates note left the screen: one line behind "Why?" ("≈ Each move's points are estimated from the polls you both saw…").

**Pace: the reveal grows with its steps (approved timer tuning).** The fixed 8 s left 5.5 s for the moves: 0.64 s a step on an average week, only 0.32 s on the biggest (17 steps), where a count-up and a badge barely land. The server now gives the week's reveal `Resolve.revealTime` = clamp(`TIMER_RESOLVE_BASE` 4.5 + `TIMER_RESOLVE_PER_STEP` 0.55 × steps, `TIMER_RESOLVE` 8, `TIMER_RESOLVE_MAX` 14) s. `Resolve.revealSteps` counts from the week's events, private ones included, so both seats get the same deadline (D-001): one step per move (rally, ad, attack, backlash, pivot, stance), one for a player with only still events, one for the new polls; it is never fewer than any screen shows (spec). The deadline stays absolute (`setStage`), the client already paces from `deadline − stageStart`, so each step now gets at least about 0.55 s plus its share of 2 s; both skipping still cuts it to `TIMER_RESOLVE_SKIPPED` (3 s). *Match time (D-005), 60 bot matches, 720 weeks:* the reveal averages 9.4 s (was 8; half the weeks stay at 8, the longest seen 13.3 s), adding 16 s a match on average (median 17, p90 21, max 24 s). Worst case (every week at 14 s) adds 12 × 6 = 72 s: the old steps' pinned worst case goes from 940 s to 1,012 s (`Config.spec`), still under the 17.5-minute line, and typical matches stay well inside 15 minutes.

## A deeper palette (client only)

### D-067 — A deeper, richer palette (no rule changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "for the campaign colors make them less pastel and light", then "the color scheme in general". Kept: the navy-and-white system, what each color means (red and blue are the default players; orange `loss` is anything against you; white is press here / selected; Amber stays gold, clear of the orange) and every contrast floor. Everything goes through `Shared/UI/Colors` (names and functions unchanged), so the map and screens being rebuilt in parallel pick it up.

**Why it looked pastel.** The map blended from a light grey (`neutral` 118, 124, 142) toward the leader's color in straight RGB, and a lean took only 45 % of the color: a Crimson lean came out 158, 92, 111 (42 % saturation), a dusty pink; a Cobalt lean a grey-blue. Every node also wore a near-white `undecided` ring (190, 196, 210) that washed out the fill inside it. Player text was a fixed quarter of the way to white (Crimson text 215, 100, 117, a salmon pink).

**New values (old → new).**

| Token | Old | New | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Crimson | 206, 52, 72 | 212, 28, 66 | deeper cherry, hue 348 (a little cooler, farther from the orange) |
| Cobalt | 52, 104, 214 | 34, 98, 228 | saturated royal blue |
| Emerald | 36, 168, 108 | 0, 170, 96 | full saturation; still takes dark text (luma 0.504) |
| Amber | 240, 186, 36 | 246, 190, 8 | richer gold, hue 46 (was 44), so no closer to the orange |
| Violet | 146, 84, 214 | 152, 56, 228 | deeper purple, hue 273: farther from Cobalt |
| Teal | 24, 168, 178 | 0, 164, 186 | full saturation |
| `neutral` (toss-up) | 118, 124, 142 | 70, 78, 104 | a dark slate: blends from it are deep, not grey pastels; toss-ups recede and leads pop |
| `undecided` (node ring, silver medal) | 190, 196, 210 | 164, 174, 204 | a slate ring that frames a fill without whitening it |
| `BANDS` lean / likely | 0.45 / 0.75 | 0.6 / 0.82 | a lean reads as its color (Crimson lean 155, 48, 81, 69 % saturation); strength shows as depth: lean darkest, safe brightest. Steps between bands stay as wide as before (CIEDE2000 27 / 7 / 6 for Crimson, was 23 / 9 / 6) |
| `stateFill` | linear to `FULL_MARGIN` | `(margin / 12) ^ FILL_CURVE` (0.6) | a 3-point lead is 44 % of the way, 6 points 66 % (was 25 % / 50 %); no screen calls it today, kept in step with the bands |
| `good` / `goodEdge` | 92, 200, 128 / 64, 136, 94 | 80, 212, 96 / 50, 140, 66 | a clear green, not mint; 8.6:1 on surface; 13 CIEDE2000 from Emerald (was 9.8) |
| navy stage | background 13, 17, 30 · field 18, 23, 38 · surface 24, 30, 48 · surfaceTop 31, 38, 60 · raised 38, 46, 70 · raisedTop 46, 55, 82 · raisedHover 54, 64, 94 · raisedLip 9, 12, 22 · line 70, 80, 112 · glow 44, 62, 120 | 11, 16, 34 · 16, 23, 44 · 22, 30, 55 · 29, 38, 68 · 36, 47, 80 · 44, 56, 93 · 52, 65, 106 · 8, 12, 26 · 68, 81, 124 · 40, 60, 136 | same lightness per step (L* within 1), about a third more blue chroma: navy, not blue-grey |
| `muted` | 158, 168, 194 | 156, 168, 204 | same contrast (7.0:1 on surface, 5.6:1 on raised), a little bluer |

Unchanged: `text`, `accent`, `accentEdge`, `accentLip`, `loss` and `lossEdge`, and every name.

**Readable player text (`Colors.textOn`).** Was a fixed 25 % toward `text`. Now: the color itself when it already reads at 4.5:1 on a card's lit top (`surfaceTop`; `Colors.TEXT_CONTRAST`); else the same color brightened (channels scaled up in 5 % steps, so hue and saturation stay); and only if full brightness is not enough, tinted toward `text` in 5 % steps until it reads. Crimson text is 252, 76, 113 (5.0:1 on surface, was 215, 100, 117 at 4.7:1), Cobalt 89, 143, 254, Violet 188, 108, 254; Emerald, Teal and Amber text is the color itself (4.9 to 8.7:1 on `surfaceTop`). New helper `Colors.contrast(a, b)` (WCAG ratio).

**Measured.** CIE76 (D-056's measure) / CIEDE2000 (D-051, D-055's): closest pair of player colors Cobalt–Violet 39 / 19 (was 30 / 18); Crimson–Cobalt 107 / 42 (was 96 / 41); closest to the loss orange Amber 45 / 27 (was 42 / 26) and Crimson 46 / 30 (was 45 / 28). Under simulated deuteranopia Crimson and Cobalt are 56 CIEDE2000 apart (was 52). White text on Crimson, Cobalt and Violet fills 4.7 to 4.8:1 (was 4.2 to 4.6); dark text on Emerald, Teal, Amber 6.2 to 11:1. The accent stays lighter than every color (closest Amber 1.58:1, floor 1.5). `onAccent` 17.5:1, `onLoss` 7.3:1, `loss` on surface 6.3:1, the primary's lip 3.9:1 on surface and 4.4:1 on the stage.

**Not changed (screens own them).** Election night's feed rows (`WINNER_TINT` 0.3 from `raised`), the Draft legend (0.6 from `neutral`) and the map's lip shade keep their own constants; they get richer through the new base colors. `Kit.EDGE_TINT` is unchanged.

**Tests.** `Helpers.spec` checks every player's text reads at `TEXT_CONTRAST` on `surfaceTop` and is never darker than its fill, and that every player color is ≥ 35 CIE76 from every other and ≥ 40 from the loss orange; the stateFill and band cases follow the new curve and shares; `Kit.spec` pins the new Crimson, Teal and stage. 0 audit findings on every snapshot at all three sizes.

## Candidate portraits and custom names

### D-068 — Illustrated candidate portraits; neutral plates on Select (no rule changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "work on the candidate portraits". Candidates were initials monograms, and on Select every free card's circle was in your color (D-060), so the eight cards looked alike.

**Art.** Eight flat, vector-style head-and-shoulders busts, one per fictional candidate, drawn by a committed generator (`assets/portraits/make.py`, Pillow, drawn at 4x and scaled to 512x512, transparent background; `--preview DIR` writes all eight on plates at 40, 80 and 160 px). Each differs in face shape, skin tone, hair, outfit and one prop that hints at the perk: Dana Okafor (Fundraiser) gold coin earrings and brooch, mustard blazer, a natural puff; Rex Calloway (Barnstormer) a straw boater, rolled sleeves and braces; Priya Venkat (Ad Wizard) a headset microphone, long hair; Marcus Hale (Debater) a bow tie, beard and suit; June Alvarado (Insider) a lanyard access badge and pearls, a bob with bangs; Tomas Reyes (Insurgent) a lightning-bolt pin, a curly mop and a bomber; Helen Strand (Pollster) round glasses, a pencil behind the ear, a silver bun; Walt Begay (Organizer) a field cap, a mustache and a work vest. No outfit is a player color's red or blue, no party symbols, nobody modeled on a real person. Each bust is centered so the circle the game crops it to keeps the face and the top of the shoulders; hats and buns stay inside it. At 40 px the hair, hats, glasses and skin tone tell them apart; at 160 px the props read.

**Kit.** `Kit.portrait(id, name, px, opts?)` builds Frame "Portrait": a round neutral plate (slate gradient 104, 118, 156 to 66, 76, 108; dataColor), the bust as ImageLabel "Face" with its own `UICorner` (the image is rounded with it, so the shoulders end at the circle), and UIStroke "Ring": a dark 1.5 px edge, or with `opts.ring` a ring in a player color (6 % of the size, 2 px at least). `opts.fade` dims it (a taken candidate). `Kit.paintPortrait(root, opts)` repaints ring and fade without rebuilding; `Kit.portraitImage(id)` gives the asset id. `Kit.monogram` is unchanged (the map's candidate pin and the color swatches still use it).

**Assets.** `UI/Assets.portraits` maps candidate id to the uploaded image's number, 0 until the lead uploads the PNGs. While 0 the Face is empty and the initials of `name` show on the plate (a monogram on the neutral plate), so nothing breaks before the upload.

**Select.** Free candidates sit on the neutral plate with the plain edge: the faces, not the color, tell the cards apart. Your current pick and your claimed candidate get a ring in your color; the opponent's candidate a ring in theirs, dimmed with the card. Portraits are 56 px on wide screens (card 136 px, was 48 and 128) and 40 px on a phone (card 102 px, was 36 and 100), so step 1 with its coach tip still fits a 390x844 phone without scrolling (`Select.spec`); 44 px pushed it 19 px over. The color line and the Color Pass swatches keep monograms: they show a color, not a face.

**Everywhere else a candidate's face appears**, ringed in the player's color: the Draft's strongholds card (24 px, replacing the 12 px dot), the Campaign header's score strip (22 px at each outer end; hidden on a phone, where the strip only has room for the numbers), the week reveal's group heads (32 px, was a 24 px monogram) and a new debate face-off over the scoreboard (44 px, "vs" between), the election spotlight (44 px; a "?" plate while counting), Results' player cards (64 px) and How to play's first page (two 40 px sample candidates, ringed blue and red, over the race bar).

**Renderer.** The headless snapshots draw the local PNG for any ImageLabel with attribute Portrait (a base64 data URI, defined once per shot, clipped to the image's corner radius) whether or not the game has the asset id yet, and skip the stand-in initials (attribute PortraitFallback). 0 audit findings on every shot at phone, iPad and desktop.

### D-069 — The Name Pass: a custom candidate name, chat-grade filtering, reports and owner review (Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "a way to pay to get the ability to make a custom name (censor not allowed content and limit to 16+)". The user's follow-ups: only for (and only shown to) 16+ accounts, using Roblox's chat permission as the stand-in; "give the names the same filters as chat and filter any other stuff that could be against TOS"; a note that names breaking Roblox's rules are not allowed; "add a report name button that works", then "owners should review instead of automatic ban. that name and similar names will then be added to blacklist"; and "give me and him free color and name selection".

**Rule.** A Game Pass, "Name Your Candidate" (`Net/Products.NAME_PASS_ID`, 0 until the humans create it: then nothing is offered, except to admins). An owner names their candidate for their matches, on Select; the candidate keeps its portrait and perk. Bots never have custom names. The name is saved in `Profile.customName` as typed (after the character rules) and filtered again on every visit.

**Who (the 16+ stand-in).** Roblox exposes no age to scripts (see "Age research"). The user chose its chat permission instead: `server/NameEligibility.isEligible(player)` = `TextChatService:CanUserChatAsync(userId)` (server, yields, errors for a user not in the server), asked once per visit on join, cached, asked again on SetName; pcall'd, an error is "no" (fail closed). Text chat now needs an age check, but a parent can also allow chat, so this is close to, not the same as, "age-checked 16+". Only an eligible player is offered the pass (buy button and name line hidden otherwise) or may set a name; a viewer sees another player's name only if both are eligible and `TextChatService:CanUsersChatAsync(setter, viewer)` is true (the pairwise check: blocks and parental controls between the two), cached per pair. In Studio, where the chat APIs may not answer, `NameEligibility.STUDIO_OVERRIDE = true` (a constant, committed as false) makes everyone eligible, but only while `RunService:IsStudio()`; a live server ignores it.

**Character rules** (`Net/CustomName.normalize`, before any filter): trim, collapse spaces (a ’ becomes '); only letters (A-Z, a-z and the accented Latin letters U+00C0-U+017F), spaces, hyphens, apostrophes and periods. So no digits (phone numbers, ages, dates, addresses), emoji, symbols, "@" handles, "/" or ":" (URLs), zero-width or combining marks (no zalgo), and no period followed by two letters (".com", "x.gg"; "J.R." is fine). At least 2 letters, no character three times in a row, at most `NAME_MAX_LENGTH` = 16 characters (code points). Reasons: `NameBadText`, `NameEmpty`, `NameTooShort`, `NameRepeats`, `NameTooLong`, each with a short, non-revealing message.

**Blocklists** (server only; they complement Roblox's filter, never replace it). `server/NameBlocklist` is a static list, never sent to clients. It holds real heads of state and government and leading politicians (US and UK first, then other major countries), national parties, well-known political slogans and movements (Roblox's Community Standards forbid political content about real people and parties), Roblox, its staff and moderation ("roblox", "admin", "mod", "official", "staff", "builderman"), social apps and contact bait ("discord", "snap", "insta", "tiktok", "youtube", "dm me", "add me", "robux"), and the game's own words ("you", "rival", "bot", "tie", "nobody"). Titles stay allowed on fictional names ("Senator Zed"); a real surname makes a name political. Matching (`CustomName.blocked`) compares folded text: lower case, accents dropped, look-alikes folded (0/o, 1/l/|/!/i, 3/e, 4/@/a, 5/$/s, 7/t, 8/b, 9/g, rn/m, vv/w), letters only. A plain entry matches anywhere in the name, and for entries of `NAME_SIMILAR_MIN_LETTERS` (5) or more also anything within `NAME_SIMILAR_DISTANCE` (1) edits ("Rablox"). An "=" entry matches whole words only, exactly (common surnames and short words: "Bush" but not "Bushnell", "you" but not "Young"; near misses of such words are ordinary names: "farage" would catch "garage"). The reviewed list (below) is merged in. A match is `NameFiltered`, worded like the filter's refusal: "That name isn't allowed. Try another."

**Filtering like chat.** At set time (and on each visit for a saved name), `TextService:FilterStringAsync(name, setterId, Enum.TextFilterContext.PublicChat)`; the result's `GetNonChatStringForBroadcastAsync()` must come back unchanged (no "#"), else `NameFiltered`; an error is `NameFilterFailed` (fail closed, the old name stays). Each viewer then gets the name through the same result's `GetNonChatStringForUserAsync(viewerId)` (the docs' per-recipient method for non-chat text, "based on age and other details"; `GetChatForUserAsync` is deprecated and returns ""), so each viewer gets their own filter level, like chat. A changed string, any "#" or an error shows that viewer the candidate's own name. The TextFilterResult is kept only in the server's memory while the setter is there; nothing filtered is stored (the text guide: stored text "should be filtered when retrieving").

**Per viewer.** `Session` keeps each seat's name and, per viewer, the other seats' names as filtered for them (`Session.showName`, sent by `MatchService.shareNames` once the checks finish; a new name is hidden from everyone else until it is re-shared). `View.build(config, match, you, names?)` stays pure: the server passes only the names that viewer may see, so they never reach anyone else, the snapshots or the duel harness.

**The rules note.** Under the Name Pass offer on Select and on the Rename sheet, muted: "Names must follow Roblox's Community Standards. Names that break them won't be allowed." (`Words/Setup.NAME_RULES`).

**Reports.** Wherever a viewer sees another player's custom name: "Report "Big Wave"" in the in-match Help sheet (every match screen has its "?") and "Report name" on Results' opponent card, behind a confirmation. New op `ReportName { userId }` (the setter; digits only, else `BadShape`). Turned down: yourself (`ReportSelf`), a name you were not shown, a bot or a candidate's own name (`ReportNoName`), within `NAME_REPORT_WAIT` (10 s) of your last report (`ReportTooSoon`), past `NAME_REPORTS_PER_VISIT` (10, `ReportLimit`). Effect: the reporter sees the candidate's own name at once, for the rest of the match and in later matches against that player (`Profile.hiddenNames`, at most `NAME_HIDDEN_MAX` = 30, oldest dropped), and gets "Thanks for the report. You'll see the candidate's own name instead." The report goes into the review queue (`NameReports_v1`, one key "Queue": per setter the name, distinct reporters (at most 20), first and last time; at most `NAME_REVIEW_MAX` = 200 names, the longest-quiet one drops). Nothing is removed automatically. **Roblox's own report:** the engine reference has no developer-callable report API (`Players:ReportAbuse` is not documented for developers; `StarterGui:SetCore` offers PromptBlockPlayer, PromptSendFriendRequest and so on, but no report prompt), so the confirmation adds "For anything serious, also use Roblox's own Report in the Roblox menu."

**Owner review.** Admins (`server/Admins`): the experience owner (`game.CreatorId` when `game.CreatorType` is User; for a group-owned game a role of `ADMIN_GROUP_RANK` (255) or more, read with `GroupService:GetRolesInGroupAsync`, which the docs say supersedes the deprecated `Player:GetRankInGroupAsync`) plus `Config.ADMIN_USER_IDS` (5618560436, the owner; 479739965, "originalyellowboss", the partner). The Lobby shows admins "Review names (N)"; the sheet lists each reported name with its owner's username, report count and first and last dates, and **Keep** (dismiss the reports; the name stays) or **Remove**. Every request (`ReviewList`, `ReviewKeep`, `ReviewRemove`) is checked on the server (`NotAdmin`). Remove clears the name for that player (profile, their match in any phase, notice "Your custom name was removed after a review. You can pick a new one.") and adds it, folded, to the reviewed blocklist (`NameBlocklist_v1`, key "Entries", at most 2,000), which every server merges with the static list. It is pushed to running servers with `MessagingService:PublishAsync("NameBlocklist", entry)` (a 1 kB message; publish budget 600 + 240 per player per minute, checked in the docs), and each server reloads it every `NAME_BLOCKLIST_REFRESH` (300 s) after its first name check, so a missed message is caught up. Matching "similar names" is the blocklist match above: the removed name, anything containing it, and one edit away (5+ letters). A saved name that newly matches is cleared on its owner's next join (with the notice), and a present player's name is cleared when the list grows. All DataStore calls are pcall'd with three tries and backoff; the review queue costs one UpdateAsync per report.

**Admins' free passes.** Admins own both the Color Pass and the Name Pass for free: `PassService` answers yes for them without asking MarketplaceService, and the color picker and the name line show for them even while the pass ids are 0 (for everyone else they stay hidden while 0). The chat-permission eligibility still applies to an admin's custom name.

**Config** (not rules; the balance harness is unaffected): `NAME_MAX_LENGTH` 16, `NAME_SIMILAR_DISTANCE` 1, `NAME_SIMILAR_MIN_LETTERS` 5, `NAME_REPORT_WAIT` 10, `NAME_REPORTS_PER_VISIT` 10, `NAME_HIDDEN_MAX` 30, `NAME_REVIEW_MAX` 200, `NAME_REPORTERS_MAX` 20, `NAME_BLOCKLIST_MAX` 2000, `NAME_BLOCKLIST_REFRESH` 300, `ADMIN_USER_IDS`, `ADMIN_GROUP_RANK` 255.

**Age research** (creator-docs, Oct 2026). What exists: `Player.AgeChecked` (`Enum.AgeCheckStatus` Unchecked / Checked: the player finished an age check through `Player:PromptAgeCheck`, but not which age it found); `Player:IsVerified(Enum.VerifiedLevel.Low | High)` (phone or government-ID "real-world signals", not an age; always false in Studio); `PolicyService:GetPolicyInfoForPlayerAsync` (policy flags such as `IsContentSharingAllowed` and `IsPhotoToAvatarAllowed`, which "reflects age (for example, users under 13)"; no age or 16+ field); `TextChatService:CanUserChatAsync`, `CanUsersChatAsync` and `GetChatGroupsAsync` (chat permission, fed by age checks and parental controls; not an age). Nothing on `Players` or `Player` gives an age, age group or birthday. The only exact 16+ guarantee is at the experience level: a game rated "Content May Vary" is open only to age-checked users 16+ (content-maturity and kids-and-select guides).

**Tests.** `tests/net/CustomName.spec` (the character rules with many allowed and refused names, the limit in characters, folding, the blocklist match: contains, look-alikes, near misses, whole words; the static list lets fictional names (all eight candidates) through and stops real politicians, parties, slogans, staff, contact bait and the game's words; the profile fields; Session's per-viewer names and setName rules). `tests/ui/NamePass.spec` (real server and client on the fake engine, which now has TextService with per-viewer hiding, TextChatService, MessagingService and `setNamePass`, `setCanChat`, `blockChat`): hidden while the id is 0; not eligible without chat permission; a chat-check error fails closed and SetName asks again; the Studio override in Studio only; setting, saving, resetting; every refusal; the filter failing; the blocklist before the filter; a saved name re-filtered; Select only; per-viewer visibility, a blocked pair, per-viewer filtering and its failure; the buy flow and the sheet; the rules note; admins' free passes while the ids are 0, still subject to chat permission. `tests/ui/NameReport.spec`: a report hides and queues; self, unseen, bad ids and too soon are refused; hidden in the next match; Help → confirmation → report; non-admins refused on every review op; Keep; Remove clears, notifies, blocks look-alikes and one-edit names, publishes; a matching saved name cleared on join. Shots `03d`–`03g`, `01g-lobby-admin`, `02k-lobby-review`: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

## The real map (client only)

### D-070 — Real state shapes, Polls marks for momentum and ground game, candidate pins (no rule changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "Make the map be an actual map of America rather than just circles", "Add a way to see momentum and placed/revealed ground game on the poll map view" (only under the Polls tab; the pins in every tab), and "Make it easier to identify which [state] your candidate is in by just looking at the map".

**Geometry.** `scripts/mapgen/data/states-albers-10m.json` is us-atlas 3.0.1 (ISC, Mike Bostock; `LICENSE-us-atlas` beside it), built from US Census Bureau cartographic boundaries (public domain): the 50 states and DC already projected (Albers USA) into a 975x610 frame. `scripts/mapgen/make.py` (Python and Pillow, committed; `topo.py` decodes the TopoJSON) tucks Alaska in under California (82 % size) beside Hawaii, and writes: two atlases of white state masks (`assets/map/map-states-1.png` 1024x944, `-2.png` 926x281: Roblox keeps images up to 1024 px, so the shapes are drawn at 1.3 px per unit, the US about 1,240 px wide, enough for a 1,000 px iPad map, 4x supersampled for soft edges, 2 px apart), one image of the borders between states (`map-borders.png`, white lines, tinted navy in game), and `src/shared/UI/MapShapeData.luau`: per state its sprite's rect in the map box, its atlas rect, a label anchor, the inner circle's diameter ("room"), outlines simplified to 0.6 units for tap tests and snapshots, and coarser ones (1.5 units) for the selection outline. The label anchor is the point farthest inside the state (polylabel), so Florida's sits in the peninsula and Michigan's in the mitten; Hawaii's islands are too small for that, so the inset's free sea counts as its room. `Shared/UI/MapShape` reads the data and settles taps (pure, specced headlessly).

**Layout.** The box keeps `MapLayout.ASPECT` (1.6), so no screen's layout changed. On a portrait phone the map's width is what binds (the screen is 390 px), so a taller box would only add empty space; instead the land now fills 87 % of the box width (the circles spread over 81 %) and its bottom strip holds a legend row. The nine small Northeast states (`MapLayout.LADDER`) are callout chips in one column right of the coast, north to south, each in its state's fill with a thin leader line to the real state, which is still drawn. Every chip is 40 px wide on the narrowest map (`PHONE_WIDTH` 360); nine 36 px rows would need a map 340 px tall, so on a phone the chips' 36 px tap squares overlap in height and a tap goes to the nearest chip. A second chip column would have shrunk every other state by 12 %; we kept one.

**Drawing.** Each state is an ImageLabel showing its rect of an atlas (`ImageRectOffset`, `ImageRectSize`) tinted with `ImageColor3` by the same rules as the circles (`Colors.bandFill` toward the public-poll leader, the lean before the first poll), lit a little from the top. The selection is a 3 px white outline (thin rotated frames along the coarse outline) with the breathing glow; an owner's ring and a moved state's halo are the same outline in their color, plus a plate in the mover's color behind the code. Codes sit on the anchor; the lens value shows under the code where the state's inner circle is at least 24 px (most states on an iPad, the big ones on a phone; a chip at least 56 px wide), and may reach past the state's edge. Marks, tags, the plan badge, the ghost tag, routes, motion (`pulse`, `ring`, `wipe`) and the names screens and specs use (`Nodes/<id>`, `Tags/<id>`) are as before.

**Taps.** One button covers the map and each state has a square on its anchor at least 36 px each way (a chip state: its chip). A press reads `InputObject.Position` (the docs: it counts the top bar inset, which our ScreenGui ignores, so the inset is added back) and `MapShape.resolve` decides: the chip column picks the nearest chip; a point inside a state's outline picks that state, so a neighbor's bigger square never steals it; a border, the coast or the sea next to a small state goes to the square that was pressed; open sea selects nothing. A press without a position (gamepad) picks the pressed square.

**Until the upload.** `UI/Assets` holds `mapStates1`, `mapStates2` and `mapBorders` with empty ids; `NodeMap.new()` returns the real map only when all three are set (`ShapeMap.ready()`), else the circles, so the game never shows a blank map. Specs and snapshots set `NodeMap.preview` (Rig) to build the real map anyway; the snapshot renderer draws each shape from its outline. `NodeMap.circles()` stays for the fallback and its specs. The images were uploaded the same day (ids in `UI/Assets`), so the real map now shows by default.

**Polls marks.** The lens rule was one mark per state (D-046). Polls now carries up to three, because it answers "where is it moving and who is working it", and the designers asked for it there only. Under the lead: ▲ in the momentum leader's color when one side's momentum leads by 0.05 or more (only the leader drifts, `DRIFT_RATE` x the lead per week, capped, `Resolve` step 7), ▲▲ from a lead of 2 (1.2 points a week or more); then your ground-game units as filled dots in your color (`StateView.gotv`), then the opponent's as hollow dots in theirs, only where your internal polls saw them (`View.scouted`, D-049, the latest reading). Everything comes from the player's own View: momentum is public, units are your own, scouting is yours; nothing hidden is guessed. A legend row in the map's bottom strip says "▲ momentum ● ○ ground game" in Polls only; the Map key adds two lines. The circles fall back to showing the ▲ alone.

**Pins.** `PlayerView.location` is public for every player (the state card already says "Rex is here"), so both candidates get a pin standing on their state's code: their face on a disc in their color with a point, a flag over it reading "You" or the rival's first name, edged in the stage navy (nothing on the map is white). A small Northeast state's pin stands on its real place. Two pins that would cover each other stand side by side. The face comes from `Map/Pins.face`, the one place to swap `Kit.monogram` for a portrait. Pins show in every lens on Campaign and Decision.

**Numbers.** No gameplay number changed; the map's sizes are presentation constants in `ShapeMap`, `Pins` and `make.py`.

**Tests.** `tests/sim/MapShape.spec` (every state, anchors inside their own outline, sprites inside the box and atlases, PNG sizes on disk at most 1024, chips east of all land, taps by outline over every square's neighborhood), `tests/ui/ShapeMap.spec` (atlas rects and fills, 36 px targets at 390 and 340 px, tap resolution through the real buttons with and without the inset, outlines, the Polls row, pins, legend, strict audit at four sizes), `Lens.spec` (momentum and pips from the View only, Polls only, the legend), `NodeMap.spec` (the circles, now `NodeMap.circles()`, and the choice between the maps), the motion specs for both maps. Shot `13b-lens-polls-ground` shows the Polls marks; every shot reports 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

### D-071 — Plan preview on the map (no rule changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "plan preview on the map".

**What it shows.** On Campaign, while you draft the week's plan and until you lock in, every state a queued action would move carries a faint signed tag on the map: the expected change in your margin there (your support minus the opponent's, in points), green for a gain, orange with its minus sign for a loss, summed when several actions hit one state. Under the map the strip reads "Plan: 268 → 281 EV", then "if nothing else changes" in muted text where it fits (it drops first on a narrow phone row; the Map key always says it). Both update as actions are added or removed, in every lens.

**Where the numbers come from (`Shared/UI/PlanPreview`, pure).** Each action is `Explain.action`'s estimate (the state card's own number) with the actions before it queued, so a repeat ad counts less and a second attack fills the backlash meter, exactly as the card showed when it was added. Rally and Ad: your gain minus the opponent's loss; Attack: your gain (less backlash) plus their loss; Ground game: units x `GOTV_PER_UNIT` (it counts on election night). Fundraise, polls and Pivot move no state's margin, so they get no tag. The votes line starts from the header's count (`View.projectedEV`: public-poll leaders) and moves a polled state's votes when its lead would change hands; an unpolled state never moves the count, as in the header. Only your View and your draft are read; the opponent's plan is unknown, hence the caveat. Momentum gained is not in the number (it drifts next week).

**Map.** `MapInterface.setPreview(tags)` (new; nothing else changed): the real map draws the tags in a "Preview" layer above everything, under each state's label stack (so the Polls lens's lead, chevron and dots stay readable), over the code near the map's bottom edge, and left of a callout chip. The circles draw them under each circle. The legend strip now runs the full width under the land (Alaska and Hawaii end above it), and `LegendItem` gains a text-only mark and `optional` items.

**Tests.** `tests/ui/PlanPreview.spec` (empty plan; a repeat ad summed at its lower value; ground game counted, money and polls not; an attack's sign; a state taking the lead moves its votes, an unpolled one does not; on Campaign: tags while drafting, two rallies in one state summed, the votes line, every lens, gone at lock-in), `ShapeMap.spec` and `NodeMap.spec` (tags on top, colors, placement, the strict audit). Shots `10c-plan-preview-empty`, `10d-plan-preview` (two rallies in PA, one in OH) and `10e-plan-preview-polls` (the same on the Polls lens with ground game): 0 audit findings at all three sizes.

**Map clutter pass (lead review, same day).** Around Ohio, Pennsylvania and West Virginia the pins, route, plan badges, preview tags and Polls marks piled onto the codes. Now: (1) a mark layout pass in `ShapeMap` places every mark around its own code, never on any code: each kind tries spots in its own order (lens rows under, pins and preview tags beside, tags above, plan badges at a corner), then the same spots 1.6x as far with a thin leader; codes never move; lens rows that find no code-free spot near their state are left out on that map size (the state card has the numbers). The layout runs once a frame (deferred) and only when a mark or the map's size changed. (2) Pins are compact: a 14 to 20 px face, a "You" or name tab beside it and a tiny pointer, placed beside the code (a little farther with a leader if needed); two pins, in one state or neighbors, never overlap. (3) The route is 2 px at half transparency (flights fainter), under every mark. (4) On Polls the preview tag sits beside the code and its lead, and takes another spot only when that one is taken. On a phone, while a plan exists, the legend row keeps the plan line and drops the Polls legend first (the Map key keeps it). The fake engine now applies `UIAspectRatioConstraint` (FitWithinMaxSize), so specs measure the map as Roblox draws it. Shot `10f-pins-same-state` (both candidates in Ohio).

## Debates as live rounds

### D-072 — Debates are three live rounds: Defend, Attack or Pivot, with visible counters and a stakes Zinger (approved reshape, Oct 24, 2026, branch `debates`)

**Feedback.** The designers: debates "kinda feel like glorified stance questions rather than their own thing". They chose "Live rounds: short back-and-forth rounds: attack, defend or pivot to your platform, with visible counters. More drama, more reading the opponent", within their standing rules: skill-based and learnable (no blind rock-paper-scissors), tied to the states you need, visible on the map, deciding about 1 match in 5. The proposal and its paper model are in `docs/proposals/debates-live-rounds.md` (revision 2 after the lead's audit). This replaces the focus decision, the roll and the gaffe of D-040 and D-044.

**Rules.** A debate week opens with its Debate phase: one round per topic (the topics are still drawn at match start and announced weeks ahead). Each round both players pick, at once and in secret, Defend, Attack or Pivot, and may stake their one Zinger. Strength is public arithmetic (`Debate.board`, the same function for the server, the View, the bots, the screen and the duel board):

| Move | Base | Bonuses |
|---|---|---|
| Defend | 2 | +3 the topic is on your platform · +1 you lead the topic's states |
| Attack | 2 | +2 it is on an opponent's platform · +1 you trail its states · +2 you trail the crowd |
| Pivot | 4 | +1 it is not on your platform |

The move you played last round is −2. "Lead the topic's states": the EV of the topic's states where you lead the public poll is more than every other player's. Defend beats Attack, Attack beats Pivot, Pivot beats Defend: the counter adds `DEBATE_COUNTER` = +2 in round 1, +3 in rounds 2 and 3. The higher score wins; equal is a split. No dice: the debate's seeded stream is retired.

A win gives `DEBATE_ROUND_WIN` (0.8) support in each of the winner's landing states, ×`DEBATE_ZINGER_MULT` (2) if it staked its Zinger (Hale ×4). Defend and Attack land in the topic's states. Pivot lands in the player's pivot issue: the platform issue other than the topic whose states hold the most EV within `DEBATE_PIVOT_CLOSE` (5) public-poll points. A split gives each tied player `DEBATE_ROUND_SPLIT` (0.5) in its own landing states. A player who lost to a counter loses `DEBATE_BACKFIRE` (0.5) where its move aimed. Gains use the normal split and losses go to undecided, all from one start-of-round snapshot (D-001). The polls are republished after every round with the same error terms (`Polls.refresh`), so the map shows each round live. The winner gains crowd by its margin (at most 4), ×Zinger. A final crowd lead of `DEBATE_OVATION` (8) is a standing ovation: +2 momentum in every battleground. There is no flat national bonus (0.05 a crowd point was invisible under poll noise). A Zinger is spent whether the round is won or lost, and a second one is `ZingerSpent`.

**Timeouts and disconnects.** No move in time is a canned answer: Defend at a flat `DEBATE_CANNED` (2). It never earns or suffers a counter and never backfires, and a round won against it gains at most 1 crowd. A disconnect during a debate spends at most one grace use (D-005); a second drop in the same debate gets canned answers at once.

**N players and fairness.** "Their platform" means any opponent's. The counter is earned once if your move counters any non-canned opponent's. The unique top score wins and a tie at the top splits. "Trail the crowd" means below the top. `Debate.spec` checks the seat-swap mirror, three-player debates and determinism.

**Payloads and View.** `{ move = "Defend" | "Attack" | "Pivot", zinger = true? }` is validated whole: `BadMove`, `BadShape`, `ZingerSpent`, `WrongPhase`. A plan carrying a move is `UnexpectedMove`. A client can never send the canned flag. `view.debate` (Debate phase only) carries the week, round, topics, crowd, Zingers left, finished rounds, the board, and `yourMove` (yours alone). The opponent's pending move is `PlayerState.debateMove` and `View.spec` forbids the key. `DebateResult` keeps its old fields for older readers: a "topic" is a round, `gaffes` are backfires, and `focus`/`prep`/`national` are always 0. It adds `rounds`, `crowd` and `ovation`. The Focus decision, `debateFocus`, `focusEdge`, `debateStat`, `DEBATE_STAT`, `DEBATE_MAX_STAT_GAP`, `DEBATE_FOCUS`, `DEBATE_PLATFORM_BONUS`, `DEBATE_ROLL_MAX`, `Debate.odds`, the topic-win, national, sweep and gaffe numbers, and `TIMER_DEBATE` are gone.

**Flow and timers.** The flow is: intro (Show, `TIMER_DEBATE_INTRO` 3 s), then three times round (Input, `TIMER_DEBATE_ROUND` 15 s; it ends early once both lock in) and clash (Reveal, `TIMER_DEBATE_CLASH` 4 s), then verdict (Reveal, `TIMER_DEBATE_VERDICT` 5 s, skippable), then a stance if it is a Choice week, then the plan. All deadlines are absolute server times (`Session.setStage`). The worst case per debate is 65 s, against 30 s for the focus and reveal before. On the D-005 steps that is 1,050 s, the 17.5-minute line, which `Config.spec` pins. **True worst case with every stance and debate step run out: 1,050 + 8 × 20 = 1,210 s (20:10); it was 940 + 200 = 1,140 s (19:00).** Typical: a round locks in after about 7 s, so a debate takes about 41 s against about 18 s before, roughly +46 s a match.

**Bots (all from their own View, D-057).**
- Medium (the harness bot) values each cell of the round's 3×3 table by its result first (win +1, split 0, loss −1), plus at most half a point for what its map effects are worth. Map worth is EV × closeness of the landing states, the bot's usual weight. It plays the move with the best worst cell.
- Medium stakes its Zinger where its landing states are worth most: it compares this round with every later topic judged on today's facts, shares ties evenly by its own rng, and stakes in round 3 otherwise.
- Easy plays a random move half the time (`BOT_EASY_SLIP`) and stakes in a random round.
- Hard best-replies to the move Medium would play from the opponent's side (`BOT_DEBATE_READ` 0.15). At 0.5 Hard read Medium so well that it won 86% of matches.
- Extreme also blends in the opponent's own past moves this match (`BOT_EXTREME_HISTORY` 0.5).

New harness styles: `RandomMove`, `NoAnswer`, `AlwaysDefend`, `AlwaysAttack`, `AlwaysPivot`; `RandomFocus` and `NoFocus` are gone.

**Tuning (from the proposal).**
- `DEBATE_PIVOT_BASE` 3 → 4. At 3, always Defending beat the standard bot 52.3% ± 2.5 (1,500 matches), so Defend was dominant. At 4 the three always-one-move bots measure 32–39%.
- `DEBATE_ROUND_WIN` 1.0 → 0.8. At 1.0 debates decided 25.9% of winners (2,000 matches); at 0.8, 23.7%.
- Hale's Zinger: ×3 measured 43.7%, ×4 49.2%, two Zingers at ×2 41.2%, two at ×3 48.9% (1,400 matches each, Hale against the field). ×4 is the simplest of the two that sit in the band.
- The paper model for the final numbers: rounds pre-decided by public facts 22% (round 1 33%, rounds 2–3 17%). A move that cannot lose exists 45% of the time (round 1 69%).

**Harness** (`lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 2000`; "before" is this branch's start, `front-page` v0.12.0, on the same seeds).

| Check | After | Before | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| Debates change the winner (recount) / EV swung | **23.7%** / 19.3 EV | 25.9% / 22.9 EV | 15–25% |
| Medium's moves (D / A / P) | 25.5 / 31.9 / 42.6% | — (one focus) | each 20–50% |
| AlwaysDefend / AlwaysAttack / AlwaysPivot vs Medium | 37.2 / 34.8 / 36.2% | — | each ≤ 45% |
| RandomMove / NoAnswer vs Medium | 37.6 / 17.8% | RandomFocus 51.9 / NoFocus 26.6% | ≤ 42 / ≤ 35% |
| Rounds a player had a can't-lose move / and did not lose | 44.2% / 100% | — | informational |
| Rounds that split (bot vs bot) | 31.6% | — | ≤ 35% (two bots both take the safe move) |
| Zinger staked in rounds 1 / 2 / 3; staked rounds won | 51 / 24 / 25%; 41% | — | — (the audit's +3 Zinger: 93% in round 1) |
| Standing ovations per match | 0.14 | — | — |
| Candidates | Okafor **58.8**, Calloway 53.0, Venkat 49.5, Hale 47.5, Alvarado **43.8**, Reyes 48.7, Strand 46.8, Begay 52.1 | Okafor 53.3, Calloway **57.4**, Venkat **55.1**, Hale 48.7, Alvarado **43.1**, Reyes 47.3, Strand 48.8, Begay 46.6 | 45–55% |
| Comeback / 40+ EV / 100+ EV | 38.5 / 57.8 / 15.7% | 37.5 / 60.8 / 20.8% | ✓ |
| Difficulty (400 matches): Easy / Hard / Extreme vs Medium, Extreme vs Hard | 18.8 / 67.5 / 76.5 / 58.8% | 17.0 / 64.3 / 78.3 / 60.3% | 15–25 / 60–70 / 75–85 / > 50 |

*For the designers.*
1. Okafor moved up (+5.5, about one and a half standard deviations at 480 games) and Alvarado is where she was. A candidate pass is the next step, as D-047 said.
2. Bots split 32% of rounds because both take the safe move when there is one. People should split less. The playtest checklist asks.
3. The splits pay +0.5 each, so a split still moves the map. If they feel flat, the lever is a crowd tie-breaker.
4. The "Safe" tag shows only in a player's first debate of the session, with a coach tip.
5. The old Reveal debate scoreboard (`CampaignParts/Reveal.luau`, its debate rows) is no longer reached: debates have their own screen. Its owner can remove it.

## The end screen: polls against the result, votes week by week

### D-073 — Results gets Map and Graph tabs; the match keeps its weekly projected votes (no rule or Config changes, Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "an option at the end screen to switch between the final polls vs the real results", and "another screen on the end screen where you could see the EV polls week by week on a graph".

**Data (Sim, public only).** The final polls were already in the end-of-match View (`view.states[i].poll` is the poll published after the last week, the one players last saw; `view.projectedEV` its projection) and so was election night (`view.election.results`: winner and margin per state). The week-by-week projection was not kept anywhere, so the match now keeps it: `MatchState.pollHistory` (`State.PollPoint { week, ev }`) gets `Polls.projectedEV` each time the weekly polls are published: week 0 after the draft, week w at the end of week w (after its reveal's "New polls are in"; a debate's republished polls show up in its week's point). The View carries `pollHistory` only from election night on (the same gate as `election`), identical for every player, so it survives a rejoin; the client records nothing. It reads public numbers only and draws nothing from the seed: `lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400` prints the same report before and after (only the run time differs). `tests/sim/PollHistory.spec`.

**Rules for the screen (Shared/UI, pure, tested).** `PollMiss`: a state is a **miss** when its final poll had a leader, the count had a winner, and they differ (a tied poll called nobody, so it cannot miss). Misses are listed most votes first: "Polls missed 3 states: CA, SC, OK" (at most 6 named, then "+2 more"; none: "The final polls called every state right."). The real-results map paints each state as a poll whose lead is its margin, at least 3 points (`RESULT_MIN_LEAD`), so a narrow real win still shows its winner's color instead of the toss-up grey; the pill shows the true margin ("+0.4"). `EVChart`: the points (Start, weeks 1–12, Result), a y range that always includes 270 on whole 50- or 100-vote steps within 0–538, x positions (the result 1.5 steps after the last week, set apart), each line piece as a Frame centered on its midpoint and turned by `atan2` (GuiObject.Rotation turns about the center, clockwise, y down), x labels thinned to every k-th week so none overlap (Start and Result always kept), and one marker per debate and Choice event with its line ("Week 4 · Debate: Rex won 2 of 3 topics", "Week 6 · Plant at a crossroads: you chose …; Rex chose …").

**Screen.** Results pins a three-chip row at the top once the election is in: **Summary | Map | Graph** (`Tab_*`), Summary first and unchanged (banner, Landslide stamp, why you won). A new match opens on Summary; a repeat payload of the same match keeps the tab. No election (a forfeit): no tabs. **Map** (`ResultsParts/MapTab`): a two-chip switch "Final polls" | "Real results", the mode's votes ("Final polls: you 235, Rex 277" / "Election night: you 290, Rex 248"), the miss line, the map (made the first time the tab opens, driven only through `MapInterface`: `setView`, `setMarks`, `setLens`, `select`, `onSelect`) with a lead pill per state in the leader's color and a **MISS** tag edged in the real winner's color on every miss in both modes, so flipping the switch shows what changed under the tags; tapping a state says "California · 54 votes. Final poll: Rex +1. Result: you by 1.3 points.
