The polls missed it." **Graph** (`ResultsParts/GraphTab`): one card, "Votes in the polls, by week", a legend (you first), the chart drawn from Frames: gridlines with their numbers, the 270 line in white, each player's line (3 px rotated pieces) with a dot per week and a bigger white-ringed dot for the result (the piece into it a little see-through: polls end there, the count begins), "D"/"S" chips over debate and stance weeks with a faint guide down the plot, each a 44 px tap target that writes its week's line under the chart (selected = white outline), and "You: 265 at the start, 235 in the final polls, 290 on election night." The map and the chart are sized to the room under the tabs so neither tab scrolls (the snapshot shows the map tab 58 px short on desktop: the fake engine has no top-bar inset, the snapshot does). `default.project.json` sets `StarterGui.ClipsDescendantsSupportsRotation = Enabled` so rotated pieces clip inside a scrolling frame. Still four text sizes (the tabs use three). Shots `23d-results-map`, `23e-results-map-real` (a missed state tapped), `23f-results-graph`, `23g-results-graph-marker`: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

**Tests.** `PollHistory.spec` (one point per published week, equal to the projection at that time, the last equal to the final polls, deterministic, in the View only from election night, more than two players), `PollMiss.spec` (misses, ties, agreement with `Polls.leader` and with `election.ev`, the summary and its cap, the result map, the words), `EVChart.spec` (points, range, x positions, segments land on both points, labels never overlap on a phone, markers and their lines, the summary), `ResultsTabs.spec` (tabs, switching, MISS tags in both modes, a tapped state, every dot and line piece, pieces where EVChart puts them, markers' lines and 44 px targets, forfeit, strict audit on every tab at three sizes).

## Integration: Landslide 0.13.0

### D-074 — The worst-case timer line moves from 17.5 to 19 minutes when the reveal and live debates land together (no timer changed, pending the designers, Oct 24, 2026)

**What happened.** Two approved branches each spent the D-005 worst-case budget on their own: the week's reveal grows with its steps up to `TIMER_RESOLVE_MAX` 14 s (D-066, +72 s: 940 → 1,012 s) and debates became three live rounds of up to 65 s each (D-072, +110 s: 940 → 1,050 s, exactly the 17.5-minute line). Together, every timer run out on the D-005 steps is **1,122 s (18 min 42 s)**, 72 s past the line; with every stance screen run out too it is 1,282 s (21:22). Merging kept both features and every timer as their branches set them; `Config.spec` now pins 1,122 s under a 19-minute line.

**Typical play is still inside D-005's 15 minutes.** A scratch run of 60 matches on the server's clock (both seats bots acting after human-like think times: 15 s Select, 10 s a draft round, 60/70/80 % of the plan timer in weeks 1–4/5–8/9–12 as in D-005, 10 s a stance, 7 s a debate round; every reveal and election night run to its end, no skips): mean **12:31** (median 12:32, p90 12:46, max 13:00).

**For the designers: keep the 19-minute line, or pull one lever** (each alone brings the worst case back to 17.5 minutes or under):
- `TIMER_PLAN` 50 → 44 s (−72 s), the largest pool, but it is thinking time on every week.
- `TIMER_RESOLVE_MAX` 14 → 8 s (−72 s): undoes the reveal's longer busy weeks.
- `TIMER_DEBATE_ROUND` 15 → 11 s and `TIMER_DEBATE_CLASH` 4 → 3 s (−30 s) with `TIME_BANK` 30 → 9 s (−42 s).

## The tutorial and slower bot matches

### D-075 — The first bot match is an untimed tutorial; bot levels scale the choice timers; Sound and Reduce motion move to the Lobby's corner (Oct 24, 2026)

**Ask.** The designers: "players get unlimited time in the tutorial", "more time against easy bots" (Easy ×2, Medium ×1.5), "allow players to replay the tutorial in the 'how to play' menu", "move the sound and reduce motion toggles to the corner".

**The tutorial.** A player's first match against a bot is the tutorial (the one with the first-match coach tips). Every step that waits for the player has **no deadline**: Select, each draft round, a stance, each debate round and every weekly plan wait until the player locks in. Reveals, the draft reveal, debate clashes and the verdict, the map reveal and election night still run on their own and keep their skips. The bot still answers at once. Inside `Net/Session` the stage's deadline is `Session.NO_DEADLINE` (`math.huge`), so every comparison, `math.min` (`nextWake`) and the reconnect window stay plain arithmetic; it never leaves the server: `StatePayload.deadline` is **nil** for such a step (and `tutorial = true` on every State of the match). Every client reader handles nil: the header shows **"No timer"** in the clock's place (muted, no bar, no ticks), the plan hides **+15 s**, AutoLock never fires, the debug UI reads "no timer"; reveals and election night always have a number. The time bank is refused in a step without a deadline (`NoTimer`). A disconnect still ends it: the grace period runs as before and a match nobody is in is abandoned. The player is told as it starts (notice `Tutorial`: "Tutorial: no timer on any choice, so take your time.").

**"Tutorial done" lives in the saved profile** (`Profile.tutorialDone`, optional, no version bump). It is set when the tutorial match ends in an election or a forfeit (Leave match, or missed weeks); an abandoned one (the player quit the game) leaves it unset, so the next bot match is the tutorial again. `Profile.needsTutorial`: a loaded profile without the flag **and with no game on record** (rated or practice). Players from before this change already have games, so they never get an untimed match they did not ask for; a profile that has not loaded (or a DataStore outage) means an ordinary match. The first tutorial keeps the bot level the player picked.

**Replay.** How to play gets **"Play the tutorial"** under its pages (on every page; the sheet grows from 70 to 82 % of the screen height so the tallest page still fits on a 720 px screen), with the line "A match against the Easy bot, with tips and no timers." It sends `{ op = "Practice", difficulty = "Easy", tutorial = true }`; the server always plays the **Easy** bot for a replay whatever level arrives (`Lobby.practice(..., "Replay")`), so the replay is gentle and the same for everyone. The client resets the coach tips (`CoachTip.reset`) just before, so every tip shows again in that match. Any player can replay any time. The schema keeps only `tutorial = true` and never rejects the field.

**Bot timers.** Against a practice bot every choice timer is multiplied by its level: `BOT_TIME_MULT_EASY` = 2, `BOT_TIME_MULT_MEDIUM` = 1.5 (the designers' numbers), Hard and Extreme ×1 (`Session.timeScale`). Plan 50 → 100 / 75 s, Select 38 → 76 / 57 s, a draft round 20 → 40 / 30 s, a stance 20 → 40 / 30 s, a debate round 15 → 30 / 22.5 s. Not scaled: the map reveal, the draft reveal, the week's reveal, the debate's intro, clashes and verdict, election night, the time bank (still 15 s taps of 30 s), the disconnect grace and the reconnect window. People against people, and the harness bots (an explicit style, no level), keep every timer. Draft and Select's time bars now run over the stage's own length (the payload's `deadline − stageStart`) instead of the Config timer, so a doubled round's bar starts full. D-005 has the match-time notes. Bot-vs-bot balance is untouched (the sim has no timers).

**Corner settings.** On the Lobby, Sound and Reduce motion leave the column and become two 44 px icon toggles in the top-right corner (`Settings.corner`): inside the Game ScreenGui's `CoreUISafeInsets` area (clear of Roblox's own buttons at the top left, of a notch and of the iPad's status bar) and above the leaderboard card. Each is outlined and its icon lit while its setting is on (the sound icon also switches to volume-x, struck through as a glyph); each carries its label as attribute `Label` ("Sound on", "Reduce motion off"), and a small tooltip at the pair's left (same row, so it covers nothing under the corner) shows it while a mouse or gamepad selection is on the toggle (`MouseEnter`/`SelectionGained`) and for 1.5 s after a tap. The in-match settings stay where they were (the Help sheet's How to win tab and the header's sound button).

**Tests.** `tests/net/Tutorial.spec` (the multipliers at every level for every stage kind over whole matches, a real timeout at 100 s, people and harness bots unchanged; the tutorial: nil on the wire and math.huge inside, a day-long wait, a whole match with a slow player, the notice first, no bank, only finite numbers on the wire, disconnect and reconnect; `Lobby.practice` replay and first; the schema; the profile flag), `tests/e2e/Tutorial.spec` (first bot match untimed, forfeit saves the flag, the next one timed ×1.5; replay always Easy; an abandoned tutorial comes back; a returning player never gets it), `tests/ui/Tutorial.spec` ("No timer" and no +15 s at three sizes, no auto-lock, the Easy bot's 1:40 plan, live Select and draft, Play the tutorial's request and the tips reset, the corner toggles at three sizes with their labels and tooltip). Specs that wait for a practice timer to run out now start as returning players (`Rig.practice`, `Support.returning`). Shots `01`, `01h`, `02f`–`02j`, `09t`, `09u`: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.
### D-076 — Issues get symbols instead of short codes (no rule, sim, server, payload or Config changes, Oct 24, 2026, branch `issue-icons`)

**Asked.** "Give issues symbols instead of using abbreviations." The map's Issues lens showed each state's top issue as a three-letter code (JOB, HLT, SCH, ENV, SAF, TRN, TEC, FRM) that players had to decode.

**The symbols.** Eight original white glyphs on transparent, 256x256, drawn by `assets/issues/make.py` (PIL, 8x supersampled) on the Lucide icons' 24-unit grid with round caps, a heavier line (2.5 units) and a filled core in each, so they still read at 16 px: Jobs a briefcase, Health a heart with a pulse line cut through it (no cross emblem), Schools a graduation cap, Environment a leaf, Safety a shield with a check mark, Roads & Transit a bus from the front, Technology a microchip, Farms & Rural a stalk of wheat. Fictional and neutral: no logos, flags, party or campaign symbols. Ids live in `UI/IssueIcons.luau`, "" until the lead uploads them (`assets/issues/README.md`).

**One helper family in Kit.** `Kit.issueIcon(issue, size?, color?)` is the symbol alone (ImageLabel "IssueIcon", attributes IssueIcon and IssueName; before the upload, the old code as text). `Kit.issueTag(issue, props?)` is symbol + full name (the name alone before the upload). `Kit.issueLead(label, issue?, size?)` puts the symbol in front of a label's or button's own text, tinted like the text; it hides itself while the box is too narrow for the symbol and the word at 12 px, so a word always stays readable (UI-STYLE §2.6: an icon never carries meaning alone on a button). `Kit.setIssueIcon` is the map's plate version. `Kit.MIN_ICON` (14 px) is the symbols' floor.

**Where.** Symbol alone only where the screen also names it:
- **Map, Issues lens:** the symbol on the same lens plate (16 px, filled in your color when it is on your platform), inside the existing mark layout; `LensPart.icon` and `LegendItem.mark = "issue"` are additive, so the Lens/NodeMap API is unchanged. The legend row names the symbols on the map (your platform's first, the others dropped first on a phone), the Map key lists all eight with names in the Issues lens, and tapping a state opens the card with its top issues by symbol and name.
- **Symbol + name:** Select's issue cards, the state card's message and pivot chips and its "Top issues" row, the event strip's plate and the event's effect line for a spotlight or a stance, the debate's round label and its intro's topics, and the Help pictures (the debate scoreboard; the eight issues under Actions).
- **How to play** gets a sixth page, "Your issues": the eight symbols with their names and 29 words. D-059 planned five pages; the key did not fit on the Actions page without pushing Back/Next below the fold on a phone, so this is the one extra page (the designers may prefer to drop it: the in-match Help's Actions tab and the Map key carry the same list).
- Sentences (feeds, reveal lines, verdict lines) keep the full names: there were no codes there.

**Audit.** New rule `iconFloor`: an issue symbol under `Kit.MIN_ICON` either way; `tokenColor` also checks a symbol's tint. The fallback code is ordinary text under the text rules. The headless engine now applies a text object's UIPadding to its children (as Roblox does) so a lead symbol is judged where it really sits; other padding is still ignored. The specs and snapshots preview the symbols as if uploaded (`IssueIcons.preview`, like `NodeMap.preview`); `IssueIcons.spec` also audits the fallback. The snapshot renderer draws the local PNGs tinted (an SVG mask).

## Review fixes: server, saved data, names

### D-077 — Leaving before week 1 is unrated; one pair plays at most 3 rated games a day (rule change, Oct 24, 2026, branch `bugfix-server`)

**Why.** The security review found that a rated private-code match plus an instant Forfeit lets a player trade wins with a second account: host, the alt joins and forfeits in Select, repeat. Private matches stay rated (D-059's intent), so the fix limits what a throwaway match can earn.

**Rules.**
- A match that ends in a forfeit by a player who forfeited, or dropped and never came back, **before week 1 begins** (Select, the map reveal, the draft) is unrated for both players (`Session.Result.unrated`). Nobody's rating, totals or record move; both players get the notice "The match ended before week 1, so it doesn't count for anyone's rating." A player who drops early but comes back and plays on leaves the match rated. Practice is unchanged.
- At most `RATED_PAIR_DAILY_MAX` (3) rated games between the same two players in `RATED_PAIR_WINDOW` (24 h). A fourth match still plays, unrated, and both players are told as it starts ("This match is unrated: you two already played 3 rated matches today."). Each profile keeps the times of its recent rated games per opponent (`Profile.ratedWith`), so the count follows the players across servers.

**Config.** New `RATED_PAIR_DAILY_MAX` 3, `RATED_PAIR_WINDOW` 86400. Also new, server limits rather than rules: `SET_COLOR_COOLDOWN` 1 s and `SET_NAME_COOLDOWN` 2 s (a client spamming SetColor or SetName could burn the server's DataStore write budget and its text-filter calls). Saved data is now written in a batch every 15 s, unchanged profiles not at all; a match result is still saved at once.

**For the designers.** A real rematch streak with a friend stops counting after three games a day; say if that is too few.

### D-078 — A removed custom name blocks whole words only; review queue split; reports counted per day (rule change, Oct 24, 2026, branch `bugfix-server`)

**Why.** The reviewed blocklist used the static list's loose match (contains, one edit away), so removing a short name hit innocent ones ("Ann" blocked "Hannah", "Joanna") and the server then deleted those names from their owners' saved data.

**Rules.**
- A name a reviewer removes is stored as its folded words (`CustomName.reviewEntry`: "Captain Zed" → `=captain zed`) and matches **whole words only**: "Captain Zed", "CAPTAIN  ZED", "CaptainZed" and "Captain Zed Jr" are blocked; "Captain Zeb", "Hannah" and "Annabel" are not. Entries saved by 0.13.0 (no "=") match the exact name. The static list (`server/NameBlocklist`) keeps its own rules.
- Only a saved name that **is** the removed name is cleared from its owner's saved data. A saved name that only contains it is not used (the candidate's own name shows, with a notice) but stays saved.
- Keep and Remove carry the name the admin saw and act only if it is still the queued one ("That name changed since the list loaded").
- Remove writes the blocklist first and takes the report off the queue only after that write worked, so a failed write changes nothing. A reload merges the stored list with what the server already has.
- Until a server has read the reviewed blocklist once, no new name passes (fail closed); it retries every `NAME_BLOCKLIST_RETRY` seconds.

**Config.** `NAME_REPORTS_PER_VISIT` 10 becomes `NAME_REPORTS_PER_DAY` 10 per `NAME_REPORT_WINDOW` (24 h), kept in the profile so rejoining no longer resets it. New `NAME_REVIEW_SHARDS` 8: the queue is split over eight DataStore keys by setter (each key takes at most 4 MB of writes a minute), and 0.13.0's single key is still read. New `NAME_BLOCKLIST_RETRY` 20 s.

## Map polish: a phone map you can read

### D-079 — The map stands up on a phone; one line per state; fewer, better labels (no rule or Config changes, Oct 24, 2026, branch `map-polish`)

**Ask.** The user: "audit, polish, and improve the map views". A design audit of the 0.13.0 snapshots found the iPad and desktop Votes map close to done and the phone the problem: at 390 px the map was 390x244 (28 % of the screen; `PORTRAIT_MAP_MAX` never bound because the width did), and the mark layout dropped about 33 of 51 values in every lens (PA, OH, MI, NC, VA, NY and all nine chips). Reveal and Results broke the "about 8 labels" rule (§4.6). This entry is the twelve fixes and three bigger improvements, and where they were done differently.

**1. Two layouts (`Shared/UI/MapShape.layout`).** `wide` is the old box (1.6, chips in a column). `tall` is 1.25 for a phone in portrait: the land fills the width (12 % bigger than in `wide`), the nine chips stand in one row under it (each 40 px tall and about 42 px wide on a 390 px phone), the legend under the chips. Both come from the same atlases and outlines by a scale and a shift, so no image changed and the snapshot renderer needed none. `MapShape.fit(w, h)` picks: tall only when the box is narrower than `TALL_BELOW` (480 px) and standing up shows more land; the map picks it from the room its frame gives it, and screens size the frame with `map:aspectFor(w, hMax)` (new on `MapInterface`; the circles answer 1.6), so both agree. Campaign's phone map may now take half the body (`PORTRAIT_MAP_MAX` 0.42 → 0.5): 390x312 on a 390x844 phone, the audit's number. Election night and Results stand up too; Decision and Debate keep their short boxes, where the wide map is the bigger one. iPad and desktop keep `wide` and their layout as a whole.

**2. One line per state on a phone (`ShapeMap`, maps under 480 px).** Each state gets "PA 19": the code (12 to 16 px; Kit.MIN_TEXT is 12) and its value on one line. The audit asked for a 12 px number; the value stays at the caption size (14), the type scale's floor for fixed text. The lines are laid out biggest state first, each on its anchor or nudged up to 28 px off it with a thin leader, never over another line. A state under 24 px of room keeps its full line only within 10 px of its anchor, else it shows its value alone (the audit's "the value replaces the code"); where a line with a value cannot fit, the last resort is the value alone, never the code alone, because a code alone next to a neighbor's value reads as that value's. Alaska and Hawaii always keep their codes (an inset's title). Result on the phone Votes map: all 51 values, 44 with their codes (before: 18 values). Chips show code over value when they are tall enough, side by side when wide (iPad), and drop what does not fit them. Big maps are unchanged: code on the anchor, value under it.

**3. Polls is one pill per state.** The lead, then ▲ momentum and the ground-game pips inside the same plate ("+8 ▲ ●●"); a state with no lead shows none of them. The separate `Extras` row is gone (the names `Momentum` and `Pips` now live in `Lens`).

**4. Flips.** A flip is the 3 px outline in the new leader's color (as before) and, on the 5 biggest flips by votes, a one-character tag: ▲ when it comes your way, ▼ when it goes away from you, edged in the new leader's color. `SwingMarks.LABELS` (8) caps every preview, reveal and reveal step: flip tags first, then the biggest moves (`SwingMarks.MOVES` 5) in what is left; everything else only glows. The step marks used to label every state a move touched.

**5. Plan preview.** Preview tags never cover each other (they count as codes for one another), and on a phone the tag rides at the end of its state's line ("19 +9.4"), the states in your plan laid out first. The "!" badges the audit saw are the headless renderer's stand-in glyph for the Rally icon; in game the badge is the megaphone image (`UI/Icons`), so nothing changed there.

**6. Results misses.** A miss is a dashed 2 px outline in the real winner's color and "×" after its value in the same pill (a chip: its edge in the winner's color, as UIStroke cannot dash). The MISS tags are gone; the miss line under the map still names them.

**7. Northeast leaders.** At rest one hairline bracket ties the chips to the coast (a brace along the column, its point at the coast; on the tall map a line down the sea and along the row). A chip's own leader shows only while it is selected or hovered (MouseEnter).

**8. Pins.** At rest a pin is its face (14 to 20 px) and pointer; yours is 2 px bigger and drawn on top, ringed in your color, the rival's in theirs, so "which one is me" still reads at a glance (the user asked for it in D-070). The name tab shows in the first week (`Pin.label`, set by `Pins.of`) and while the pin's state is selected. Pins never sit on a code or a value (unchanged).

**9. Alaska and Hawaii.** Hairline inset boxes in the Pacific under California: Hawaii at 1.4x its generated size at the bottom, Alaska at 0.45x above it. The audit asked for Hawaii at 2x; with Alaska beside it that ran into Texas, and stacked, 2x Hawaii pushes Alaska into California, so 1.4x (the islands about 70 px across on a phone) is the most that fits. A spec keeps every other state's land out of both boxes. Hawaii's label stands in its box's free sea, not on the Big Island.

**10. Money.** Only the 8 richest pools (`Lens.MONEY_TOP`) are marked, with 1 to 3 gold coins (the coins icon in Amber; see the review fixes); the rest are on their cards. The audit offered "dim the rest" as well; dimming fills would hide who leads, which every lens keeps, so the rest just carry no value.

**11. One meaning for slate.** Slate stays "toss-up". Election night draws a state not called yet hollow (the stage's field color and a hairline outline, `Mark.hollow`); a dimmed state (Decision, Debate) keeps 60 % of its band fill (`DIM_FILL` 0.45 → 0.4 transparency), never a flat slate.

**12. Decision legend.** A stance's preview outlines the states it helps in `good` green and those it hurts in `loss` orange (not your player color, which is also the "leans you" fill), the 5 largest gains carry a green "+" tag, and the legend's two rings use the same colors. A preview's outline now outranks an owner's ring while it shows. Debate move previews use the same colors.

**Bigger improvements.**
- **Close filter.** A "Close" chip beside the lens chips (Campaign and Draft; on a phone the Key chip becomes its icon to make room) quiets every state more than `Lens.CLOSE` (8) points from close: its fill at 40 % and no value. It is remembered for the session like the lens and works in every lens. **Off by default on phones too:** with the tall map every value now shows, and a new player planning a path to 270 needs the safe states' votes as much as the close ones; the shots `14b`/`14c` look good with it on, but not clearly better than the full board. A playtest question asks.
- **Reveal as motion.** During a reveal the fills tween to each step's polls (`setView(view, colors, animate)`; instant under Reduce motion) while the header's votes count (as before), and the step tags are capped as in 4.
- **Election night.** Each call shakes its state (`map:shake`, nothing under Reduce motion); the state that will take someone to 270 is outlined in white before its call (and keeps the ring and glow after); the "?" tag on the state everyone waits on is gone, so the race bar's "270!" stamp is the night's one big label.

**Display constants, not gameplay.** `MapShape.TALL_ASPECT` 1.25, `TALL_BELOW` 480, `INSET_AK` 0.45, `INSET_HI` 1.4; `ShapeMap.COMPACT_W` 480, `LENS_ROOM` 24, `NUDGES` up to 28 px, `SMALL_REACH` 10, `DIM_FILL` 0.4, `QUIET_FILL` 0.6, `HOLLOW_FILL` 0.35; `Lens.MONEY_TOP` 8, `Lens.CLOSE` 8; `SwingMarks.LABELS` 8, `FLIP_TAGS` 5, `MOVES` 5; Campaign `PORTRAIT_MAP_MAX` 0.5. No Config value changed.

**Issue symbols (D-076, merged).** On the real map an issue's symbol sits alone at 20 px on a 24 px disc (filled in your color when it is on your platform), the slot this entry left for it; the code still shows while the image is missing. Money's coin is a separate field, `LensPart.glyph` (a `Kit.icon` name, `glyphs` times, `glyphColor`), so `LensPart.icon` keeps D-076's meaning.

**Review fixes (design re-check, same day).** The audit's re-check accepted the 14 px floor, Hawaii at 1.4x, Money without dimming, the "!" placeholder and Close off by default, and asked for five more:
1. *Orphan values on a phone.* A label now tries, in order: its line ("PA 19"; a small state, only within 10 px and after the stack); its code at 12 px stacked over the value; and last its value alone, which always stands off its state's anchor (up to 55 px) so a hairline leader shows whose it is. The code alone is never a fallback (beside a neighbor's value it would read as that value's). Small states try the narrower stack first, which leaves their neighbors room. Phone Votes: 48 of 51 values with their code (was 44), the other 3 with a leader; Results' "+3 ×" no longer sits on Texas.
2. *Plan preview and reveal tags on a phone.* The plan's action badge leads its state's line and the preview number ends it ("! OH 17 +5.4"); a reveal's tag (▲ / ▼, a move's points) ends it too, so none lands on a neighbor's value, and the line's leader covers all of it.
3. *Money.* The 8 richest pools show 1 to 3 gold coins by how far they are above the median pool (`Lens.COIN_TIERS` 1.12x and 1.25x), not five near-identical "$6.1"; the dollars are on the state card. The coins sit on their state's line like any value.
4. *Hawaii.* Its inset box is filled in its state's color (`INSET_FILL` 0.55, under the islands), like a chip, so who leads reads even where the islands are specks.
5. *▲▲.* The Polls legend says "▲▲ strong" and the Map key "▲ momentum (▲▲ strong)".
Pins also keep 3 px clear above and below when they look for a spot (the fake engine draws a value's plate a little taller than its estimate).

**One label form, every state (user feedback after 0.14.0).** "Just put the number of votes in a consistent position relative to the state initials every time. The variety of placements causes confusion." Every state with a lens value now wears one plate, at every map size: its code, then the value right after it (2 px gap), on one dark plate ("PA 19"; a battleground's plate outlined in white with "★13"). No stacked code-over-value, no value alone, no value under the code on big maps. The same text size for every plate on a map: the layout tries 14, 13 and 12 px (12 is the floor) and keeps the largest at which every plate finds a clear spot no farther than 5 % of the map's width past its state; a plate that does not fit on its state moves whole to the nearest clear spot with a hairline leader. The Northeast chips wear the same plate at the same size (in the tall layout they now stand in two rows, VT NH MA RI CT / NJ DE MD DC, so a "VT +16" plate fits a cell; the box is 1.1, 390x354 on a phone; Campaign's phone map may take 57 % of the body, election night 43 %), and so do Alaska and Hawaii. A chip's reveal tag and plan preview follow its plate inside its cell. The code is right-aligned against the gap and the value left-aligned after it, so the spacing never varies with how wide the letters draw. Measured on the Plan fixture (Votes): phone 12 px, 6 plates with a leader (3 of them more than 20 px off); iPad 12 px, 1; desktop 14 px, 1.
*Other lenses, same form.* Polls: "PA [+8] ▲ ●●", the lead a filled pill inside the plate (phone 12 px, 20 leaders: the plates are wider). Issues: "PA [symbol]", the symbol on its disc inside the plate. Money: "PA $$" on the 8 richest pools; the other states, which have no value in this lens, show their bare code (as on Decision and Debate, which set no lens). The Close filter's quiet states show their bare code too. Results' map: "CA [+9] ×". Before, each lens mixed the same per-state variety (line, stack, value alone).
*Constants* (ShapeMap, display only): `PLATE_SIZES` 14/13/12, `PLATE_PAD` 2, `PLATE_PAD_Y` 2, `PLATE_GAP` 2, `PLATE_REACH` 0.05, `VALUE_EM` 0.6, `CAPS_EM` (I, J, L, M, W), `FAR_NUDGES` up to 78 px; MapShape `TALL_ASPECT` 1.25 to 1.1, `TALL_CHIP_PX` 40 to 38, `TALL_CHIP_ROWS` 2. The map root carries `PlateSize`, `PlateLeaders` and `PlateMisses` attributes for the specs. Tests: `ShapeMap.spec` "gives every state the same plate" (every state at 390x360, 390x244, 620x388, 832x520: plate, code, then value 2 px after it on the same line, one text size, never under 12, no two plates overlap, the leader count matches), `MapShape.spec` (two chip rows in reading order, cells wide enough), `Lens.spec` (the Votes plate and its white battleground outline).

**Tests.** `tests/sim/MapShape.spec` (both layouts: sprites, insets, chips and legend inside the box, no state's land in an inset, the tall chip row under all land at 36 px, taps by outline, `fit` and `heightFor` agree with themselves, the bracket), `tests/ui/ShapeMap.spec` (the bracket and the selected chip's leader, the tall layout and switching to it, every value on one line with no two labels overlapping at 390x312, the Polls pill, dashed / hollow / quiet, quiet pins, preview tags apart, strict audit at six sizes), new `tests/ui/MapLabels.spec` (8 tags at most in SwingMarks and at the end of every reveal fixture, the 5 biggest flips ▲ / ▼, green and orange previews, the Close filter on the map and remembered, election night hollow, white tipping outline, no tags, pins named in week 1), `Lens.spec` (Money's top 8 with 1 to 3 coins by the median, the pill, the ▲▲ legend), `ShapeMap.spec` (also: at most 3 values without their code on the phone, stacked codes, Hawaii's filled inset), `ResultsTabs.spec` (dashed outlines and ×), `Stances.spec` (outline colors, flip tags, the legend rings). Snapshots: new `14b-close-filter`, `14c-close-polls`; every shot 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

## The short tutorial

### D-080 — The tutorial is a short scenario: 4 weeks, one stance, one debate (rule change for the tutorial only, Oct 24, 2026, branch `short-tutorial`)

**Ask.** The user: "Shorten the tutorial." D-075's tutorial was a whole 12-week match against the Easy bot with no choice timers, about 13½ minutes for a brisk new player. Now it is a short scenario that still teaches every core idea and ends in about 5 to 6 minutes, then says "You're ready" and offers a real match.

**The schedule (`Config.tutorial(config)`).** The same rules on a short schedule. It returns `config` with seven keys replaced; every other number, `EV_TO_WIN` 270 included, is the real game's:

| Key | Real match | Tutorial | From |
|---|---|---|---|
| `WEEKS` | 12 | **4** | `TUTORIAL_WEEKS` |
| `DEBATE_WEEKS` | 4, 9 | **3** (one debate, three live rounds) | `TUTORIAL_DEBATE_WEEKS` |
| `EVENT_FIRST_WEEK` / `EVENT_LAST_WEEK` | 2 / 11 | **2 / 2** | `TUTORIAL_CHOICE_WEEK` |
| `EVENT_CHOICE_ONLY` (new) | false | **true**: week 2's event is a Choice (a stance), drawn from the seed among the deck's Choice events | — |
| `CLOSING_FROM_WEEK` | 10 | **4** (the last week's push) | `TUTORIAL_CLOSING_FROM_WEEK` |
| `START_CASH` | 8 | **8** (the real economy; see the tuning) | `TUTORIAL_START_CASH` |

The flow, one new idea a step: Select (candidate, 3 issues) → map reveal → the draft (home, two battlegrounds) → **week 1** plan (actions, money, the map) → **week 2** the stance, then the plan (the lenses) → **week 3** the debate's three rounds, then the plan → **week 4** the last plan (closing bonus; ground game) → election night → Results with "You're ready!". `EVENT_CHOICE_ONLY` is a new normal key (false): with it false, `Events.draw` deals exactly as before.

**Where it applies.** `Session.new` with `options.tutorial` builds the session on `Config.tutorial(config)`, so the match, the View, the bot and the timers all read it (`session.config`); the match is deterministic from its seed like any other. Nothing new goes on the wire: the client already gets `tutorial = true` on every State (D-075), and the Router sets `ctx.config` to `Config.tutorial(Config.defaults)` while a tutorial screen is up (back to the defaults in the Lobby), so "Week 2 of 4", the debate week, Help ("After week 4, election night counts them", "Debates open week 3", "The tutorial has one event, a Choice, in week 2") and Graph follow it. Help's tab intros are now refilled at every open (they were built once at start).

**Always the Easy bot.** The first tutorial now plays the Easy bot too (`Lobby.practice`); D-075 kept the level the player had picked. The short tutorial is tuned against Easy, and the user's ask names it. Unrated and the tutorial-done flag as in D-075.

**Tuning: no map scale needed** (`lune run tests/harness/tutorial.luau 400`: 400 matches a pairing, seeds 500001 on, seats alternating; the "player" is a bot standing in for a new player, always against the Easy bot). "Decided on election night": before the votes are counted, the state that carries the winner to 270 (its tipping state) is within 5 points of true margin (ground game counted), so election night's swing could still decide it; "toss-up": within 2 (`ELECTION_SIGMA`).

| Player vs Easy bot | Win | Decided on election night | Toss-up | Last poll's leader lost | Mean margin | Same on 12 weeks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Medium (a sharp new player) | 75.5 % | 99.8 % | 79.0 % | 20.0 % | 75 EV | 83.0 % · 76.0 % · 34.8 % · 12.5 % · 76 EV |
| Easy (a careless one) | 50.8 % | 99.5 % | 85.0 % | 20.5 % | 63 EV | 46.2 % · 78.5 % · 42.0 % · 14.2 % · 53 EV |
| Random (taps anything) | 0.0 % | 48.2 % | 3.5 % | 0.0 % | 215 EV | 0.0 % · 0 % · 0 % · 0 % · 316 EV |

Four weeks move the map less than twelve, so the draft's symmetric bonuses and the balanced leans keep the race *closer*, not wider: a sharp player beats the Easy bot 3 times in 4 (it neither steamrolls nor gets crushed), a careless one about half the time, and nearly every match is still open on election night. So leans, draft bonuses and action effects stay the real game's. `TUTORIAL_START_CASH` 12 measured the same (74.5 % / 50.2 %), so the tutorial keeps the real $8. Bot heuristics (`Bot.tuning`) are unchanged: the bots buy paid polls from week 4 and ground game from week 6, so in the tutorial the Easy bot does neither; the player's last-week tip points at ground game.

**Length** (the same harness, through `Net/Session` against the Easy bot; the player's choices after fixed think times; every reveal, clash, verdict and election night run to its end unless the profile skips):

| Player | Think times (Select, draft round, plan (week 1), stance, debate round) | Tutorial | Old 12-week tutorial, same pace |
|---|---|---|---|
| Brisk | 30, 12, 35 (50), 15, 8 s | **6:14** | 13:28 |
| Brisk, skipping the week's reveal after 6 s, the verdict after 3 s, election night after 20 s | same | **5:33** | — |
| Careful | 60, 20, 60 (90), 30, 15 s | **9:39** | — |

About 2 minutes of it is watching (the map reveal, three draft reveals, four week reveals, the debate's intro, clashes and verdict, 45 s of election night); the rest is the player's own pace.

**Coach tips in order (`CoachTip.TUTORIAL`).** In the tutorial the tips use their tutorial copy where it differs (`CoachTip.setTutorial`, set by the Router), still at most 12 words: Select "4 quick weeks to learn. Pick a candidate: one strength, one weakness."; week 1 "Week 1 of 4. Tap any state to see who's ahead."; week 2 "Week 2: take a side, or stay out. States that care react."; the debate "Debate night: 3 rounds. Each move shows how it does against theirs."; and a new tip only the tutorial has, first in its last week: `campaign.ground` "Last week! Ground game in close states adds votes on election night." The rest (issues, the draft, Lock in, the reveal, the lenses, Safe, election night) keep their copy. `CoachTip.has(id)` lets Campaign skip a tip with no copy (a normal match's last week shows no ground tip).

**The end.** After the tutorial, win or lose, Results shows a card under the score, **"You're ready!"** "That was the whole game in 4 weeks. A real match has 12 weeks, 2 debates and a timer on every choice." and the primary button reads **Play a real match**: it queues for a match against a person (the secondary is Back to lobby). How to play's hint: "A short 4-week match against the Easy bot, with tips and no timers."; the start notice: "Tutorial: 4 short weeks and no timer on any choice, so take your time."

**The normal match is unchanged.** `lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400` prints the same report as before this branch (only the timing lines differ), and `tests/sim/Tutorial.spec` pins a seeded 12-week bot match (its deck, every week's projected votes, the result) recorded before the change.

**Tests.** New `tests/sim/Tutorial.spec` (the schedule; only those keys change; one Choice event in week 2 and debate topics for week 3 only, over 60 seeds; the Choice event drawn from the seed; the normal schedule and a pinned normal match; whole bot-vs-bot tutorials run Select, map reveal, draft, weeks 1 to 4 with the stance in week 2 and the debate in week 3, then election night; determinism; the session's config). `tests/net/Tutorial.spec`: a slow player's whole tutorial has exactly 12 choices (Select, 3 draft rounds, 4 plans, 1 stance, 3 debate rounds); the first tutorial plays Easy. `tests/e2e/Tutorial.spec`: the first bot match says "bot Easy, tutorial". `KitStyle.spec`: the tutorial's tips are short too. New harness `tests/harness/tutorial.luau`. Snapshots `30-tutorial-select`, `30a-tutorial-plan`, `30b-tutorial-stance`, `30c-tutorial-debate`, `30d-tutorial-last-week`, `30e-tutorial-election`, `30f-tutorial-results`, `30g-tutorial-help` (new fixture moments `Tutorial*`): 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

**For the designers.**
1. Length: about 5½ to 6 minutes for a brisk new player, nearer 10 for a careful one. Levers if playtests run long: a pre-made draft (about −45 s, but the draft goes untaught), a shorter election night in the tutorial (45 → 25 s, −20 s), or 3 weeks (about −50 s, the stance and the debate in weeks 1 and 2).
2. "Play a real match" queues for a person. If new players often wait alone, it could start a Medium bot match instead.
3. The tutorial's Easy bot never buys ground game or polls (the bot's own schedule starts later); say if it should.

## Admin: timers off

### D-081 — Admins can turn the choice timers off in games where every human is an admin; such a match between people is unrated (rule change for admins only, Oct 24, 2026, branch `admin-timer`)

**Ask.** The owner: "give me an admin option to turn off the timer in my games so i can better take notes during play testing."

**Rule.** An admin (D-069: `game.CreatorId`, `Config.ADMIN_USER_IDS`, a group rank; always the server's check) has a "Timers off" toggle, saved in their profile (`Profile.adminTimersOff`, optional, no version bump). It turns the choice timers off in a match **only while every human in it is an admin and at least one of them has it on** (`Session.adminRule`; bots never count). So it works for the owner against the bot, the owner and the partner in a private match (or if they meet in the queue), and nowhere else: in a match with any other human the toggle is kept but does nothing, so a public opponent is never stalled. Each admin seat's State carries `adminTimers = { on, allowed }` (only theirs; nobody else learns who is an admin), every seat gets `adminNoTimer = true` while it applies.

**Machinery.** The tutorial's (D-075): an Input stage's deadline is `Session.NO_DEADLINE` inside the session and nil on the wire; the time bank is refused (`NoTimer`); a reconnect gets no window; disconnect grace and forfeits work as before; Show and Reveal stages keep their times and skips; the bot answers at once. The header reads "No timer" over "(admin)" (`Header.NO_TIMER_ADMIN`, two lines in the clock's 72 px place, so no layout moves).

**Mid-match.** New op `SetTimersOff { on }` (schema: a boolean, else `BadShape`). The server checks `Admins.isAdmin` on every request (`AdminOnly` otherwise), cools it down (`SET_TIMERS_OFF_COOLDOWN` 1 s: each one writes saved data), saves it, and in a match calls `Session.setTimersOff` (which checks the seat again, never throws, `Over` after the end). When the rule flips, every human is told (`AdminTimersOff`, `AdminTimersOffUnrated`, `AdminTimersOn`) and an open choice step follows at once, in the same step (an in-flight lock-in still counts): off drops its deadline; on gives it a fresh full timer from now (`stageStart` moves, so the bars start full; a bot level's multiplier included). A reveal running when it flips keeps its time; the next choice follows the rule. Inside the tutorial nothing changes (it is untimed anyway).

**Ratings.** Bot matches were already unrated. A match between two or more humans in which the timers were off at any point is unrated (`Result.unrated` and `Result.timersOff`), even if they came back on: one player could otherwise run the clock out on the other after a long, untimed think. Both are told as it happens and again at the end (`AdminUnrated`); an early forfeit keeps D-077's reason. An unrated result records nothing, as D-077's.

**UI.** The Lobby's corner gets a third 44 px toggle for admins, a clock struck through while timers are off, labels "Timers off (admin)" / "Timers on (admin)" (`Settings.setTimers`; the tooltip grows to 160 px). On a phone with an admin's taller column the eyebrow line sat under the corner (already true with two toggles), so the eyebrow now steps aside when it would sit under the corner, latched per screen size so the column does not jump. The "?" sheet gets, above its tabs and for admins only, the chip "Timers off (admin)" and one line: what it does here (and "That makes this match unrated." with two humans), or "Only in games where every player is an admin."

**Config.** New `SET_TIMERS_OFF_COOLDOWN` 1 (a server limit, not a rule). No gameplay number changed; bot-vs-bot balance is untouched (the sim has no timers).

**Tests.** `tests/net/AdminTimers.spec` (the rule for admin vs bot, toggle off, two admins, admin vs non-admin, a non-admin's flag ignored; mid-step off and on with an Easy bot's full timer; off during a reveal; no bank or reconnect window; turned down for a non-admin, a bot seat and after the end; the tutorial unchanged; unrated after off-and-on between admins, rated without, no flag against the bot over a whole untimed match, an early forfeit keeps D-077's reason; schema; profile), `tests/ui/AdminTimers.spec` (the corner toggle for admins only, saved, a non-admin's request turned down; a bot match with "No timer (admin)" for ten idle minutes, back on from the sheet with Medium's 57 s Select; a match with a non-admin keeps its timers and the sheet says why; two admins off mid-match from the sheet, then unrated at a forfeit in week 1). Shots `01i`, `03h`, `09v`, `24b`, `24c`: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

## The Daily Race

### D-082 — One seeded race a UTC day, the same candidate for everyone, one counted run (new mode, Oct 24, 2026, branch `daily-race`)

**Ask.** The user: "add the daily race, but add a stipulation that a different candidate is required to be used everyday and the candidate is the same for all players".

**The race is a function of the day.** `Sim/Daily.race(config, day)` (day = whole UTC days since 1970-01-01; the server passes `os.time() // 86400`) gives the match seed (so the leans, the issue map, the debate topics and the event deck), every player's candidate, the bot's candidate and its level, all through `Sim/Rng` (`daily-seed:<day>`, `daily-cycle:<cycle>`, `daily-bot:<day>`). Nothing else feeds it, so every server and every player gets the same race, and the bot plays the same moves against the same moves (its own stream comes from the seed, D-057).

**A different candidate every day.** Days are cut into cycles of one day per candidate (8 now). Each cycle is a seeded shuffle of the whole roster, so every cycle uses every candidate once; when a cycle would open with the candidate the previous one closed on, its first two days swap (the swap never touches a cycle's last day, so this needs only the previous cycle's raw shuffle, no recursion). So no candidate ever runs two days in a row. A ninth candidate would reshuffle the rotation from that day on; that is fine. Next days: Oct 24 Dana Okafor, 25 June Alvarado, 26 Helen Strand, 27 June Alvarado, 28 Dana Okafor, 29 Walt Begay.

**The bot.** Another candidate (seeded by the day) at `Config.DAILY_BOT_LEVEL` = Hard (the review's pick: a real test, not Extreme's wall), so the choice timers are Hard's ×1 (D-075). The Daily Race is never the tutorial, whatever the player's tutorial flag says, and it does not use or mark it: a brand-new player who plays the Daily first still gets the tutorial on their first Play vs Bot.

**The platform stays the player's choice.** Everyone gets the same map and the same issue map, so picking three issues from it is the same decision for everyone: it is skill, not luck, and fixing it would remove the one choice before the draft. The candidate is claimed for the player as Select opens (`SeatSpec.candidate` on a human seat, settled in the same batch as the bot's claim), so Select opens straight on step 2 (the issues); another candidate is turned down (`AlreadyClaimed`), and a timeout keeps the day's candidate with a seeded platform. Colors work as always.

**Scoring.** A counted run scores its final electoral-vote margin over the bot (−538 to +538). Ties: fewer weeks behind in the weekly polls (the public projected votes, `View.pollHistory`: a week where the bot's projection was above yours), then whoever finished first that UTC day. Simple to say, needs no extra state, and rewards leading the whole way over a late swing. One whole number holds all three (`Net/Daily.score`: `((margin+538)·100 + (99−behind))·100000 + (86399−seconds into the day)`), so the board is one sorted read. A counted run that is left or abandoned after week 1 scores `DAILY_FORFEIT_MARGIN` (−538, every vote) with the most weeks behind, below every finished run.

**One counted run a day.** The first Daily Race run that reaches **week 1** is the day's run (D-077's line: leaving in Select, the map reveal or the draft counts for nothing, so a disconnect at the start costs nothing, but the campaign itself cannot be scouted and replayed). From then on it is saved in the profile (`Profile.daily`: the day, the streak, and the margin once it ends); a later run that day is practice: the player is told as it starts ("Practice run: today's Daily Race already counts. This one won't."), and its Results say so. A run counts only if the player's profile is loaded and writable when it starts (else it is practice, as nothing could be saved). A run that ends after midnight belongs to the day it started. The Daily Race never touches the rating, the win/loss record or the practice record.

**Streak.** Consecutive UTC days with a counted run (`Net/Daily.begin`: +1 after yesterday, 1 after a gap; the best streak is kept too). A missed day just shows 0 again (`Net/Daily.streak`); no penalty, no reminder, nothing paid.

**Board.** OrderedDataStore `DailyRace_v1`, scope the day's `YYYY-MM-DD`, key `Player_<UserId>`, the score. Written once per player a day (the counted run's end); read as the global board is (D-059): the server caches the day's top 50 and reads `GetSortedAsync(false, 50)` at most once a minute, only when someone asks (the card asks when the Lobby opens and when the sheet opens; the server also asks right after a counted run ends). With the global board that is at most 2 sorted reads a minute per server against 5 + 2 per player. Every call is pcall'd; a failed read keeps the last list or says "Today's board is unavailable". Usernames, shared with the global board's cache. A rank is shown only from a board this server has read (no "#1" from a list of one). Wire: ops `DailyRace` (start) and `Daily` (ask), message kind `Daily` (`Net/Daily.Info`: the day, its candidates and level, when it ends as server time, whether today's run counts already, its margin, the streak, the board with `you`, and `run` right after a run ends).

**Screens.** Lobby: one card in the column, under How to play (`UI/DailyRace`): today's candidate's portrait, "Daily Race · Sat, Oct 24", "Everyone plays as Dana Okafor today", "5h 11m left · 3-day streak · Not played yet" (or "Won by 62 today"), **Play today's race** (**Practice today's race** once today counts) and **Today's board** (a sheet: how it ranks, "You: #7 · +184", the top 50 by margin). The card takes the room the phone's leaderboard rows had: a phone shows the global top 3 only from 870 px tall (`PHONE_BOARD_H` + `DAILY_CARD_H`), else "You" and See all as on a short phone (D-059). Select's bar reads "Daily Race: everyone is Dana today". Results: a Daily Race card under the reason line ("You won by 24 votes." / "Rank today: #7 · 3-day streak", or "Practice run: …" / "Only your first run counts. Today: won by 62."), no rating slot, and the primary is **Practice again**.

**Config.** New `DAILY_BOT_LEVEL` "Hard" and `DAILY_FORFEIT_MARGIN` −538. No rule, sim or balance number changed (the harness was not run: nothing it plays moved).

**Tests.** `tests/sim/Daily.spec` (dates and leap days, 400 days with no back-to-back repeat, 50 cycles each covering the roster, any 30 days cover all 8, the swap path over 1,000 cycles, a pure function of the day, the bot's other candidate at Hard, 200 distinct seeds, the same map for two players), `tests/net/Daily.spec` (score order and tie-breaks, encode/decode, clamps, the forfeit score, weeks behind, the streak's life, saving once per day, cleaning, the profile, the board, `Lobby.daily`, the schema, a Session with the fixed candidate: claimed at Select, Hard ×1, never the tutorial, other candidates refused, a timeout keeps it, the same bot platform for two players), `tests/e2e/Daily.spec` (the real server: the day's seed and candidate and Hard bot for a brand-new player, leaving in Select counts for nothing, a full run saved with its streak and written to the day's store, the second run is practice and changes nothing, the board request ranks the player, the tutorial still ahead; an abandoned run after week 1 scores −538; two players get the same race), `tests/ui/DailyRace.spec` (the card, the countdown ticking, played and practice, the sheet and You: #7, empty and unavailable, Play on the real server opening Select on the issues, Results counted and practice, strict audit 0 at phone, iPad and desktop). Shots `01-lobby`, `01i-lobby-daily-played`, `02l-lobby-daily-sheet`, `23h-results-daily`, `23i-results-daily-practice`: 0 audit findings at all three sizes. The fake engine's OrderedDataStore now keeps a scope as its own store and counts sorted reads per store (`sortedReadsBy`), which `tests/e2e/Leaderboard.spec` now reads; `tests/ui/LobbyExtras.spec` checks the phone's top 3 on a 430x932 phone and "You" only at 390x844.

## Matchmaking: across servers, invites, rematch

### D-083 — Matching across servers, bigger servers, a bot game while you wait, online count, rematch and invites (Oct 24, 2026, branch `matchmaking`)

**Ask.** The designers: "add a rematch button, the 'online now' count … add matching across servers, make bigger servers, add an option to play bot games while waiting, and add functioning invites."

**Matching across servers.** The queue and private codes used to be per server. Now:
- The local lobby still goes first and at once: two players on one server are paired there, with no teleport (as before).
- Every player still waiting has a **ticket** in a MemoryStore sorted map (`Queue_v1`, key UserId, sort key = when they joined the line), every hosted code one in a hash map (`Codes_v1`, key the code). The server they are on owns the ticket: it writes it, reads it back and refreshes it with one `UpdateAsync` every 3 s (`Matchmaking.POLL`), and removes it when they stop waiting; tickets expire after 90 s, so a crashed server's players vanish from the line (a ticket not refreshed for 45 s is never claimed).
- Pairing is four `UpdateAsync` steps on the ticket, each a pure transform in `Net/Matchmaking` that returns nil (no change) unless the ticket is in the right state, so two servers can never both win a step: **Claim** (the claimer first locks its own player's ticket, then claims the other's), **Answer** (the owner accepts, or declines if the player left or said no from a bot game), **Ready** (the claimer reserves a server with `TeleportService:ReserveServerAsync`, writes the match spec, and marks the ticket Ready with the access code), then both servers teleport their player (`TeleportAsync` with `TeleportOptions.ReservedServerAccessCode`). Only the **newer** player's server claims the oldest open ticket of an older player (same second: the smaller UserId is older), so two servers never claim each other's players at once. An unanswered claim lapses after `QUEUE_ACCEPT_SECONDS` + 10 s and the player keeps their place in line; a failed reservation or teleport puts both back with "Couldn't move you to the match. Try again."
- **Match servers** are those reserved servers (`game.PrivateServerId ~= ""` and `PrivateServerOwnerId == 0`, the engine reference's own test). The spec (match key, seed, both UserIds in seat order, Queue or Code, events) is written to the hash map `MatchServers_v1` under the server's `PrivateServerId`; the match server reads it there, **not from the teleport data**, because `Player:GetJoinData` says teleport data passes through the client. It holds each arriving player ("Waiting for your opponent to arrive…"), starts the match with the spec's seed once both are in and their saved profiles have loaded (or after 10 s: then unrated, like any match whose ratings are not known), and plays it like any other match. If the opponent never arrives within 60 s (`ARRIVAL_TIMEOUT`) the player goes back to a lobby server with "Your opponent couldn't reach the match…" and, from the public queue, straight back into it. After the match, **Back to lobby** sends them to a public server (`Return`); Rematch works there as anywhere.
- **Rated rules (D-077) hold everywhere:** the match server rates exactly as a lobby server does (both ratings loaded; leaving before week 1 is unrated; `RATED_PAIR_DAILY_MAX` per pair from the profiles).
- **Private codes are global:** a code is unique game-wide (a host whose code collides gets a new one); a code typed on any server is looked up there first, then in `Codes_v1` (one read; at most one lookup per player every 2 s). The host's server answers the claim at once, the joiner's server sets up the match server. A host who cancels meanwhile leaves the joiner with "Your friend isn't hosting that match anymore."
- **Studio and failures:** `TeleportService` does not work in Studio playtests and Studio's memory stores are a separate test scope, so a Studio server (and an unpublished place, PlaceId 0) never uses them: matching is in-server exactly as before. A memory-store error is retried 3 times with backoff and then simply leaves the player in the local line; nothing throws.

**Bigger servers.** `MATCHES_PER_SERVER` 10 → 50: a full 50-player lobby server can all play the bot at once. The server size itself is the place's **Max Players**, set by the humans (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Matchmaking"). Everything else already scales per player (the request rate limit, the DataStore save batching, MemoryStore's budget of 1,000 + 120 × players per minute).

**A bot game while you wait.** After `QUEUE_BOT_OFFER_SECONDS` (30) in the queue the waiting screen offers **"Play a bot while you wait"** (the level picked under Play vs Bot). It is a practice match, never the tutorial, that keeps the player's place in line; their ticket says they are busy. When an opponent is found (here or on another server) the bot game shows **"Opponent found! Leave this bot game?"** with **Join match** and **Keep playing**, for `QUEUE_ACCEPT_SECONDS` (15); the found opponent sees "They're finishing a bot game…" with the countdown. **Join match** ends the bot game as abandoned (unrated, nothing saved, the tutorial flag untouched; its Results are skipped) and starts the match. **Keep playing**, or no answer, keeps the bot game and takes the player out of the queue, and **the other player goes back to the front of the line**. Why this and not "re-queue the decliner at the front": the decliner chose their bot game, and putting them back would ask them again at the next opponent and make that opponent wait out the countdown again; the player who did nothing wrong keeps their place. A bot game that finishes normally leaves the player in line, and it counts in their practice record as any bot match.

**Online now.** Every server writes its player count to the sorted map `Online_v1` (key JobId, 90 s expiry) every 30 s; whichever server finds the shared total (`OnlineTotal_v1`) older than 30 s sums the map and writes it back; every server reads the total. The Lobby shows "● 1,234 online" (and "1,234 online · you'll start as soon as someone joins." while queued); never fewer than the players this server can see. In Studio it is this server's count; when the live read fails for over a minute it is hidden.

**Rematch.** Results' primary is now **Rematch** (it replaces "Play again"/"Play vs Bot again"; Find match is one tap away in the Lobby). Against the bot, one tap starts a new match at the same level (still in line from a bot game while waiting: another one). Against a person, both must tap within `REMATCH_WINDOW_SECONDS` (20): the bar reads "Waiting for Rex… · 14 s" / "Rex wants a rematch! · 9 s", then a new match with a new seed and the same seats. The button says when it can't: **"Opponent left"** (they left the server, started something else, or forfeited the match) and **"Rematch expired"**. No rematch after an abandoned match. Pure state machine: `Net/Rematch`.

**Invites.** "Invite a friend" sits at the top of the Play a friend sheet (it makes a code, then opens the prompt) and on the code screen. The client checks `SocialService:CanSendGameInviteAsync(player)` (pcall'd) and calls `PromptGameInvite(player, options)` with `ExperienceInviteOptions.LaunchData = "landslide:code=AB3CD"` (20 of the documented 200 characters) and a `PromptMessage`; when invites are unavailable it says "Invites aren't available here. Share your code instead." A friend who joins from the invite (any server) has the code in `Player:GetJoinData().LaunchData`, read on the server for up to 10 s as the guide recommends (it can arrive late), and is taken straight into the host's match, here or on the host's server. If the host left or the code expired: "That invite has expired: your friend isn't hosting anymore." If someone else is joining it: "Someone else is joining your friend's match right now."

**Checks.** New ops `WaitBot { difficulty? }`, `Answer { accept }`, `Rematch { matchId }`, `Return`, schema-checked (`Net/Schemas`), each rate-limited per player (1 s; Answer 0.5 s), with reason codes `Matching`, `NotQueued`, `BotTooSoon`, `NoOffer`, `NoRematch`, `RematchExpired`, `OpponentLeft`, `NotMatchServer`, `CodeBusy`, `Unavailable`. Nothing a client sends picks a seat, a seed or an opponent; teleport data carries only a whitelisted notice (`Matchmaking.readTravel`) and launch data only a well-formed code. Every engine call is pcall'd. LobbyStatus gains `online`, `botAt`, `found` (`Answer`, `Waiting`, `Joining`, `Arriving`, `Leaving`) and `matchServer`; message kind `Rematch` carries `Rematch.Status`; PlayerState gains `Found`.

**Budget.** A server with a waiting player makes about 20 ticket requests a minute per waiting player, plus a scan of the 10 oldest tickets (≤ 10 units) every 3 s while one of its players waits with nobody, plus 4 a minute for the online count and, for one server a period, one unit per running server. Well inside 1,000 + 120 × concurrent users a minute; each sorted map stays far below the ~30,000 units a minute per partition at hundreds of servers.

**Roblox APIs used (checked against the engine reference and guides):** `MemoryStoreService:GetSortedMap/GetHashMap`; `MemoryStoreSortedMap:GetAsync, SetAsync(key, value, expiration, sortKey), UpdateAsync(key, fn(value, sortKey), expiration), RemoveAsync, GetRangeAsync(Enum.SortDirection, count ≤ 200, lowerBound)`; `MemoryStoreHashMap:GetAsync, SetAsync, UpdateAsync, RemoveAsync` (UpdateAsync re-runs the transform on a conflict and a nil return cancels; keys ≤ 128 characters, values ≤ 32 KB, expiry ≤ 45 days); `TeleportService:ReserveServerAsync` (ReserveServer is deprecated), `TeleportAsync` (server only), `TeleportInitFailed`, `TeleportOptions.ReservedServerAccessCode`, `TeleportOptions:SetTeleportData`, `Enum.TeleportResult`; `DataModel.PrivateServerId`, `PrivateServerOwnerId`, `PlaceId`, `JobId`; `Player:GetJoinData()` (`TeleportData`, `LaunchData`); `SocialService:CanSendGameInviteAsync`, `PromptGameInvite`; `ExperienceInviteOptions.LaunchData` (≤ 200 characters), `PromptMessage`.

**Config.** `MATCHES_PER_SERVER` 10 → 50; new `QUEUE_BOT_OFFER_SECONDS` 30, `QUEUE_ACCEPT_SECONDS` 15, `REMATCH_WINDOW_SECONDS` 20. Request timing and budget (poll 3 s, ticket expiry 90 s, arrival timeout 60 s, …) live in `Net/Matchmaking`, like the leaderboard's in `Net/Leaderboard`. No sim rule changed; the harness is untouched.

**Only a published game can show:** real teleports and match servers, two servers finding each other, invites and their launch data, and the online count across servers (`docs/TESTING.md`, "Matchmaking"). The fake engine plays all of it with fake MemoryStore, Teleport and Social services.

**Tests.** `tests/net/Matchmaking.spec` (tickets: every step and who may take it, stale and foreign values, pick's older-player rule and ties; specs; launch and teleport data; the online sum; the rematch machine; the lobby's bot game while waiting, offers, decline, expiry, remote offers, holds, direct matches, capacity; the new schemas), `tests/e2e/Matchmaking.spec` (two and three worlds sharing memory stores and data stores: queue and code pairings across servers into a match server that starts from its spec and is rated, same-server pairing without a teleport, a missing code, a host who cancels while the joiner waits, a player asked in a bot game on another server, a failed teleport and a failed reservation, a no-show sent back and re-queued, Return; Studio and failing memory stores fall back to in-server; the online count across two servers; invites here and across servers, an expired one, the prompt and its unavailable path; the bot game while waiting: too soon, accept, Keep playing, no answer; rematch against a person, Opponent left, the window closing, and against the bot), `tests/ui/Matchmaking.spec` (every new state at three sizes with the strict audit). Shots `01i`, `02l`–`02n`, `09v`, `23h`, `23i` (`-mm-`): 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop. `tests/e2e/Bughunt.spec`'s "server full" fills the new 50 slots.

## 0.15.0: the new features together

### D-084 — How the tutorial, the Daily Race, admin timers and matchmaking meet; a phone Lobby that fits (integration, no Config changes, Oct 24, 2026, branch `landslide`)

**Ask.** The lead: merge D-080 to D-083 into one release, "resolving every conflict by understanding both sides and keeping every feature working", and redesign the phone Lobby, which the Daily Race card and matchmaking had crowded.

**Where they meet.**
- *The tutorial's end.* Results' primary after the tutorial stays **Play a real match** (D-080), not Rematch (D-083): replaying the tutorial is How to play's job. It sends `Queue`, which is now the queue across servers, so the bot game while waiting (after `QUEUE_BOT_OFFER_SECONDS`) is offered as for anyone. A bot game while waiting is never the tutorial (D-083), and the tutorial is marked done by then.
- *Bot games stay on the lobby server.* The tutorial, Play vs Bot, the Daily Race and the bot game while waiting are practice outcomes; only a pairing of two people on different servers reserves a match server. A match server's players only see Results, Rematch and Back to lobby, so a Daily Race or a tutorial never starts there.
- *Rematch after a Daily Race.* The finished match's `MatchInfo` remembers it was a Daily Race; its Rematch starts today's race again through `Lobby.daily` (today's seed, candidate and bot), and `DailyService.canCount` decides as for the card's Play: practice once today's run counted. Results' status line says "Rematch plays today's race as practice." (`Words/Daily.REMATCH_PRACTICE`; "Practice again" is gone). Starting a Daily Race gives up a pending rematch, like any other start.
- *Admin Timers off in match servers.* A match server builds its seats with the same `humanSpec` (the server's admin check and the saved toggle), so two admins paired across servers play without timers, unrated (an e2e spec covers it).
- *Admin Timers off and the Daily Race.* **Rule:** the admin rule never applies in a Daily Race, counted or practice (`Session.adminRule` returns not allowed when `session.daily`): everyone's runs on the day's board are played against the same clock. The "?" sheet's admin line says "Not in the Daily Race: every run has the same clock." The toggle stays saved.

**The phone Lobby.** At 390x844 the 0.15.0 column needed about 800 px: the Daily Race card (136 px) had pushed the leaderboard to "You" and See all, the eyebrow sat under the corner toggles (an admin's three hid it entirely, and the toggles' tip covered the wordmark), and matchmaking added the online line. On any screen too narrow for the leaderboard beside the column (`SIDE_MIN_W`), now:
- the column starts `CORNER_CLEAR` (52 px) down, below the corner toggles, so the eyebrow and the wordmark are never under them or their tip, and Roblox's own top-left buttons stay above the safe area as before;
- the Daily Race card is one row (`DailyRace.setCompact`, 80 px): the portrait (40 px), "Daily Race", "As Dana Okafor", "5h 11m · 3-day streak", then **Play** (**Practice** once today counts) and **Top 50** (today's board); same instance names, so the specs and taps did not change;
- the record and "● 1,234 online" share one line ("Stats");
- How to play shares a row with the Color button once the Color Pass is set up ("Extras");
- the spacer under the wordmark goes;
- the leaderboard shows the top 3 from `PHONE_BOARD_H` 830 px of screen (was 740 + `DAILY_CARD_H` 130, which no phone reached); an admin's Review names button needs `REVIEW_ROOM` (52 px) more, so an admin's 390x844 Lobby keeps "You" and See all.
Considered and not done: tabs between the Daily card and the board (one of them hidden, and the card is a main action), and both side by side (190 px leaves the board's names about 70 px). iPad and desktop are unchanged: the full Daily card and the top 10 beside the column.

**Display constants, not gameplay:** Lobby `PHONE_BOARD_H` 740 → 830, `DAILY_CARD_H` removed, new `CORNER_CLEAR`, `REVIEW_ROOM`; DailyRace `COMPACT_FACE` 40, `COMPACT_PLAY_W` 84, `COMPACT_BOARD_W` 68. No Config value changed; the balance harness is unchanged apart from its timing lines.

**Tests.** `tests/net/AdminTimers.spec` (a Daily Race keeps its timers for an admin with Timers off), `tests/e2e/Daily.spec` (the second run of the day starts from Rematch: the day's seed, practice), `tests/e2e/Matchmaking.spec` (the tutorial's Play a real match queues across servers and offers the bot game; a Daily Race stays on the lobby server and timed for an admin; admin timers off in a match server, unrated notice), `tests/ui/DailyRace.spec` (the one-row card on a phone, the full card on an iPad, Rematch's practice line), `tests/ui/LobbyExtras.spec` (top 3 on a 390x844 phone beside the one-row card; the full card on an iPad).

### D-085 — The plan's time bank can also be spent on a debate round; the debate screen says what a Zinger and a loss cost (timer rule change approved by the user, no Config changes, Oct 24, 2026, branch `pt-debate`)

**Ask.** The new-player playtest review (`playtest-changes.md`, top 10 items M1, S1, S5): four of six players lost their only Zinger on a split or a loss without knowing it could happen; the debate "Why?" showed only what a win gains; 15 s a round with the rules on screen felt tight. The user approved all three, S5 as option (a): "let the plan time bank be spent on debate rounds", no new number.

**The time bank in debate rounds (S5).** `Session.bank` now accepts a chunk in an Input stage of phase **Plan or Debate** (it was Plan only). Same pool: `TIME_BANK` 30 s per seat per match, taken in `TIME_BANK_CHUNK` 15 s taps; a chunk spent on a plan is gone for the debate and the other way round. As on the plan it moves **only the asking seat's own deadline** (`bankUsed`, an absolute `GetServerTimeNow` value via `seatDeadline`), and only for that one round (it resets when the next round's Input opens). The other seat's deadline does not move: if it has not locked in by its own deadline it cannot answer after it (`StaleStep`) and gets the canned answer as before; the bank only keeps the round open for the banking player. Refused as before: in a clash or the verdict (`NotInput`), once locked in (`NotInput`), past your own deadline or with less than a chunk left (`NoBank`), and in a round without a deadline, the tutorial and admin Timers off (`NoTimer`, D-075, D-081). A stance (Decision) still has no bank. The debate screen shows **+15 s** (`BankButton`, the plan's ActionBar secondary) while a round is open, the bank holds a chunk and the round has a deadline. Payloads, schema and remotes are unchanged (`Bank` already existed).

**Match length.** Unchanged. `Config.spec` already counts the bank once per seat (`TIME_BANK × 2` = 60 s) inside D-074's 1,122 s worst case, wherever it is spent; a banked debate round holds the other player at most the same 15 s a banked plan does. D-075's bot figures (one 30 s bank, not scaled by level: Hard/Extreme 18:12, Medium 24:46, Easy 31:20) and the tutorial and admin rules (no deadline, so no bank) hold as written. Typical play is unchanged too: the bank is opt-in.

**The Zinger is used either way (M1, copy only).** Checked in `Sim/Debate.round`: a staked Zinger is taken off (`live.zingers[p] -= 1`) before the clash, whatever the result; only the winner's points and crowd are multiplied (`mult` is 1 on a split, and a loser gets nothing for it). The copy now says so: the toggle reads "Stake Zinger: win ×2 · used either way" / "Zinger staked: win ×2, used either way"; with a Safe move that can still split (a "=" cell) and the Zinger staked, the bar says "A split still uses the Zinger" (warn tone; shortened from the review's two sentences so it stays one line beside the opponent's status on a phone); the `debate.safe` coach tip says "Safe can't lose this round, but a split still uses your Zinger."; Help's debates line: "A win counts ×2; a split or a loss uses it up for nothing." No rule changed.

**"Why?" shows the downside (S1).** Each move gets a third line under its parts and its win line, from `Swing.debateBoard` (new `MoveRow.counteredBy`, `canBackfire`, `lossFlips`, `lossLine`; `DebateBoard.theirZingerMult`): "Lose to their Pivot: -0.5 in WI, MI…; could lose TN (-11 EV)". It is the sim's own rule on public facts: the move's counter is the one `Debate.BEATS` says beats it; it can backfire when that result cell is below 0 (`Debate.clash`: lose and countered); the cost is `DEBATE_BACKFIRE` in the move's landing states (`Debate.landing`), and the flips are `Polls.projectFlips` on today's public polls with that backfire plus the rival's plain win (`DEBATE_ROUND_WIN`, no Zinger) in the landing states of their countering move, since that is what losing to it does (`Debate.moves`). Flips are grouped "lose X (-EV)", "tie X", "hand X to them". A move their counter cannot beat says "Their Attack can't beat it: no backfire." While the rival's Zinger is unspent, a last line: "Rex still has a Zinger: a round they win, even by 1, can count ×2." (their first name and their own multiplier, ×4 for Hale). Only the player's own View is read: the public board, public polls and the public Zinger count; never true support or the rival's choice.

**Tests.** `tests/net/Session.spec` (a chunk in a debate round moves only that seat's deadline, the other seat cannot answer after its own, the next round starts plain, no bank in a clash, the pool shared with the plans and `NoBank` when empty, a locked or late seat refused), `tests/net/Tutorial.spec` (no bank in the tutorial's debate either), `tests/ui/Swing.spec` (the loss line follows `BEATS`, the cells and the backfire rule; a narrow lead in a landing state shows "could lose"), `tests/ui/Debate.spec` (the toggle's copy, the bar's warning only for a Safe move with a "=" cell and the Zinger staked, the loss lines and the rival's Zinger line in Why?, +15 s shown and sent in a round, hidden with an empty bank, without a deadline, after Lock in and outside rounds). Shots `08c-debate-safe-zinger` and `08d-debate-why` (new) and every `08` shot: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

### D-086 — Teach idle money, rename the plan's Pivot, caption battleground rounds (playtest review items M3, S2, S4; Oct 24, 2026, branch `pt-plan`)

**Why.** The new-player playtest review (top-10 list, approved): five of six players banked $30-80 because nothing said ads and ground game cost money and no action; three were confused by the plan's "Pivot" next to the debate's Pivot move; three did not know whether nominating a battleground helped them.

**What.**
- *Idle-money tip* (`campaign.cash`, UI/CoachTip, Campaign): from week `IDLE_CASH_TIP_WEEK` (3), when cash ≥ `IDLE_CASH_TIP` (20) and the plan spends no money (`PlanDraft.idleCash`: `Plan.cost(...).cash` is 0, so no ad, attack ad, ground game, paid poll or flight), the side column shows "You have $X spare. Ads and ground game cost money, not actions." ($X as `Numbers.money` writes it). It sits under the state card (`tipAfter`), after the first-match map tip and before the lens tip. It shows once per session like every other tip: dismissing it, or it going away for any reason (money spent, locked in, the week ends), marks it seen. It is in the normal copy (`TIPS`), so like `campaign.lens` and `campaign.actions` it can show in the tutorial too (its week 3 rarely holds $20; its last week leads with `campaign.ground`).
- *"no action" on the tiles:* Ad, Attack ad and Ground game add " · no action" to their price, read from the candidate's `mediaCost` / `gotvCost` (`Words.NO_ACTION`; a spec checks against `Plan.cost` that exactly these cost money and 0 AP). Attack ad is included because it is an ad and is priced the same. That price line is its own one-line label that shrinks rather than wraps (`<name>Cost`), and the action buttons grow 64 → 72 px (`StateCard` `ACTION_H`, a display constant) so ground game's two-line effect and its price fit on a phone and an iPad. Help's Actions text adds "None of those use an action." (true of ads, polls and ground game).
- *Swap issue:* client copy only; the plan action stays `Pivot` on the wire and in Sim. Button "Swap issue", effect "−`PIVOT_PENALTY` support everywhere", chooser "Swap an issue (once per match, −`PIVOT_PENALTY` support everywhere)" + "Uses `PIVOT_AP` action. Drop which issue?", plan line "Swap issue · X for Y", reveal "Swapped X for Y" (the drop comes from the public week card), News "swapped X for Y", Details/Help "Swap issue", reason texts. Once per match is `pivotUsed` in `Plan.validate`; the penalty is `Support.national` in every state (weighted by undecided). The debate move, the DebugUI command `pivot` and its `pivot X->Y` card line keep their names.
- *Battleground caption:* draft rounds 2-3 show "Battleground: every rally and ad here counts ×`BATTLEGROUND_MULT`, for both of you. Pick where you can win." under the round's pill. In `Resolve` the multiplier applies to rallies, ads and attack ads only (not fundraisers or ground game), so the line names rallies and ads.

**Config.** New `IDLE_CASH_TIP` 20 and `IDLE_CASH_TIP_WEEK` 3: UI thresholds, read by nothing in Sim. No rule changed; the balance harness is unaffected.

**Tests.** `tests/ui/PlanDraft.spec` (idleCash shows and does not), `tests/ui/Campaign.spec` (the tip appears once and not below the threshold or before week 3; the tiles' "no action"; Swap issue in the chooser and tray), `tests/ui/CampaignWords.spec` (NO_ACTION against Plan.cost, swap words from Config), `tests/ui/RevealWords.spec` (the swap line, the Help line), `tests/ui/SetupWords.spec` and `tests/ui/Draft.spec` (the caption in rounds 2-3 only).

### D-087 — The bot ladder shifts down a step: a new Easy and Medium that misjudge stubbornly; the old Hard and Extreme retire (user-approved "shift the ladder", Oct 25, 2026, branch `bot-ladder`)

**Ask.** The designer loses to Easy more than half the time. The novice study (`tests/harness/novice.luau`, scripted Weak, Typical and Attentive first-time players; the Attentive one reproduces the designer's result) found D-057's Easy beats a typical new player 92% of the time, because its plans are nearly the standard bot's: it puts every action where EV × closeness is highest, the one skill new players lack. The user approved "shift the ladder": every level plays fair (no handicap, no extra or less cash, AP or information, the rubber band unchanged); levels differ only in judgment.

**Rule.** The four levels a player picks now map to play profiles (`Bot.styleFor`):

| Level | Profile | How it plays |
|---|---|---|
| Easy | Rookie | every state's worth × a log-normal factor of spread `BOT_ROOKIE_NOISE` 4, **drawn once a match** (`BOT_ROOKIE_ONCE`), so it stays wrong the same way every week; also drafts on that read; every stance and debate move random (`BOT_ROOKIE_SLIP` 1). The study's option A. |
| Medium | Casual | the same at spread `BOT_CASUAL_NOISE` 1.5, once a match, always slips. The study's "soft setting". |
| Hard | Hazy | D-057's Easy, unchanged: spread `BOT_HAZY_NOISE` 0.45 redrawn every plan, half its decisions random. |
| Extreme | Balanced | the standard bot, unchanged (D-057's Medium). |

D-057's Hard and Extreme (renamed profiles Foresight and Strategist, Config keys `BOT_FORESIGHT_*`, `BOT_STRATEGIST_*`) are no player's level now. Their code stays: the Daily Race plays Foresight (below) and the harnesses can name them.

*Once a match, without state:* the bot keeps nothing between turns, so the once-a-match factor is drawn from a stream seeded only by what its View shows that never changes after the draft: its seat, both candidates and home states, every state's lean and top issues (`Bot.misjudgedWeights`). The match seed set all of them, so it is the same every week of a match, differs between matches and seats, and no hidden number can reach it (the fair-play spec covers every profile).
