It does not consume the plan's random stream, so the plan does not depend on it at all.

**The default is Easy.** Absent or unknown `difficulty` on Practice or WaitBot is Easy (was Medium), and so is the Lobby's chip until the player picks; the Practice field values are unchanged ("Easy" | "Medium" | "Hard" | "Extreme"), so older clients keep working. Reason: the people who play the bot without picking are new players, and the tutorial they just finished is Easy.

**Where a level is used.**
- *Choice timers* (D-075) follow the level the player picked: Easy ×2, Medium ×1.5, Hard and Extreme ×1. Unchanged numbers; Easy and Medium are now also the gentle bots.
- *The tutorial* (D-075, D-080) is still always Easy, now the Rookie: `lune run tests/harness/tutorial.luau 400` gives the sharp stand-in 98.0% (was in D-080's 55-80% band) and the careless one (Hazy) 97.0% (was 50.8%); 68.5% / 79.8% of those are still within 5 points of the tipping state on election night (was about 99%). A first-timer should win the tutorial, so D-080's "neither steamrolls nor gets crushed" target is retired for the bot's side; the schedule and length are unchanged (brisk 6:14).
- *A bot game while you wait* (D-083) plays the level picked under Play vs Bot, Easy unless changed (the waiting player chose that level; the request's field is unchanged).
- *The Daily Race* (D-082) keeps its strength: it plays Foresight (`DAILY_BOT_STYLE`, new), D-082's Hard bot, so every day's board, before and after this change, means the same; `DAILY_BOT_LEVEL` "Hard" → "Extreme", the level it counts as (timers ×1, as before). `lune run tests/harness/difficulty.luau Hard Medium 1000` on the commit before (68.9%) and `Foresight Extreme 1000` after (68.9%) match exactly. Today's board copy says "over the bot" (was "over the Hard bot"). Session's seat spec takes `profile` for this (it keeps the level, unlike the harness's `style`).
- *Saved data:* nothing is kept by level: the practice record is one tally and the leaderboard counts only rated matches, so nothing changes meaning or needs a reset.
- *The Lobby* says what the picked level is like on one line under the chips (`Words/Lobby.LEVEL_NOTES`): Easy "Learning the ropes: it often misreads the map.", Medium "Know the basics? It still makes mistakes.", Hard "A real test: it rarely misreads the map.", Extreme "Full strength: the bot at its best." On a phone shorter than `PHONE_BOARD_H` + `NOTE_ROOM` (852 px) the eyebrow above the wordmark gives way to it, so the 390x844 phone keeps the leaderboard's top 3 (D-084). Seat payloads still carry the level; no screen shows it.

**Measured** (`lune run tests/harness/novice.luau ladder 1000`; 1,000 matches a row, seeds 500001 on, seats alternating, two different candidates a match, every player from its own View, 0 invalid inputs, no ties; ± is the 95% band; "median margin" is over all games, "median win" over the reported side's wins, in EV):

| Pairing (reported side first) | Win rate | Median margin | Reported side's median win (share by 40+) | Its median loss |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weak novice vs Easy | 38.0% ± 3.0 | 60 | 48 (58%) | 68 |
| Weak novice vs Medium | 13.3% ± 2.1 | 90 | 28 (38%) | 102 |
| Weak novice vs Hard | 1.4% ± 0.7 | 182 | 12 (14%) | 184 |
| Weak novice vs Extreme | 0.3% ± 0.6 | 238 | 24 | 238 |
| **Typical novice vs Easy** | **65.5% ± 2.9** | 50 | 56 (66%) | 38 |
| **Typical novice vs Medium** | **40.7% ± 3.0** | 46 | 40 (51%) | 50 |
| Typical novice vs Hard | 7.6% ± 1.6 | 101 | 24 (30%) | 106 |
| Typical novice vs Extreme | 3.4% ± 1.1 | 146 | 18 (21%) | 148 |
| **Attentive novice vs Easy** | **96.4% ± 1.2** | 113 | 116 (91%) | 24 |
| **Attentive novice vs Medium** | **85.1% ± 2.2** | 69 | 76 (76%) | 26 |
| Attentive novice vs Hard | 41.4% ± 3.1 | 46 | 42 (54%) | 48 |
| Attentive novice vs Extreme | 15.5% ± 2.2 | 74 | 22 (35%) | 84 |
| Easy vs Medium | 21.5% ± 2.5 | 76 | 38 (49%) | 88 |
| Easy vs Hard | 0.7% ± 0.6 | 158 | 44 | 158 |
| Easy vs Extreme | 0.5% ± 0.6 | 186 | 12 | 188 |
| Medium vs Hard | 9.1% ± 1.8 | 98 | 22 (30%) | 106 |
| Medium vs Extreme | 2.4% ± 0.9 | 136 | 22 (25%) | 138 |
| Hard vs Extreme | 16.2% ± 2.3 | 70 | 30 (36%) | 80 |
| Extreme vs Foresight (the Daily Race's bot) | 29.9% ± 2.8 | 22 | 24 (29%) | 22 |

Against the targets: Easy typical 65.5% (target ~65), attentive 96.4% (~96); Medium 40.7% (~36: the study's prototype replaced the whole plan stream, this draws only the weights once, a few points softer) and 85.1% (~83); Hard and Extreme reproduce the study's Easy and Medium rows exactly (7.6 / 41.4 and 3.4 / 15.5), as they are the same bots. The steps between levels are steep against a person (typical 65 → 41 → 8 → 3), and Medium to Hard is the biggest. `tests/harness/difficulty.luau 1000` (seeds 300001 on): Easy vs Medium 21.8%, Medium vs Hard 9.1%, Hard vs Extreme 18.0%, Foresight vs Extreme 68.9%. A plan takes 0.41 ms on average at Easy (0.26 at Extreme), 5.4 ms worst seen.

**Balance.** `lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 400` prints the same report as before, line for line, apart from its timing lines: the standard bot did not change.

**Tests.** `tests/sim/BotDifficulty.spec` (the Easy default; the level → profile map; each profile's misjudgment from Config; valid and deterministic matches for all six profiles from either seat; the misjudging levels still raise and spend; Easy and Medium misjudge the same in week 2 and week 10 and differently in another match or seat, their plan independent of the plan stream, Hazy redraws; fair play for all six profiles: every decision identical when true support, undecided, ground game, pools, poll errors and the opponent's polls change, and when the opponent's cash moves inside its bracket), `tests/net/Session.spec` (a `profile` keeps the level, its timers and seat label), `tests/net/Lobby.spec`, `tests/net/Matchmaking.spec`, `tests/e2e/Game.spec` (Easy by default), `tests/sim/Daily.spec`, `tests/net/Daily.spec`, `tests/e2e/Daily.spec` (the Daily bot is Extreme playing Foresight), `tests/ui/LobbyExtras.spec` (Easy picked first, the line follows the pick, every line short), `tests/sim/Tutorial.spec` (the pinned D-080 match now names the Hazy profile). Harnesses: `novice.luau ladder` (new; the study modes now run against Hard, the study's Easy, with `BOT_HAZY_*`), `difficulty.luau` (the new table; names a level or a profile), `tutorial.luau` (against the new Easy). Lobby shots at phone, iPad and desktop: 0 audit findings.
### D-088 — Key states: the campaign map highlights the states that decide 270 (learning aid approved by the user; UI only, no rule change, Oct 25, 2026, branch `key-states`)

**Why.** The Easy-bot study (`tests/harness/novice.luau`, Oct 25) found that new players lose mainly because their weekly plan aims at the wrong states: the typical scripted novice beats Easy 7.6% of the time, and giving it the attentive player's targeting alone lifts it to 34%. Easy's one real edge is that every rally and ad goes to the big states that decide 270. Today only election night shows the deciding state. The user approved the aid they had been holding: highlight the deciding states during the campaign. Direction: learnable cause and effect, on the map, no hidden information.

**The rule (one sentence).** Line the states up by your public-poll lead; the tipping state is the one that carries the leader past 269; the key states are it and the biggest states polling within `KEY_STATE_BAND` (10) points of it, `KEY_STATES` (3) at most. (`Shared/UI/KeyStates`, pure, tested in Lune.)
- *Inputs:* the player's own View only: each state's public poll (its public lean before the first poll) and its electoral votes. Never true support, internal polls, ground game, money or anyone's plan.
- *Same for both seats:* the opponent walks the same public polls from the other end and stops at the same state. Two details make that exact: states that tie on the tipping margin are one step and its biggest state is the tipping state; when the states safer than a step hold exactly 269 (a 269-269 line) the step nearer 0 in the polls tips it (then the bigger state, then the code). A spec checks 200 random poll sets from both seats: identical every time.
- *Every mode,* the tutorial, Practice, the Daily Race and rated matches: it is public information arranged, so it gives nobody anything the other side lacks. Nothing in `Sim/` or `Bot` reads it (a spec checks), so the bots and the balance harness are unchanged.

**How the numbers were picked** (`lune run tests/harness/novice.luau KeyStates Easy 500 KEY_STATES=n KEY_STATE_BAND=b`, the typical novice aiming its rallies, ads and ground game at the key states, 500 matches each, ±4.3): the band is the lever, the count is not. Band 3: 8%; 5: 20%; 8: 38%; **10: 39%**; 12: 42%; 15: 38%; 20: 27%; 40 (the biggest states, close or not): 7%. Count 3 to 6 at band 8-12: within the noise of each other (35-43%); 2 states: 22%. Weighting the band by closeness instead of size, or dropping the forced tipping state, measured the same or worse. 3 states keep the map quiet (D-079's "fewer labels") and fit a phone line; 10 points is a round number for the sentence.

**What the player sees** (while planning only; never in a reveal, a stance or a debate):
- *Map:* each key state glows softly (the accent bloom, softer than a moved state's) and wears a dashed accent outline, 3 px. Dashed so it never reads as the selection (solid, D-056); an owner's ring (home, stronghold) fills the dashes' gaps in its color, so both still read. A Northeast chip gets a glowing accent plate around it instead (UIStroke cannot dash). Labels, plates (D-079), ★, pins, plan badges and preview tags are untouched. The circles fallback (`NodeMap`) draws an accent ring and the glow. `MapInterface.Mark.key` (new, optional: backward compatible).
- *Side column, first line:* a small dashed swatch, "Key states", and one chip per key state, biggest first ("TX 40 · FL 30 · NY 28"); a tap selects that state as a tap on the map does. On iPad and desktop it sits level with the lens chips; on a phone it is the first thing under the map and steps aside while a state's card is open, so the card's actions stay in view.
- *Legend row:* "▭ key state" (the same dashed swatch) leads the map's legend in every lens; on a phone it drops with the lens items when the plan line needs the room.
- *Coach tip* (`campaign.keys`, once, after the map tip): "Key states decide 270. Aim your plan there."; the tutorial's: "Glowing states decide 270. Put your rallies and ads there." Tapping a key chip marks it seen.
- *Map key:* "Glow and dashed outline: a key state, where 270 is decided this week." *Help → Your turn* (where Help opens while you plan) ends "Glowing key states decide 270: aim there."; *All the numbers → Election night* states the rule with its two numbers.

**Measured: a player who uses it** (`lune run tests/harness/novice.luau keys 1000`; seeds 500001-501000, seats alternate, same pairings as the study):

| Player | vs Easy | vs Medium | Median margin vs Easy | Unspent $ |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Typical novice (the study's) | 7.6% ± 1.6 | 3.4% ± 1.1 | 101 | 32.9 |
| **Typical, aiming at the key states** | **41.1% ± 3.0** | **14.2% ± 2.2** | 36 | 25.7 |
| Typical, half its effort by the key states | 23.2% ± 2.6 | 9.3% ± 1.8 | 50 | 27.6 |

So the aid alone takes a typical new player from one win in thirteen against Easy to about two in five, the attentive novice's level (41.4%), and its losses shrink from 106 EV to 38. It does not touch the other leaks (idle money, stances, debates), and Easy is still not easy for a player who ignores it: this complements the Easy-bot options in the study, it does not replace them.

**Config.** New `KEY_STATES` 3 and `KEY_STATE_BAND` 10: a UI aid, read only by `Shared/UI/KeyStates`. No rule changed: `balance.luau 400` is identical to before (every line). Display constants: `ShapeMap.KEY_BLOOM` 0.5, `KEY_PLATE` 0.6; `NodeMap.KEY_GLOW_FADE` 0.55; `Legend` swatch 16x10, 2 px dashes.

**Tests.** `tests/ui/KeyStates.spec` (the tipping state; the band, the count and biggest-first; a lopsided race keys its tipping state's neighbours, not a far 1-point state; both seats agree on 200 random poll sets; private fields change nothing; the lean before the first poll; nothing in Sim reads it; on the Campaign screen at 16:9 and phone: the line, its chips and the map's Key attributes match `KeyStates.of`, the legend item, audit clean; a chip selects its state and marks the tip seen; the phone hides the line under an open card; the tip after the map tip, its tutorial copy, both ≤ 12 words; none in a reveal; the Map key, Help and All the numbers). `Campaign.spec`, `CampaignWords.spec` (9 key lines now), `Lens.spec` updated. Novice harness: variants `KeyStates` and `KeyHalf` (`tests/lib/Novice`, param `keyFollow`), mode `keys`; the Typical rows reproduce the study exactly (the variant draws no extra random numbers). Snapshots: new `09k-campaign-key-tip`, `09l-campaign-key-tapped`, `30h-tutorial-key-tip`; every shot 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

## Names-only reskin

### D-089 — Landscape phones: the orientation lock, the phone viewport and the size classes (user request, UI only, no rule change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `landscape`)

**Why.** The user: "Make the phone app landscape mode." Phones now play in landscape only; the partner's iPad keeps its layout. This is the foundation other UI work builds on, so the rule is one shared function, not per-screen guesses.

**The lock.** `StarterGui.ScreenOrientation = LandscapeSensor` in `default.project.json` (it applies from join: the docs say it sets each joining mobile player's `PlayerGui.ScreenOrientation`), and `App.client` sets the player's own `PlayerGui.ScreenOrientation` to the same at startup (inside `pcall`), so a place file built without the project setting behaves the same. `LandscapeSensor`, not `LandscapeRight`: the game turns with the phone, whichever way it is held (cable left or right), where `LandscapeRight` would show it upside down to half the players. Desktop ignores the property; a tablet is held landscape too, which is how the partner already plays. Checked against the creator docs (`enums/ScreenOrientation`, `classes/StarterGui`, `classes/PlayerGui`).

**The phone viewport.** The `phone` snapshot size is **844 x 390** (an iPhone 12 to 16 in landscape, in points). Roblox's top bar takes 58 px off the top of the `Game` ScreenGui (`CoreUISafeInsets`, which the snapshot renderer already models), and the screen container pads 10 px, so a screen gets about **824 x 312**: height is the constraint. A notched iPhone's side cutouts (about 47 pt each side) also come off the width under `CoreUISafeInsets`; the snapshots do not model them, but every landscape layout sizes its columns from the space it is given, so 750 px wide loses width, not content (checked by hand with the snapshots at 750 x 390: every shot clean but one, the debate verdict, where a Northeast chip's tag pokes 3 px past the small map's edge). An iPhone SE (667 x 375) is the same class.

**The size classes** (`Kit.sizeClass(size)`, `Kit.compact(size)`, of the game area): **portrait** when taller than wide (a portrait tablet; the old phone layouts stay as they were), **compact** when wider than tall and under `Kit.COMPACT_H` (500 px) high, **wide** otherwise. An iPad at 1024 x 768 (710 px after the bar) and a 1280 x 720 window (662) are wide and unchanged. `ctx.compact()` (optional on `Types.Ctx`) asks it of the board. 500 px: between the tallest landscape phone (about 430 x 932, 372 px of game area) and the shortest wide screen we design for (662).

**The compact pattern: map left, a compact side column right, every column the full height under the header.**
- *Shared pieces.* `Sheet`: every sheet takes the whole height on a compact screen (a 0.5 share was 156 px). `Layout.page{ side = true }`: the header and footer lanes stand in a right column (`Layout.SIDE_SHARE` 0.4) and the scroll area takes the full height on the left (Results). `LensToggle.setColumns(toggle, n)`: the lens chips two to a row.
- *Lobby:* the button column on the left (no eyebrow, the one-row Daily card, How to play beside Color and an admin's Review names), a "Brand" pane on the right under the corner toggles: the wordmark, the record and the leaderboard card ("You" and See all; the top 3 from 440 px tall).
- *Campaign (and its reveals, the tutorial):* the map column is a chip rail (Key and Close, then the four lenses 2 x 2) beside the map, as wide as the map is at the full body height (at most 0.6 of the width); the event strip moves to the top of the side column; while a state's card is open the strip and the key states line step aside (as on a portrait phone), and the card shows the phone's 3 main actions.
- *Draft and the map reveal:* the map the full height on the left, as wide as it is at that height (never narrower than its chips); the legend and the board go in the panel as on a phone.
- *Debate:* three columns: the map, the stage (its own scroll; the crowd meter on its own row, `Stage:setNarrow`), and the moves or the clash with the action bar.
- *Decision:* the map column as wide as the map at that height (at most 0.42), the stances the rest.
- *Results:* summary, map and graph scroll on the left; the tabs top right, the action bar bottom right.
- Select, Election night, Help, How to play, Leave and the other sheets fit as they were (0 findings).

**Config.** None (no gameplay number). Display constants: `Kit.COMPACT_H` 500; `Layout.SIDE_SHARE` 0.4; Campaign `COMPACT_MAP_SHARE` 0.6, `RAIL_W`; Draft `COMPACT_MAP_SHARE` 0.6; Debate `COMPACT_MAP_SHARE` 0.28, `COMPACT_STAGE_SHARE` 0.33; Decision `COMPACT_MAP_SHARE` 0.42; Lobby `COLUMN_SHARE` 0.56, `COMPACT_BOARD_H` 440; Sheet `COMPACT_HEIGHT` 1; Stage `CROWD_H` 32.

**Tests.** `Rig.PHONE` and every spec's phone size are 844 x 390 (specs that test a portrait screen name 390 x 844 explicitly); `LobbyExtras.spec` (the brand pane), `Election.spec` (full names on a landscape phone, first names in portrait), `Lens.spec` (the rail may stand left of the map), `e2e/Game.spec` (a whole match at 844 x 390). Snapshots: every shot 0 audit findings at phone (844 x 390), iPad and desktop.

**Still rough (for the polish pass).** Campaign's map is small (about 250 x 230) and a state's actions are a scroll below its card's title; the Decision's stances B and C are a scroll away; the Debate's third move button is half a scroll down; the Lobby's wordmark is large for its pane.

### D-090 — Start screen, queue, How to play, end-screen social and bug reports (designer's list items 1-11 and 4.2, approved by the user; UI and server, no rule change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-a`)

**Why.** The designer's 0.17 list: the start screen had little energy (a grey Play slab), the queue was static and the logo jumped when it opened, "Opponent found!" buried Join under an unclear ×, the tutorial ended on an orange "You lost", the Daily Race card read like a list row, How to play said "1 of 6", and there was no way to report a bug. Every change is designed for the landscape phone (D-089, 844 x 390), the iPad and desktop.

**Start screen.** A faint red and blue map of the states with stars sits behind the wordmark (`UI/BrandMap`: the real map's shapes from the same atlases as `Map/ShapeMap`, transparency 0.86, red or blue by a fixed scatter of the state ids that follows no real election; the stars twinkle unless motion is reduced; `Decor`, so the audit skips it; on a landscape phone it stays inside the brand pane). Play vs Bot gets a blue play badge and `Motion.shine` (every 4.5 s; nothing under reduced motion). The bot levels carry a colored dot each (Easy green, Medium gold, Hard red, Extreme violet: player colors, decorative, the word still says the level). How to play leaves the column and becomes a "?" chip in the corner beside the bug toggle (`Settings.corner`'s new `extras`); for a player with no games (`profile.games` and wins + losses 0) its outline breathes until they open it once. The Daily Race card has a 2 px gold edge, Play in gold (`UI/Featured`), a flame before the streak and "Today's board" as a chip.

**Queue.** On a wide screen the column stands at a fixed top: the idle column's height is added up from its parts' own sizes (`idleHeight`) and the column starts where that would sit centered, so the wordmark stays exactly still when the queue, the code or "Opponent found" opens (before, the centered list moved it). A ring with the time waited ("0:35", from the server's `botAt` less `QUEUE_BOT_OFFER_SECONDS`) pulses (`Motion.ring` every 1.4 s and a breathing edge; still under reduced motion). "Play a bot while you wait" is the queue's primary.

**Opponent found!** The server now sends `found.opponent` (user id and Roblox display name, when the other player is on this server) with phases Answer and Waiting. The prompt shows their headshot (`rbxthumb://type=AvatarHeadShot&id=…&w=150&h=150`, the documented Content thumbnail format) and name, a bar that shrinks over `QUEUE_ACCEPT_SECONDS`, "Join match" featured, "Keep playing" a ghost, and no ×: `Sheet` gained `closable = false` (no ×, the scrim does not close), so the two buttons are the only ways out.

**Private code and invites.** The code is a read-only TextBox (`TextEditable` false); a tap focuses it and selects the code (`CursorPosition`, `SelectionStart`), labeled "Tap to copy". Roblox gives games no clipboard API, so the OS copy does the last step (desktop: Ctrl/Cmd+C; a phone's own menu). The waiting line's dots step every 0.4 s (static under reduced motion). "Invite a friend" is the friend sheet's main button.

**How to play.** Progress dots replace "1 of 6" (the page on show is a wider lit dot). "Play the tutorial" stays on every page (D-075) and is the featured main button on the last page. On a landscape phone the picture and the words stand side by side and the page title moves into the sheet's title row, so the nav row is in view.

**Accent buttons in sheets.** A sheet may now hold its own featured accent button (Invite a friend, Play the tutorial, Join match, Send report). The audit's `oneAccent` rule counts only an open sheet's accent buttons while one holds any (`Sheet` roots carry attribute `Modal`); the scrim covers the screen's. `PrimaryAction` is unchanged: still one per screen and never in a sheet.

**End screen (social only; E owns the layout).** When the opponent asks for a rematch, a banner with their portrait, "Buster wants a rematch!" and the seconds left stands just above the action bar and pops in; the bar's status line steps aside. A counted Daily Race run shows its rank as a big gold "#7" beside the flame, with gold confetti once (none under reduced motion). After the tutorial the stamp is "You're ready!" in green (half the usual tilt so it stays on screen), over a "Practice done!" card, win or lose.

**Tutorial tip.** On a landscape phone a coach tip goes first in the campaign's side column (`ORDER.tipTop`), so its Got it is never under the action bar (the last week's "Ground game" tip was).

**Bug reports.** A small bug toggle in the Lobby's corner and "Report a bug" at the end of the in-match Help sheet open `UI/BugSheet`: a text box with an "N/400" counter, Send and Cancel. The client checks the length (`Net/BugReport.clean`, shared with the server) and sends `BugReport { text, screen }` (the screen from `Router.pick`). The server (`server/BugReports`): cleans the text again, rate-limits per player (one a minute, five a day, in this server's memory; a failed try does not count), filters it with `TextService:FilterStringAsync(text, userId, PublicChat)` and stores `TextFilterResult:GetNonChatStringForBroadcastAsync()` (the docs: if the filter fails, show the text to nobody: such a report is not stored), then appends `{ at, userId, version, screen, matchId, text }` to `BugReports_v1` key `Day_<unix day>_<userId % 4 + 1>` (at most 50 per key, the oldest dropped: about 25 KB, far under the 4 MB value limit; one player only ever fills their own shard). The text field is the one request string allowed past `Schemas`' 64-character limit (up to 2048 bytes before cleaning). Admins (the Name Pass's admin pattern, D-069: the server checks `Admins.isAdmin` on every request) get "Bug reports" beside Review names; `BugList` (cooled down 5 s) reads every shard of the last 7 days and sends the newest 60 with each sender's username, the time (UTC), screen, version and match id. APIs checked against the creator docs: `TextService:FilterStringAsync`, `TextFilterResult:GetNonChatStringForBroadcastAsync`, `GlobalDataStore:UpdateAsync`/`GetAsync`, `TextBox.TextEditable`/`CursorPosition`/`SelectionStart`, Content `rbxthumb` AvatarHeadShot sizes.

**Config** (not rules: bug reports): `BUG_REPORT_MIN_CHARS` 5, `BUG_REPORT_MAX_CHARS` 400, `BUG_REPORT_WAIT` 60 s, `BUG_REPORTS_PER_DAY` 5, `BUG_REPORT_SHARDS` 4, `BUG_REPORTS_PER_KEY` 50, `BUG_REPORT_LIST_DAYS` 7, `BUG_LIST_COOLDOWN` 5 s. Display constants: `BrandMap.MAP_FADE` 0.86, Lobby `RING_EVERY` 1.4, `DOT_EVERY` 0.4, HowTo `COMPACT_ART` 0.62, Results `TUTORIAL_TILT` −3.

**Icons.** Lucide `flame` and `bug` (`assets/icons/`, ISC) are in `UI/Icons` with id `""`: until a human uploads them the game draws their glyphs (♦, ¤). **Needed from the humans:** upload `assets/icons/flame.png` and `bug.png` and send back the two ids.

**Tests.** `tests/net/BugReport.spec` (cleaning, screen tag, rate limit, keys, size cap, newest-first list, Schemas), `tests/e2e/BugReport.spec` (filtered and stored with version, screen and match id; rate limit; a failed filter stores nothing; admins only), `tests/ui/LobbyA17.spec` (every item above at phone, iPad and desktop, strict audit 0), `e2e/Matchmaking.spec` (the opponent in `found`). Snapshots `01-lobby-new-a17`, `02q-lobby-queue-a17`, `09v-found-opponent-a17`, `03-bug-sheet-a17`, `26b-help-bug-a17`, `01k-lobby-bugs-a17`; audit 0 at phone, iPad and desktop.
### D-091 — Select, the draft and the stance: picks you can see, warnings before the draft's rounds 2-3, everything important on a landscape phone's first screen (designer's list items 12-22 and two playtest notes, approved by the user; UI only, no rule change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-b`)

**Why.** The designer's 0.17 list (items 12-22) for the candidate pick, the issues, the draft and the stance, plus two playtest findings (4 of 6 new players): the rival can nominate *your* strongholds in draft rounds 2-3 and nothing warned them before picking, and tapping the rival's home in those rounds was refused without saying why. Phones are landscape now (D-089), whose notes left two of these screens rough: Select's cards scrolled about 200 px and the Decision's stances B and C were a scroll away.

**Shared pieces (new files, so no other screen changes).**
- `UI/CheckBadge`: a 24 px round check in the player's color (the accent when none is given), edged in the background color; it pops in from 0.5 (`Motion.pop`, so it simply appears under Reduce motion) and hides when the pick goes away. Used on a picked candidate (on the portrait's lower right), a picked issue tile and the picked stance.
- `UI/LinkButton`: a text link with its label right-aligned and an arrow after it, 48 px tall (was a 44 px ghost button with its words floating mid-box). "About Goldie", "How issues work", "Rename", "Get the Name Pass", "How the draft works".
- `Screens/Common/PickClock`: the last seconds of a pick. The header's timer already turns orange under 10 s and ticks under 5 s; on Select, the draft and the stance the pinned Lock in now also changes look: from 10 s left a veil drains across it (`ActionBar:setProgress`, unused until now) and it pulses once as that starts (nothing under Reduce motion). Only while there is something to press.

**Select.**
- Item 12: portraits 72 px on a wide screen (an iPad; was 56), cards 144 px; a check badge pops in on your pick (the card's own pop stays).
- Item 14: a picked issue tile is tinted 30 % toward your color, outlined and checked (no more "•" before its name); symbols 26 px (22 on a phone; were 20); the votes bold; the boost a green pill ("+41% boost").
- Item 13: the loose ghost buttons are `LinkButton`s.
- Item 18: the Color Pass swatches are plain 28 px dots in their color with the name under them (the initials read "DO DO DO …").
- Item 19: the name sheet: the box is outlined (2 px) with a live "11/16" count beside one short line of rules ("Only players 16+ see it. Follow Roblox's Community Standards."); "Save name" is the sheet's big button (56 px, full width; still a secondary, as a sheet never holds the screen's accent action, Sheet's rule); "Use the candidate's name" a quiet button under it.
- Item 20: the name line and the Name Pass offer are one card: a drawn pencil, "Name: …" and a gold "Name Pass" pill (the Amber player color), then the link, then (for the offer) Roblox's rules.
- Item 22: the header's title is a two-dot step indicator ("● Candidate ○ Issues", the current step bold) instead of "Your candidate" over "Pick your candidate"; the action bar's status no longer says "Step 1 of 2".
- Landscape phone (Kit.compact): a `side` page (Layout, D-089): the header and the action bar in the right column, the cards or tiles the full height on the left in two columns. A compact card is 68 px: the 44 px portrait beside name, strength and weakness (the kind is in "About"); a compact tile 68 px. All eight cards and all eight tiles fit with no scroll. The right column's middle (`SideExtras`) holds the tip, the color line, "About …" on its own row, the swatches and the name card.

**Draft.**
- Playtest warning 1: in a battleground round a note under the slots (`RoundNote`, orange outline) says "Heads up: Buster can nominate your strongholds too (VA CO AL VT MS). A nominated stronghold loses its bonus." (your strongholds still standing; hidden when none are left, and in the home round).
- Playtest warning 2: a refused tap still shows its short reason in the status line, and now also a notice under the slots with the rule (`Logic.why`): "TX is Buster's home state. Home states can't be nominated, so its home bonus is safe." (also for a stronghold in the home round, and a battleground). The next tap clears it.
- Item 17 (written for portrait phones; applied to landscape): the slots come first in the panel, one row of two chips with a numbered badge each (filled once a state is in it): "① WY · Wyoming ×", "② Backup". The first-pick tip floats over the map's upper left, under the lens chips, so it no longer pushes the panel down; the first tap dismisses it. On a landscape phone "Ties go to Buster" moves into the header's subtitle ("Battleground · ties go to Buster"), the pill row goes, and the round note keeps only the warning (the battleground rule is the subtitle and How the draft works). A landscape phone's panel is about 140 px above the pinned Lock in, so the slots and the warning are what fit above the fold; the map is the main way to pick.
- Item 15: the map reveal on a wide screen: the map takes 0.86 of its column's height (was 0.66; only the legend's note is under it) and the panel 40 % of the width (was 35 %); the strongholds are two columns, one per player: a 36 px portrait (was 24) with the name and the votes they hold, then each stronghold as a row ("VA Virginia 13"), biggest first.
- Item 16: a round's reveal: each card's title is one line that shrinks (no more "Buster got" / "OH" on two lines), the state's votes count up under it ("17 votes", 0.6 s, at once under Reduce motion; not "+17": a battleground is in play, not won), both states pulse as well as ring, and the draft board sits in a card ("Draft board") under the picks on every screen; the map gets the bigger height there too.

**Stance (Decision).** Item 21: each stance chip carries a net pill: states it helps minus states it hurts ("Net +11" green, "Net −1" orange with its sign, "Net 0" quiet); the picked stance a check at the top right; the Helps/Hurts key is bigger (18 px rings, 3 px outline, bold body text). Landscape phone: the three stances sit side by side (100 px each, caption-size names, "+21 −10" without "states") and come before the event's lines, so all three and their nets are on the first screen; the "Why?" and the event text follow. The chips' code lives in `CampaignParts/EventCard` (`EventCard.new`, the Decision's card); the event strip and the plan screen are unchanged.

**Reduce motion.** Every new effect goes through `Motion`: the check's pop, the count-up, the Lock in's pulse (none under Reduce motion), the map's pulse (`MapInterface`, unchanged). The veil drains under Reduce motion too: it is information (time left), not decoration.

**Config.** None (no gameplay number). Display constants: Select `FACE` 72, `CARD_H` 144, `CARD_H_COMPACT` 68, `FACE_COMPACT` 44, `ISSUE_H` 80, `ISSUE_H_PORTRAIT` 104, `ISSUE_H_COMPACT` 68, `ISSUE_ICON` 26, `ISSUE_ICON_SMALL` 22, `ISSUE_TINT` 0.3, `SWATCH` 28, `DETAILS_W` 200; Draft `REVEAL_MAP_HEIGHT_SHARE` 0.86, `MAP_SHARE_REVEAL` 0.6, `SLOT_BADGE` 24, `TIP_W` 360, `GAIN_SECONDS` 0.6, `NOTE_EDGE` 1.5; Board `HOLD_FACE` 36, `HOLD_ROW_H` 28; EventCard `STANCE_H_COMPACT` 100, `STANCE_NET_W` 80; Decision `CAPTION_H` 26, `DOT` 18, `KEY_RING` 3; `CheckBadge.SIZE` 24; `LinkButton.HEIGHT` 48.

**Tests.** `Select.spec` (the tile's bold votes and boost pill, checks on the picked tile and card, the tinted tile, plain swatch dots, an empty status line, the last-seconds drain), `Draft.spec` (the numbered slots' labels, the stronghold rows, the refusal notice with the rule, the battleground warning and its absence in the home round), `Campaign.spec` (the stance chips' net pill and check, the compact chips' numbers). Snapshots: `03i-select-name-card-b17`, `04c-select-issues-full-b17`, `06c-draft-refused-b17`, `07b-decision-picked-b17`, `07c-decision-last-seconds-b17`, and every existing Select, map reveal, draft and stance shot; 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

**Not done here.** The plan screen's header on a landscape phone overlaps the two long names on the score bar (`TopBar`, slice D's item 26); the draft panel's Lock in still takes about 90 of a landscape phone's 230 px, so only the slots and the warning are above the fold there (the map is the main way to pick).
### D-092 — The map: codes never move, the info goes around them; Money's dollars back; outlines inside their states; labeled tabs (amends D-079's label rule; user feedback items 3, 4, 5, 31 of batch 0.17, approved; UI only, no rule or Config change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-c`)

**Ask.** "Borders on states overlap and cover each other, so it's hard to tell which state is outlined." "Money screen is much worse, should just have values." "State initials should never move; other info from the views moves around it." (and the phone Votes map's WV and KY labels far off their states). "Close looks like a fifth tab": a labeled "Close races" toggle, tab icons, a labeled "?" key.

**Labels (amends D-079's "one label form").** D-079 moved a whole "PA 19" plate, code included, when it did not fit. Now:
- *The code stands at its home:* its anchor, or up to `CODE_SHIFT` (4) px off it where two codes at 12 px would touch (VA/WV, IN/OH on a phone), still inside its state. Homes depend on the map's size only, so a code is in the same spot in every lens, week and screen (a spec switches lenses and checks every code stays within 0.6 px). Chips keep their line centered in the chip.
- *The info goes around it, in a fixed order:* the lens value, then the plan's badge, the preview's number and a reveal's tag, as one line. It joins the code on one plate right after it ("PA 19", the D-079 look and the usual case), else under it; on a phone, or for a line carrying the plan's or a reveal's tags, else over it. Never left (the first try, "10 WI", read as Minnesota's 10).
- *Only the info leaves the state,* on its own plate with a 1.5 px leader to the code's plate, at 8 to 56 px, never where another code's info would join it, its leader never under another label.
- *A phone hides what cannot sit near its code.* A lone value with no clear spot within 19 px stays off the map (its card has it); long leaders across a 374 px map read worse than a missing number. Lines with tags always show.
- *One text size per map* as before (14, 13, 12: the largest with the fewest misses); plate padding 1 px on a phone (`COMPACT_PAD`), so 42 codes fit.
Measured on the Plan fixture (Campaign, the game area without the top bar): phone (map 374x234) Votes 12 px, 5 off with leaders, 7 hidden; Polls 7 / 17; Money 6 / 15. iPad (630x394) Votes 14 px, 3 leaders, none hidden; Polls and Money 12 px, 3-4. Desktop (752x470) 2-3 leaders. Before (D-079), the phone moved 17 whole plates with their codes and showed every value; the user preferred codes that never move.

**Money.** Every state shows what one fundraiser would raise ("$5.8"), the `Lens.MONEY_TOP` (8) richest in gold, drained pools dimmed; the coins and `Lens.COIN_TIERS` are gone. A phone drops the "$" (`LensPart.short`, new optional field on `MapInterface`, backward compatible). The Map key's line says it.

**Outlines.** Each outline segment is moved half its width inward along its edge's normal, so the line runs just inside its own state and two outlined neighbors sit side by side instead of one covering the other; where they still touch, a rank orders them (selection over a moved state's halo over the key dashes over an owner's ring over a hollow hairline).

**Tabs.** Votes, Issues, Polls and Money carry icons (`vote`, new `scale`, new `chart-column`, `coins`; the two new Lucide icons are in `assets/icons` and draw a glyph until uploaded). "Close" is a "Close races" toggle with a switch. The landscape phone's rail (D-089) is now the labeled "Map key", the four tabs 2 x 2 with the icon over the word, and the toggle across the rail's width; wide screens keep the row ("Key", the toggle, the tabs). Where a row is short (a portrait tablet, the Draft's lens row), the tabs take narrow cells and then the toggle stacks its switch over its word (`LensToggle.setNarrow`, `setCloseNarrow`, `row`).

**Snapshots.** The fake engine sized the Game ScreenGui to the whole screen while the snapshot drew the 58 px top bar over it, so every phone map was laid out about 30 % bigger than it was drawn (the WV/KY plates looked far off partly because of that). `snap.luau` now lays screens out without the bar by default (`world.screen.inset`, opt-in in `FakeRoblox`; `--no-inset` for the old way); specs are unchanged. The Campaign phone map is 374x234 in the wide layout, not 258x235.

**Display constants** (ShapeMap): `COMPACT_PAD` 1, `CODE_SHIFT` 4, `SIDES` R, B; `COMPACT_SIDES` R, B, A; `OFF_GAPS` 8-56, `OFF_DIRS`, `OFF_CLEAR` 3, `OFF_ZONE` 8, `OFF_FAR_COST` 150, `COMPACT_OFF` 3, `PLATE_LEADER_PX` 1.5, `PLATE_LEADER_FADE` 0.2, `PIN_REACH` up to 6.8; LensToggle `TAB_W` 76-90, `CLOSE_W` 132, `NARROW_CELL` 56, `CLOSE_NARROW_W` 96. No Config value changed.

**Tests.** `ShapeMap.spec` "keeps every code at home…" (five map sizes: code within 4.6 px of its anchor, value right of / under / over it on one plate or on its own plate, one text size ≥ 12, no two plates overlap, leaders counted, codes unmoved by a lens change), `Lens.spec` (Money's dollars, gold top 8, no coins), `Icons.spec`/`Kit.spec` (14 icons). Snapshots `10k-select-key-c17`, `11c-select-crowded-c17`, `14d-money-close-c17`; every shot 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.

### D-094 — Match records for balance analysis, per-player stats and the public landslide count, analytics funnels (data; no rule change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `data17`)

**Why.** The designer's list for 0.17, items 4.1 and 5.1: "Keep track of all players' stats… track all matches… use it for balance updates", and "a number next to your name showing how many landslides you got in public matches". Until now the server kept per player only the rating, W/L, per-candidate record, the last 10 rated matches, a practice tally and the Daily streak; nothing about what was played, and no landslide count. The balance harness tells us how bots play; this tells us how people play.

**Match records** (`Net/MatchRecord`, pure; `server/MatchLog`).
- *What:* one record per session that ends, abandoned ones included (`reason` says so). Fields: schema `v` (1), a unique `id` (server, match, seed), game `version`, `mode` (`Queue` = the public queue, its match server or a rematch of one; `Private` = a private code or its rematch; `Practice`; `WaitBot`; `Tutorial`; `Daily`), `rated`, the bot's `level`, `deck` (the event deck on or off), `tutorial`, `seed`, `at` (Unix end), `duration` (s), `weeks` planned, per seat the id (UserId as a string, or `"bot"`), kind, candidate, platform, home, strongholds and a bot's level and style; the draft picks; every week's plan per seat as compact strings (`"Rally:OH:Jobs"`, `"Gotv:PA:3"`, `"Attack:OH:Jobs@1"`, `"Pivot:Health>Transit"`, `"RegionPoll:West"`); every stance (`{week, event, choices}`); every debate round's topic, moves, Zingers, canned answers and winner, plus crowd and ovation; every resolved event; the projected EV each time the weekly polls came out (`[week, ev1, ev2]`); final `ev`, `decidedBy`, `reason`, `winner`, `landslide` (`Election.landslide`), `unrated`, `forfeits` (seats) and `timeouts` (`"Phase:week:seat"` for every input stage a seat let run out). The plans are not in the match state, so `Net/Session` now keeps a small `log` (each closed plan and each timeout) and `startedAt`; nothing in `Sim/` changed.
- *No PII beyond the UserId:* no usernames, custom names (a spec checks a custom name never appears) or chat. Everything else is what both players saw by the end, plus the seed.
- *Where:* DataStore `MatchRecords_v1`, one key per match, `"<YYYYMMDD>_<server 8>_<match id>_<seed>"` (UTC date first, at most 50 characters). Sharded by date by construction: Open Cloud's list filter `id.startsWith("20261029_")` returns one day. One write per key ever, so the per-key limit (4 MB a minute) never matters.
- *Size:* 3-5 KB of JSON for a 12-week match (spec: under 12,000 characters). `MatchRecord.fit` drops the plans, then the debate rounds, past `MAX_CHARS` 64,000 (the value limit is 4,194,304), marking `trimmed`. Storage counts towards the experience's quota (500 MB + 1 MB per lifetime user): at 5 KB, 100,000 matches is 500 MB, so the humans delete days they have exported (TESTING.md).
- *Never in a match's way:* `MatchService` calls `MatchLog.record` after the result is applied, in a pcall; it builds the record in a pcall, queues it (at most 30; the oldest drops) and returns. One writer thread checks `DataStoreService:GetRequestBudgetForRequestType(StandardWrite)` and waits (5 s steps, up to 2 minutes) while it is 20 or less, leaving that room to player saves (the server's budget is 60 + 40 per player a minute, shared); errors retry 5 times with exponential backoff (2, 4, 8, 16 s), then the record is dropped with a warning. In an unpublished place or with Studio API access off the first write turns match records off for the session with one warning. BindToClose waits up to 10 s for the queue (DataService's own wait is 25 of Roblox's 30, in parallel).

**Per-player stats** (`Profile.stats`, optional, its own schema version `Profile.STATS_VERSION` 1).
- *The public landslide count* (`stats.landslides`): election wins by a landslide (the winner took more than `LANDSLIDE_EV_SHARE` 70% of the EV in play, D-062) in **rated matches from the public queue** (a match server it made and a rematch of one included). Not counted: private codes (two friends could farm it), practice, the tutorial, the Daily Race, unrated matches (D-077, D-081) and forfeits. It is what the Lobby, the leaderboard and the opponent's name show.
- *Fuller stats:* `landslidesAll` (any recorded match), `publicGames`, `privateGames`, `elections`, `forfeitWins`, `forfeitLosses`, `evFor`, `evAgainst`, `bestEV`, `lastAt`, `since` (when counting began) and `onboard` (analytics, below). Counted by `Profile.applyResult` for every rated and practice result; the Daily Race keeps its own record (D-082) and an abandoned match counts nothing. The result carries two new optional fields, `landslide` and `public`, so a result applied later from another server's pending list counts the same.
- *Migration:* no profile version bump (a bump would make 0.16 servers read these profiles as "from the future" and stop saving them, D-028's rule for optional fields). A profile without `stats` gets one on its next recorded match, counting from then: nothing is backfilled, because `recent` cannot tell a public match from a private one. `Profile.cleanStats` cleans what is stored (counts default to 0, garbage dropped, `onboard` capped at 100); a block with a newer `v` is kept field for field, never counted into, and saved unchanged. Known gap, as for every optional field so far: a 0.16 server still running after the update drops `stats` if it saves the profile.

**Where the count shows** (UI kept small while those screens are being redesigned): the Lobby's record line ("Rating 1040 · 5 won, 2 lost · ▲3 landslides"); a gold "▲3" pill on leaderboard rows (`UI/LandslideBadge`, reusable); after the opponent's name on the plan screen's bar ("Rex ▲3 · thinking"). Nothing shows at 0. The match seat carries it (`SeatInfo.landslides`, from `SeatSpec.landslides`), so the redesigned name plates can place the badge. The leaderboard's counts come from a second OrderedDataStore `Landslides_v1` (same keys, the count as value), written when a public landslide is counted and when a player with some loads in, and read with the rating page (`GetSortedAsync(false, 100)`, one more sorted read a refresh, within 5 + 2 per player a minute). A row shows a count when its player is among the 100 with the most (or was written on this server); with a large player base a top-50 player with few landslides may show none.

**Analytics** (`server/Analytics`; checked against the AnalyticsService reference and the Funnel events, Custom events, Custom fields and Event types pages).
- *Onboarding funnel* (`LogOnboardingFunnelStepEvent`, steps 1-100): 1 Joined, 2 Tutorial started, 3-6 Tutorial weeks 1-4, 7 Tutorial finished, 8 First match started (not the tutorial), 9 First match finished, 10 Joined the queue, 11 Finished a rated match, 12 Asked for a rematch, 13 Played the Daily Race. Only for players who join as new players from 0.17 on (no games, no practice, tutorial not done: `stats.onboard` starts at 1), so veterans never pollute it. Each step is logged once and only forward (Roblox counts skipped steps as done, so a new player who queues first logs 10 and later 11, not 8 or 9); the step reached is saved in the profile, so a funnel spans visits. Steps are never renumbered; new ones go at the end.
- *Visit funnel* (`LogFunnelStepEvent`, funnel "Visit", a GUID per visit as its session id): 1 In the lobby, 2 Looked for a game (queue, bot, Daily Race, code), 3 Match started, 4 Match finished, 5 Played again, 6 Finished again. Every player.
- *Custom events* (`LogCustomEvent`; 6 of the 100 names allowed): `MatchStarted` (mode, bot level or "Human"), `MatchEnded` (value: seconds; mode, reason, Won/Lost/None), `Landslide` (mode, level, candidate), `QueueJoined`, `QueueLeft` (value: seconds waited; Matched/Left), `Rematch` (Practice/Match). Custom fields are `CustomField01-03` with string values, as the docs require; their combinations stay far under 8,000.
- *Limits:* the documented budget is 120 + 20 × CCU requests a minute; the module stops at 100 a minute per server (one warning), and a player's whole visit is a handful of calls. Every call is pcall'd; a failing service breaks nothing (spec). The client sends nothing for analytics.

**Reading the data.** `lune run tests/harness/records.luau records.jsonl [mode=Queue] [humans] [version=0.17.0]` reads JSON lines (raw records, or Open Cloud entries with the record in `value`) and prints the balance harness's shape: candidate win rates (flagged 45-55% from 20 games), the matchup matrix, match shape (margins, 40+ and 100+ EV, landslide rate, comebacks from the week-8 projection, the week-12 projected leader losing, forfeits), debates (decisive: the side that won more rounds won the match; move shares, splits, canned answers, Zingers), stances, and timings (full-match minutes p50/p90 against D-005's 15, timeouts per match, the share of human plans left to time out). How the humans export: TESTING.md, "Match records and analytics".

**Config.** None: every number here is a storage or request budget, not gameplay (`MatchRecord.MAX_CHARS`, `MatchLog`'s queue, budget reserve and retry waits, `Analytics`' per-minute cap, `Leaderboard.LANDSLIDE_TOP`), so each lives in its module with a comment. No rule changed: `balance.luau 400` prints the same report as before (timing lines aside).

**Tests.** `tests/net/MatchRecord.spec` (a played match's record: seats, plans each week with a timeout week, stances, debates, events, projected EV, final EV and the landslide flag, no custom name, size, the key and dates, `fit`, a forfeit; Profile stats: public vs private vs practice landslides, forfeits, abandoned, migration of none/garbage/old/newer blocks, pending results keep the flags, onboarding only forward), `tests/net/Records.spec` (parsing raw and Open Cloud lines, the summary's sections and counts, filters), `tests/e2e/Records.spec` (a queued match writes one record only at its end and both profiles' stats; a private code's is Private and not public; a failing record store, a low write budget and an unpublished place never break a match or a profile save; a new player's onboarding through the tutorial and a first match, the visit funnel and custom events; forward-only onboarding and none for a veteran; a failing AnalyticsService breaks nothing). The fake engine gained `AnalyticsService`, `HttpService` (JSONEncode, GenerateGUID) and `GetRequestBudgetForRequestType`.
## 0.17: a harder ladder, a quicker rubber band

### D-095 — Medium, Hard and Extreme get stronger; every level still plays fair (tuning + two new bot profiles, Oct 29, 2026, branch `balance17`)

**Ask.** The designer played 0.16.0: Medium and Hard are too easy (Easy is fine). In a scripted playtest Medium lost 2 of 2 by 140+ EV. Targets against the scripted novices (`tests/lib/Novice`): Medium, a typical new player 20-30% and an attentive one 55-65%; Hard, typical ≤ 8% and attentive 20-30%; Extreme, the strongest fair bot available, attentive ≤ 10%. Easy unchanged. Every level fair as before (D-057, D-087): no handicap, the same View, the same cash, AP and information, the rubber band the same for both seats.

**Rule.**

| Level | Profile | How it plays | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Easy | Rookie | unchanged (D-087) | none |
| Medium | Casual | every state's worth × a log-normal factor drawn once a match, spread `BOT_CASUAL_NOISE`; every stance and debate move random | noise 1.5 → **0.5** |
| Hard | **Sharp** (new) | a slight misjudgment redrawn every plan (`BOT_SHARP_NOISE` 0.3, `BOT_SHARP_ONCE` false); a quarter of its stances and debate moves random (`BOT_SHARP_SLIP` 0.25) | was Hazy (0.45, half random) |
| Extreme | **Expert** (new) | the standard bot's plan, stances and debate, but it expects the campaign to move every state's margin much further before election night (`BOT_EXPERT_CAMPAIGN_VARIANCE` 24 per week left; the standard bot's `Bot.tuning.campaignVariance` is 2), so it fights for every state still in reach instead of only today's close ones | was Balanced |

Hazy (D-057's Easy) and Balanced (the standard bot) are no level now; their numbers are unchanged, so the tutorial harness's careless stand-in, the novice study modes (`STUDY` is now the Hazy profile by name) and the balance harness mean what they did. The Daily Race still plays Foresight with its own numbers (unchanged); timers still follow the level (Easy ×2, Medium ×1.5, Hard and Extreme ×1); the Lobby's level lines still fit (UI not touched).

**How Extreme was found.** The retired bots are weaker against people, not stronger: Foresight and Strategist beat the standard bot (69%) but lose more to the attentive novice (26.5% and 25.7% against Balanced's 12.6%): they predict a standard-bot opponent, which a person is not. Foresight's debate alone (planning switched off) costs 4 points against novices. The standard bot's `Bot.tuning` knobs, one at a time against the attentive novice (1,000 each, ±2): `moneyValue` 0.2 / 0.6: 16.6 / 11.6; `gotvFromWeek` 4: 11.6; `draftSigma` 4 / 12: 13.1 / 12.5; no paid polls: 13.2; `campaignVariance` 1 / 4 / 6 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 40: 16.6 / 11.7 / 9.0 / 8.2 / 7.7 / 6.4 / 5.3 / 4.5. And head to head against the standard bot the wider view wins 70 / 73 / 78 / 80 / 81% from 6 to 40, flat from 16 on. 24 sits on that plateau. *Worth knowing:* the balance harness's standard bot is far from its best on this one heuristic; it is kept as is (its reports keep their meaning), but a future balance pass could use the Expert as its yardstick.

**Measured** (`lune run tests/harness/novice.luau ladder 1000`; seeds 500001 on, seats alternate, two different candidates, every player from its own View, 0 invalid inputs; ± is the 95% band; with D-096's rubber band):

| Player | Easy | Medium | Hard | Extreme |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Weak novice | 38.2% ± 3.0 | 4.1% ± 1.2 | 0.7% ± 0.6 | 0.4% ± 0.6 |
| **Typical novice** | 68.0% ± 2.9 | **22.3% ± 2.6** (was 40.7) | **4.6% ± 1.3** (was 7.6) | 5.0% ± 1.4 (was 3.4) |
| **Attentive novice** | 96.4% ± 1.2 | **59.5% ± 3.0** (was 85.1) | **25.5% ± 2.7** (was 41.4) | **5.3% ± 1.4** (was 15.5) |
| **Typical, aiming at the key states (D-088)** | 91.0% ± 1.8 | **52.8% ± 3.1** | 25.1% ± 2.7 | 4.3% ± 1.3 |
| *targets* | *unchanged* | *typical 20-30, attentive 55-65* | *typical ≤ 8, attentive 20-30* | *attentive ≤ 10* |

So a player who uses the key-states aid wins about half the time on Medium and one in four on Hard. Median margins: typical vs Medium 70 EV (its wins 36), attentive vs Medium 50 (its wins 58, losses 42): a contest, not a rout. Medium was also tried at noise 0.6 / 0.7 (typical 22.9 / 26.9, attentive 64.4 / 67.0) and at noise 1 with half its decisions random (18.4 / 59.7); Hard at Hazy's noise with a quarter random (4.4 / 29.4) and at noise 0.2 (4.3 / 24.4).

Between the bots (the easier side reported; `novice.luau ladder` / `difficulty.luau 1000`, seeds 300001 on): Easy vs Medium 3.9 / 5.2%, Medium vs Hard 20.0 / 22.6%, Hard vs Extreme 7.5 / 7.6%, Easy vs Extreme 0.1 / 0.0%. Extreme beats the standard bot 80.5%, Foresight 90.1 / 92.2% and Strategist 85.2 / 87.0%. The Daily Race's bot: Foresight vs Balanced 70.7% (D-087: 68.9% under D-020's rubber band; its code and numbers did not change). Plan time: Expert 0.26 ms mean, 1.4 ms worst; Sharp 0.27 / 1.2.

**Config.** `BOT_CASUAL_NOISE` 1.5 → 0.5; new `BOT_SHARP_NOISE` 0.3, `BOT_SHARP_ONCE` false, `BOT_SHARP_SLIP` 0.25, `BOT_EXPERT_CAMPAIGN_VARIANCE` 24. `BOT_HAZY_*` unchanged (no level reads them).

**Tests.** `tests/sim/BotDifficulty.spec`: the new map (Hard Sharp, Extreme Expert); Sharp's misjudgment from Config and redrawn every plan; each step up misjudges or slips less; the Expert plans exactly as the standard bot would with the wider campaign variance, and leaves the standard bot's own plan untouched; valid, deterministic, spending and fair-play checks (true support, undecided, ground game, pools, poll errors, the opponent's polls, the opponent's cash inside its bracket) now cover all eight profiles. Harnesses: `novice.luau ladder` adds the KeyStates player and Extreme against Balanced and Strategist; `keys` runs every level; `difficulty.luau` reports Foresight vs Balanced for the Daily Race and Extreme against the retired bots.

### D-096 — The underdog boost ends the week the latest polls clear it (rule change approved by the user, Oct 29, 2026, branch `balance17`)

**Why.** D-020's two rubber-band targets were both red: the boost went to a player truly 20+ EV ahead in 16-18% of underdog weeks (target < 10%) and the tier changed 3.1 times a match (target ≤ 3). The tier read a slow smoothed gap (weight 0.4 on the newest week) with 30 EV of hysteresis, so after a comeback it kept paying for weeks. Faster smoothing alone fixes the first and breaks the second (`UNDERDOG_SMOOTHING` 1, no smoothing: 11.0% and 7.8 changes; 0.6: 13.7% and 4.4).

**Rule.** The tier keeps its own smoothed lead (`underdogLead`), the same exponential smoothing of the public projected-EV lead. But when the latest public polls put the player within the tier-1 exit line (`UNDERDOG_EV_1` - `UNDERDOG_HYSTERESIS` = 10 EV behind, or anything better), that smoothed lead restarts from the latest polls. So the boost ends the week the polls show the race level or the player ahead, and has to build up again from there before it can return: one bad week 45 EV behind after that restarts at about 16, under the line. Entry and the tier-2 to tier-1 step are smoothed and use hysteresis as before. `UNDERDOG_SMOOTHING` 0.4 → 0.35, since the quick exit takes away the flicker the slower entry used to hide. Public polls only, as before: nothing hidden reaches it (a spec checks). The frontrunner drawback (Okafor's, D-020) keeps the plain smoothed lead, at the same weight. No new Config number: the clear line is the existing tier-1 exit line.

**What was tried** (2,000 balance matches each; wrong-leader share / tier changes a match / underdog weeks a match): D-020 as is 17.6% / 3.09 / 6.7; restart when the latest polls show a lead (0 instead of -10) 9.8 / 2.95; restart at -10 with smoothing 0.4: 9.0 / 3.05; **at 0.35: 8.5 / 2.70 / 4.1**; at 0.3: 8.6 / 2.28 / 3.6 (a weaker boost overall); end the tier on the latest polls without restarting the smoothing: 9.2 / 3.31 (it flickers back on); the tier from min(smoothed, latest): 9.1 / 3.72; a probability-weighted projection instead of winner-take-all (σ 3, unsmoothed): 8.8 / 5.84, a different signal from the one the screen shows.

**Measured** (`lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 2000`, the standard bot both sides, before → after):

| Metric | Before | After | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| Underdog weeks to a true 20+ EV leader | 17.6% | **8.5%** | < 10% |
| Underdog tier changes per match | 3.09 | **2.70** | ≤ 3 |
| Comeback rate (trailing true EV at week 8) | 41.6% | 36.8% | 20-40% |
| Decided by 40+ / 100+ EV | 59.8 / 15.0% | 59.6 / 17.3% | ≤ 65 / ≤ 25% |
| GOTV changes the winner | 9.2% | 8.8% | ≥ 10% (red before) |
| Candidates (Okafor, Calloway, Venkat, Hale, Alvarado, Reyes, Strand, Begay) | 56.7, 56.8, 50.1, 49.5, 46.0, 47.1, 42.2, 51.5 | 54.6, 57.2, 54.2, 44.4, 50.3, 47.9, 42.0, 49.4 | 45-55% (±4.4 each at ~500 games) |

At 400 matches: 16.2% / 3.16 → 8.4% / 2.65. Candidate rates move within their noise; Calloway and Strand were red before and still are, Hale dips just under 45 (49.1% at 400). Every other line is within noise of before.

**Okafor vs Venkat (report only).** The 400-match report flagged this pair at 14.3% on about 14 games. 4,000 matches of just that pair (seeds 700001-704000, seats alternating, standard bot both sides): Okafor wins **51.2% ± 1.5** on 0.16.0's rules and 50.7% ± 1.5 with D-096. The flag was noise; no action.

**Copy to update (UI, not in this branch):** All the numbers' underdog line ("smoothed over weeks … You drop a tier only once the gap is 30 EV under its line") is still true for tier 2 but should add that the boost ends as soon as the latest polls put you within 10 EV; the News line "it fades once you stay ahead" could read "it ends once the polls show you level".

**Tests.** `tests/sim/Polls.spec` (the smoothed entry and tier-2 hysteresis; the restart on a dead heat and on a lead, the plain lead still smoothing; one bad week after a restart stays off; a gap just over 10 still smooths; true support changes nothing), `tests/sim/View.spec` (`underdogLead` never reaches a View), `tests/sim/Tutorial.spec` (D-080's pinned normal match re-pinned: weeks 10-12 and the result, 290-248 → 292-246, moved with the boost).

### D-097 — The eight candidates get more eccentric names, personas and portraits; ids, perks and numbers unchanged (user request "replace the names and portraits ... keeping their stats", visual only, no Config changes, Oct 29, 2026, branch `reskin`)

**Why.** The user wanted the roster to feel more eccentric and memorable. The old names read like real people and the portraits like ordinary office photos. This is visual only: no new candidates, no perk, drawback, archetype, issue or number changed.

| id (unchanged) | Was | Now | Persona (the details sheet's second line) | Portrait |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `okafor` | Dana Okafor | Goldie Pennywhistle | A retired treasure hunter who pays for everything in gold doubloons. | gold-trimmed tricorn, doubloon earrings and pendant |
| `calloway` | Rex Calloway | Buster Cannonball | A retired human cannonball who still can't sit still. | red stunt helmet with goggles, blue showman's suit, epaulettes |
| `venkat` | Priya Venkat | Mavis Glimmer | A stage magician who can make an ad appear out of thin air. | tall top hat, high-collared purple cape, sparkles |
| `hale` | Marcus Hale | Percy Quibble | A penniless poet with a booming voice and a comeback for everything. | red beret, monocle, curled handlebar mustache, quill, striped scarf |
| `alvarado` | June Alvarado | Opal Crumpet | The old palace tea taster, who knows every back door in town. | lilac beehive, cat-eye glasses, pearls, a gold key |
| `reyes` | Tomas Reyes | Dash Kickflip | A skateboard stunt rider who's at his best coming from way behind. | teal-tipped spikes, sweatband, plaster, bomber with the bolt pin |
| `strand` | Helen Strand | Esme Moonbeam | A fortune teller who swears her crystal ball is just very good polling. | starry headscarf, round glasses, hoops, a crystal ball |
| `begay` | Walt Begay | Gus Bramble | A retired scoutmaster who can knock on a thousand doors before lunch. | ranger hat, big white beard, neckerchief, merit badges |

**Ids stay.** Every candidate keeps its id (`okafor` ... `begay`) and its place in `Candidates.ids`, so saved records, match history, the Daily Race's day-by-day candidate cycle (it indexes `Candidates.ids`), debug commands (`select hale ...`), the portrait files and `Assets.portraits` keys all keep working. Only `name` changed, and each entry gained a display-only `bio`. A spec pins the ids and their order, and checks first names and initials are unique (the phone score line uses first names, the plate's fallback uses initials).

**Names.** Two-word, short first names (4 to 6 letters) so the first name reads on a phone score line; every pair of initials differs; none is a real politician, celebrity, brand, party or slogan; all kid-safe. Pronouns and genders match the old roster so the copy that says "his Zinger" (Percy) and "her free polls" (Esme) stays right. The Name Pass still wins over all of this: a custom name replaces `profile.name` wherever the client shows a name (Model, Election, Select, Results, Net), unchanged.

**Persona.** `Profile.bio` is one line, shown as the second (muted) line of the Select details sheet ("About Goldie"), under the archetype. Nothing in the simulation reads it.

**Portraits.** `assets/portraits/make.py` (same pipeline, size, crop, plate and palette) draws the new looks; each gets a big headwear silhouette so they tell apart at 40 px. `assets/portraits/sheet.png` is the contact sheet. The PNG files keep their id names; the old art lives in git history. The game still points at the old Roblox asset ids until the new PNGs are uploaded and the eight numbers in `src/client/UI/Assets.luau` (`portraits`) are replaced; until then Studio shows the old faces beside the new names.

**Copy and tests.** Every example name in code comments, the specs (opponent "Rex" is now "Buster", "Dana" is "Goldie"), `docs/PLAN.md`'s roster table, `docs/TESTING.md` and the prose of `docs/UI-STYLE.md` uses the new names. Older `DESIGN.md` entries and `CHANGELOG.md` releases keep the names of their time, and the `UI-STYLE.md` wireframes keep their placeholder names so their boxes stay aligned.

**Balance.** `balance.luau 400` is identical to before, every line (run on this branch and on its base), and a dump of every profile field other than `name` and `bio` is identical too.

### D-098 — Debate, election night and the end screen: the result first and big, a quiet Skip, two columns on an iPad (designer's list items 34–42, approved; UI only, no rule or Config change, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-e`)

**Ask.** Batch 0.17, slice E: the end screen's bar hid vote counts on the iPad and cut card text on phones; "You won" and LANDSLIDE competed; the debate clash was cut off on phones (and on a landscape phone the third move sat half a scroll down, D-089); the verdict listed rounds as text; at 270 the badge covered the score; election night's tip covered the calls on a phone; "Still counting" showed an empty grey circle; Skip was the brightest thing on screen; the end-screen map crowded the Northeast with tags.

**Debate.**
- *Moves on a landscape phone* (`Moves:setCompact`): the three moves stand side by side as tiles in one row (name and strength, then the packed cells `Words.cellsShort`: "D+4 A−3 P="; the Safe tag on a row of its own; a Pivot's "Lands on" line only in Why?). All three sit above the bar with the Zinger chip under them. The stage column narrows to 0.3 of the width (`COMPACT_STAGE_SHARE`, was 0.33) so a tile is about 104 px; the stage's two faces each take half its row there, so they never overlap.
- *Clash*: the result first. The stamp (title size, its own row) leads, then both move cards big (the move in Fredoka, title size, shrinking to fit), then the counter and the round's line. The slam: both cards pop in, the stamp lands on them 0.2 s later and the panel jolts 6 px. On a landscape phone the panel packs tighter (6 px padding, 52 px stamp row, 58 px cards) so the stamp and both moves sit above the bar.
- *Verdict*: the crowd's winner big: their face (48 px) beside the stamp ("BUSTER WON THE CROWD"; none on an even crowd), then one chip per round in that round's winner's color (`Words.roundChip`: "Safety · Split", "Jobs · You", "Health · Buster"; "R2 · Buster" on a phone), then "Crowd: Buster +3". The lower-case "buster wins by 2" lines are gone. A stamp is keyed by its words, so a redrawn payload redraws it.

**Election night.**
- *270*: a full-width banner over the race bar in the winner's color, "GOLDIE WINS 270!" ("YOU WIN 270!"), with two rings bursting from it as it lands (120 and 220 px). It covers the bar, never the vote counts. Still named `Stamp270`; "270 TO WIN" still hides under it.
- *Still counting*: three pulsing dots where the empty portrait was, and a gold "DECIDING STATE" tag (the Amber player color, as the leaderboard's first medal) on the spotlight while the state that takes someone to 270 counts.
- *Landscape phone*: the map as wide as it is at the body's height and the side the rest; the side panel and spotlight pad 6 px and the spotlight is 74 px, so it sits whole above the bar (the bar covered its last line); the feed returns with the banner. The coach tip becomes the bar's status line "First to 270 wins · closest states last", once (marked seen), instead of a card over the calls.
- *Skip* (here and on the debate verdict): `ActionBar:setQuiet(true)`: the primary wears the raised look with white text and no light sweeping it, still tappable. New optional ActionBar method; other screens are unchanged.

**End screen.**
- *Top*: after a landslide LANDSLIDE is the hero (gold edge, and for the winner a gold light instead of white); "You won" / "You lost" shrinks to a filled pill over it (still `Banner`, in `BannerBox`); the eyebrow "YOU WON BY A" is gone. The plain "282 votes to 256" line is hidden after any election win (the cards say it); `Reason` stays for a tiebreak, a forfeit or an abandoned match.
- *Scoreboard*: the race bar, then both candidates side by side as compact cards: a 56 px portrait on the left; the name, "282 votes" (Fredoka), "You · 29 states", "2 debate topics won", each one line that shrinks rather than wraps, so a card's height is known (about 114 px, was about 250).
- *Two columns* on a wide screen (the page is 960 px there, was 640): the scoreboard and the rating line left; `RematchSlot` (empty, the place for the rematch banner), the tutorial card, the Daily Race summary, Why and Full breakdown right. One column on a phone. The vote counts now sit well above the bar on the iPad.
- *Map tab*: only the states the polls missed carry a tag (the value chip and "×", with the dashed outline in the real winner's color); every other state shows its code alone. The miss line stays one line, shrinking (never below 12 px) rather than wrapping. The map label code itself is unchanged.

**Reduce motion.** Every new effect goes through `Motion` (pop, stamp, shake, ring, breathe, shine), which does nothing under Reduce motion: the stamps, banner, dots and tags are simply there. `EndScreens.spec` checks the clash stamp lands at once.

**Config.** None. Display constants: Debate `COMPACT_STAGE_SHARE` 0.3; Moves `TILE_TITLE_H` 22, `TILE_CELLS_H` 18, `TILE_PAD` 6; Clash `CARD_H` 68 / 58, `STAMP_ROW` 60 / 52, `FACE` 48, `PAD_COMPACT` 6, `SLAM_DELAY` 0.2, `SLAM_PX` 6; Election `SPOT_H_COMPACT` 74, `COMPACT_PAD` 6, `WIN_BANNER_H` 30, `WIN_BURST` 120/220, `DOTS` 3, `DOT` 8; Results `MAX_WIDTH_WIDE` 960, `TWO_COL_W` 760, `PORTRAIT` 56 (was 64), `STAMP_SLACK` 24 (was 36), `LANDSLIDE_EDGE` 3.

**Tests.** `tests/ui/EndScreens.spec` (the words; tiles in a row; the clash's order and reduced motion; verdict chips, face and quiet Skip; the 270 banner's width and that it covers neither count; dots and the gold tag; quiet Skip; the phone's tip line and whole spotlight; one or two columns and compact cards; map tags only on misses). `Debate.spec` (a phone's cells are the packed form) and `ResultsLandslide.spec` (the pill, no eyebrow, the hidden vote line, the gold edge) updated. Shots `19a-debate-clash-won-e17`, `20a-debate-verdict-crowd-e17`, and every existing debate, election and results shot: 0 audit findings at phone, iPad and desktop.
## 0.17: the week's flow and the campaign screen (the designer's list, slice D)

### D-093 — The campaign screen: an action tray on landscape phones, the plan as slots, a clearer header, event strip and sheet, livelier results (approved list items 6 (UI), 23-30, 32, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-d`)

**Asked.** The designer's 0.17 list, week flow and campaign. Everything below is UI only; the stance timing (item 6) is D-099.

- **Actions without scrolling on a landscape phone (23).** Item 23 was written for portrait phones; on an 844 x 390 phone the state card's actions were 450-500 px below its title. Now, on a compact screen (`Kit.compact`, D-089), the card's six main actions are a 3 x 2 tray of 52 px buttons **first in the card**, above its title (the header's now line already names the state: "Pick an action in Ohio"), each with its short name and gain ("Rally / +4.1", "Ground / +1.5"; a greyed-out one says why in two words, "No money"). Opening a state scrolls the column to the top. *Rejected:* a tray pinned above the action bar (the side column under the header is about 228 px: the bar takes 84, a tray 92, which left 36 px for the card); a single row of six icon buttons (about 55 px each: no room for a word and its gain). iPad and desktop keep the six full buttons in their place.
- **The plan as slots (24).** `Words.planSlots` (pure, tested): one tile per action with its icon and state, repeats merged ("PA ×2"), an outlined empty tile for each free action slot, money-only actions (ads, polls, ground game) after them. They replace the action bar's wrapping "Plan: Rally OH · Ad PA" line (20 px tiles; the text stays behind them for agents and specs) and the plan card's "Your plan · 2 of 3 actions" title (44 px tiles; a tap removes that action, one unit of ground game at a time). The card keeps its detail rows (issue, flight) and Clear.
- **State card text (25).** Each action's effect is body size (16 px, was the 14 px caption floor), the poll names over the bar too; "Attack ad" and "Ground game" read "Attack" and "Ground" on the buttons (`Words.ACTIONS[k].short`; Help keeps the full names).
- **Header (26).** The candidates' faces stand beside both scores on every screen size (a landscape phone's names overlapped, so there only faces and numbers); "Week 3 of 12"; the money left is a gold coin and a plain number (green means a gain).
- **Event sheet (27).** It fits its content (between 40% and 50% of the screen; a landscape phone's sheet is full height, D-089), opens with a band in the event's color with a 56 px icon and "WINDFALL · THIS WEEK", and ends with "Got it".
- **Event strip (28).** Each kind has a color (Spotlight violet, Regional teal, Windfall gold, Choice green, closing weeks blue) on its plate and its left edge, and a "THIS WEEK" tag; next week's event says "NEXT WEEK" and is dimmed, with its name alone as the title.
- **Week results (29).** The "New polls are in" stamp shrinks to 60% after 1.2 s so the results move up; the reveal's coach tip shows once (seen as soon as it shows); a side that did nothing says "Skipped: no actions this week".
- **Results with more life (30).** The score already counted up with a "+12" chip (D-053); now a flip your way also flies a "+28" chip from the state to your score and drops confetti on the map, in the week's reveal and the stance result. Decorative, and none of it under Reduce motion (`Motion.reduced()`; the numbers still change).
- **Tutorial (32).** While nothing is picked, the tutorial pulses a suggested state every 1.6 s (Ohio when it is a key state or none are, else the first key state); none under Reduce motion. The first tap on a state closes whatever tip asked for it.
- **Idle money tip.** It stands at the top of the side column (it sat under the state card, below the fold on a phone); on a landscape phone it waits while a state's tray is open.
- **Underdog copy (D-096).** All the numbers: "Tier 2 drops to tier 1 only once the gap is 30 EV under its line; the boost ends as soon as the latest polls put you within 10 EV." News: "it ends once the polls show you level".
- **The stance result's screen (item 6, with D-099).** The reveal panel plays it in the reveal's style: "Both stances are in" over the polls before them (the Decision screen's View, kept as `Store.previous`), then "Your stance" and theirs with their totals, the map's fills moving to the new polls with every touched state badged, rings and FLIP on leader changes, the header counting to the new projection, the tally line and What changed. Skip ends it once both tap it. A rejoin without the polls before badges what the stances asked for.

**Config.** None (display constants only: StateCard `ACTION_TRAY_H` 52, `MAIN_TRAY` 6; PlanSlots tile sizes; Reveal `STAMP_SHRINK_AFTER` 1.2 s, `STAMP_SMALL` 0.6; Campaign `CONFETTI_N` 24, `FLY_SECONDS` 0.7, `SUGGEST_EVERY` 1.6 s; EventCard kind colors, `STRIP_TAG_W`, sheet `STRIP_SHEET_MIN` 0.4).

**Tests and shots.** `Campaign.spec` (the stance's result before the plan, the strip's NEXT WEEK tag), `Stances.spec` (the stance result from a rejoin and from the Decision step's polls), `RevealWords.spec`, `Explain.spec`. Snapshots `10s-plan-slots-d17`, `09w-event-sheet-d17`, `18a-stance-intro-d17`, `18b-stance-result-d17` at phone, iPad and desktop, 0 audit findings.

### D-099 — A Choice week's stances resolve as their step closes, before the plan (rule timing change approved by the user, Oct 29, 2026, branch `ui-d`)

**Asked.** List item 6: "Debate and choice results shown and tallied before the week is planned." Debates already were: each live round moves support and republishes the polls at once (D-072), and its verdict plays before the plan. A stance, though, was taken in its Decision step and applied only inside the week's resolve (step 5b, after the pivots), so its effect showed in the week's results after you had planned on polls that did not include it.

**Rule.** `Resolve.stances` runs as the Decision step closes (`Match.advance`, phase Decision): every player's stance (the hedge C by default) moves the states that react to it from one start-of-step snapshot (D-001, the same `Events.stanceEffect` arithmetic with each player's current platform and base), the public polls are republished from the new truth with the **same** error terms (`Polls.refresh`, as after a debate round), and the result goes in the public event log at once: both stances are public together from then on, to both seats. It draws nothing from the seed. Events: one "Choice" per player (its total points) and a "PollFlip" (issue "Stance") per state whose public leader changed; the log's `flips` stay the leader changes on the polls both players saw (`Polls.projectFlips`), as before. The week's resolve no longer applies stances (a direct call that skipped the step still resolves them once, at the old place). `PlayerState.stance` still holds your own until the week ends (the plan's event strip says it).
- *Platform:* a stance now reads the platform before this week's Swap issue, not after it (the preview on the Decision screen always used the current platform, so the stance now does what its preview said).
- *Server:* after the Decision step, a skippable Reveal "Stance" of `TIMER_STANCE_REVEAL` = **4 s** (new Config number) shows the result, then the plan opens with its full timer. Worst case: 8 Choice weeks x 4 s = 32 s, so the true worst case goes from 1,282 to 1,314 s (`Config.spec`); typical decks deal 3.3 Choice events, about 13 s a match, less when both skip.
- *Why not keep it in the resolve and only preview it?* The preview was already on the Decision screen; the ask is to see the real effect, map and tally, before planning on it.

**Measured** (`lune run tests/harness/balance.luau 2000`, the standard bot both sides, before → after): candidates Goldie 54.6 → 56.0, Buster 57.2 → 53.0, Mavis 54.2 → 52.4, Percy 44.4 → 46.7, Opal 50.3 → 49.5, Dash 47.9 → 46.6, Esme 42.0 → 42.0, Gus 49.4 → 53.6 (each ±4.4 at ~500 games: within noise); comeback 36.8 → 39.3%; median margin 48 → 50 EV; decided by 40+ / 100+ EV 59.6 / 17.3 → 58.9 / 16.8%; GOTV changes the winner 8.8 → 9.8%; debates change the winner 23.2 → 24.2%; stances change the winner 11.6 → 12.1% (9.5 → 10.0 EV swung); underdog to a true 20+ leader 8.5 → 8.4%, tier changes 2.70 → 2.64. RandomStance wins 26.8 → 27.1%. Red flags 5 → 4 (Buster and Percy back inside 45-55, Goldie out at 56.0; Esme, the most lopsided pair and GOTV stay red as before). Every other line within noise. The bots plan on the polls the stances made, which is the point.

**Tests.** `Decision.spec` ("the stances resolve before the plan": support moves only where the stances asked, polls republished with the same error terms, one Choice event per player and a PollFlip per leader change, not applied twice by the week, the seat-swap mirror, nothing drawn from the seed, the direct-call path, nothing outside a Choice week; the secrecy test now checks nothing is stored while the step is open and both become public together), `Session.spec` (the Stance reveal, its deadline, both skipping), `View.spec`, `Events.spec` and `Helpers.spec` (fixtures that crossed a Choice week), `Tutorial.spec` (D-080's pinned normal match re-pinned from week 3, result 292-246 → 333-205: the bots now plan on the polls after the stance), `ReviewFixes.spec` (a float comparison).
