### D-101 — Key states wear a solid gold edge instead of a glow and dashes (amends D-088's look; designer's pick from four rendered options, Oct 29, 2026, branch `keystroke`)

**Asked.** The designer said the key-state outline (D-088: a soft accent glow and a dashed white outline) "still looks bad" and, from four options rendered side by side on branch `orient-study` (a gold inner stroke, a gold wash, a gold target badge, a bright gold halo), picked the solid gold edge.

**What the player sees** (still only while planning; the rule, the line and the tips' timing are D-088's):
- *Map:* each key state wears a **solid gold edge, 3 px, just inside its own border** and inside whatever outline it already has (a home or stronghold ring, the selection, a plan preview's halo), so both read: the gold never replaces them. A 1 px dark keyline (the stage navy at 0.3) runs inside the gold so it stays apart from a pale fill (Amber, Teal). The edge is drawn over every other outline (`KEY_Z`), so a neighbor's ring never covers it. No glow, no dashes. A Northeast chip gets a solid gold plate around it. Labels, plates, ★, pins, plan badges and preview chips sit above the outlines as before (D-092's order). The circles fallback (`NodeMap`) draws a gold ring, no glow.
- *Swatches:* the legend row's "key state" mark and the Key states line's mark are the same gold box with the keyline.
- *Words:* Map key "Gold edge: a key state, where 270 is decided this week."; the tutorial tip "Gold-edged states decide 270. Put your rallies and ads there."; Help → Your turn "Gold-edged key states decide 270: aim there." The match tip is unchanged.

**The gold** (`Colors.keyGold` 255, 232, 110, a token in `Kit.colors`): the study's 255, 200, 52 was only 2.7 CIEDE2000 from the Amber player color. This paler, lemony gold is 12.3 from Amber (9.5 / 8.4 / 13.1 under simulated protanopia / deuteranopia / tritanopia, Machado 2009), 36 from the loss orange (23 / 16 / 29) and 30 from the white selection (17 under tritanopia, the closest). WCAG contrast of the edge: 6.7:1 on a toss-up's neutral, 4.2:1 on a full Crimson fill, 4.3:1 on full Cobalt, 5.8 / 5.3 on a lean Crimson / Cobalt; with color-blindness simulated it stays 3.4:1 or more on full Crimson and Cobalt. It is weak on the pale player colors (1.4:1 on full Amber, 2.4 on Teal), which is what the dark keyline is for (11.1:1 on full Amber, 6.3 on Teal).

**Every outline's corners.** A line drawn several px inside a state shows how its segments meet. Outlines are now mitered (each corner where the two inset lines cross, at most `MITER_MAX` 2 px per px of inset) and drop points where the border turns back on itself (more than about 148°, `SPIKE_COS` -0.85: Texas's barrier islands drawn as slivers of its coast), which tangled into spikes. The key edge also smooths the border to within `KEY_SIMPLIFY` 0.004 of the map's width and skips rings thinner than `KEY_MIN_RING` 0.008 (islands, the Keys). The selection, rings, halos and a missed poll's dashes get the cleaner corners too; nothing else about them changed.

**Config.** None. Display constants: ShapeMap `KEY_PX` 3, `KEY_LINE_PX` 1, `KEY_LINE_FADE` 0.3, `KEY_Z` 6, `MITER_MAX` 2, `SPIKE_COS` -0.85, `KEY_SIMPLIFY` 0.004, `KEY_MIN_RING` 0.008 (`KEY_BLOOM`, `KEY_PLATE` and NodeMap's `KEY_GLOW_FADE` are gone); Legend `KEY_LINE` 2 (now the gold edge), `KEY_INNER_FADE` 0.3.

**Tests and shots.** `KeyStates.spec` ("draws each key state with a solid gold edge over every outline, no glow": solid gold 3 px parts, above every other outline, Texas's selection kept, no bloom, the legend swatch gold). Snapshots `09-campaign-plan`, `09k-campaign-key-tip`, `10k-select-key-c17`, the lenses (`11`-`14`), the plan preview (`10d`, `10e`), the reveal (`16`, `17`) and the new `10k-key-amber-ks` (Amber and Teal, the palest player colors) at phone, iPad and desktop, 0 audit findings.
## Portrait phones

### D-100 — Phones play upright, tablets on their side; map first with a bottom sheet (designer decision, Oct 29, 2026, from the orientation study's verdict; UI only, no rule or Config change, branch `portrait`; supersedes D-089's landscape lock)

**Why.** The orientation study (branch `orient-study`, Fable's verdict) measured both ways with the real Roblox chrome. Landscape gives a phone 750 x 311 of game once the 58 pt top bar, the two 47 pt notch insets and the home bar are counted: a state's six actions, the stance's third option and the week's results all sat under the action bar. Portrait gives 390 x 705, a map nearly twice the size, and the pre-match screens fit without a scroll; it is also how the Roblox app is held when a game opens. The designer approved portrait for phones; the partner's iPad stays landscape.

**The lock.** `StarterGui.ScreenOrientation = Portrait` in `default.project.json` (applies from join). `App.client` asks for `LandscapeSensor` on a tablet: `Kit.isTablet(UserInputService.TouchEnabled, Camera.ViewportSize)`, a touch screen whose shorter side (notches left out) is at least `Kit.TABLET_MIN` = 600 pt (Android's sw600dp; the largest phones are about 440 pt across, the iPad mini 744). The shorter side does not change as the device turns, so it is re-asked on every viewport change with the same answer; it waits until the camera has a real size. Desktop ignores the property. Not `Sensor`: every screen would have to be right both ways and a turn mid-match would re-lay the board. The compact (landscape phone) layouts stay as dormant code (`--size phone-land` snapshots), the fallback if both ways are ever wanted. Checked against the creator docs: `enums/ScreenOrientation`, `classes/PlayerGui` (`ScreenOrientation`, `CurrentScreenOrientation`), `classes/StarterGui`, `classes/UserInputService` (`TouchEnabled`), `classes/Camera` (`ViewportSize`: the device safe area, top bar included).

**The phone viewport.** The `phone` snapshot size is **390 x 844** with the device's safe areas: 47 pt at the top (the notch) with the 58 pt top bar below it (105 in all) and 34 pt at the bottom (the home bar), so the `Game` ScreenGui (`CoreUISafeInsets`) gets 390 x 705 and the audit runs against that area (`FakeRoblox.Screen` and `Snapshot` model the insets; the renderer draws the notch and the home bar). *Assumption to check on a device:* that the portrait top bar sits below the notch rather than inside its band. From the `ScreenInsets` docs (CoreUISafeInsets = device safe area + the top bar) and `GuiService:GetInsetArea`'s documented landscape example (-59, -58, 792, 334 on an 852 x 393 phone, where the notch side and the bar add up). If the bar shares the notch's band the game is 47 px taller and nothing is lost. `docs/TESTING.md` asks for one `GetInsetArea` print on a real iPhone.

**The phone pattern: map first, a bottom sheet.**
- *Header (all match screens share `TopBar`):* two rows. "Week 3 of 12", sound, ? and the timer; then the score the full width, each side's face and number outside the race bar's ends (the two scores overprinted each other when squeezed between the week and the buttons). The Decision title is the event's name alone and keeps the score row; a debate round reads "Debate · Round 2/3".
- *Lens row:* the Map key "?", the Close races toggle (switch over word) and the four tabs (icon over word) sharing the rest equally by scale (`LensToggle.fillNarrow`); the toggle sat on the Votes tab.
- *Campaign:* the header, the event strip and the map with its lens row stay on screen, never scrolled. The side column is a sheet under the map (a raised panel, `SheetBack`) whose height is fixed per mode, not measured, so the map never jumps as text wraps: closed, the action bar plus the key states line and a coach tip (`SHEET_PEEK_MAX` 0.5 of the body at most); a tapped state, `SHEET_CARD` 0.67 (the title, the poll and the six actions as a 3 x 2 tray, then Lock in; the event strip and the lens row step aside while it is open); a reveal, `SHEET_REVEAL` 0.58 (the results card and its steps; the lens row steps aside, the map keeps the rest). The map shrinks into the room above the sheet, so the whole map and the selected state stay in view; the rest of the column (plan, week card, all states) scrolls inside the sheet. *Rejected:* the sheet over the map's lower half at full size (the selected state, Texas or Florida, and the Northeast chips went under it) and the old tall scroll (the actions were 542 px below the fold).
- *Coach tips:* first in the closed sheet, just over the map's bottom edge, never under the bar; after the card while one is open. The idle-money tip waits while a card is open, as on a landscape phone.
- *Stance:* the three options above Lock in: the event's story and map note move under them.
- *Debate:* no map in the intro, a round or the clash (all three moves, the Zinger and the whole clash in view); the verdict keeps it, over the result.
- *Small cuts:* the end screen's player cards on a phone say the first name, "Rival" and "2 topics won" ("Goldie Pennywhistl", "Opponent · 28 stat" were cut); the Lobby's one-row Daily card says "5h 11m · 3-day" after the flame (`Words.Daily.streakShort`; "3-day strea" was cut).
- *Corner settings (Lobby):* the corner never gets wider than its screen (an admin's row poked 30 px past the left edge); when the tooltip has no room beside the toggles it shows under them.

**Config.** None. Display constants: `Kit.TABLET_MIN` 600; TopBar `SCORE_ROW` 30, `SCORE_SIDE`; Campaign `SHEET_REVEAL` 0.58, `SHEET_CARD` 0.67, `SHEET_PEEK_MAX` 0.5, `SHEET_PAD`, `SHEET_TIP_H` 100 (replacing `PORTRAIT_MAP_MAX` 0.57).

**Tests.** `KitStyle.spec` (`Kit.isTablet` for phones and tablets held either way, the project's Portrait lock and App's tablet branch), `PortraitPhone.spec` (the header's two rows, the lens row, the card sheet with all six actions above Lock in and the map above it, a tip's Got it above the bar, the three stances above Lock in, a debate round without the map and the verdict with it; positions from the new `Snapshot.boxes`, the renderer's layout with list layouts, which the fake engine's AbsolutePosition ignores), `DailyRace.spec` (the phone's short streak). `Rig.PHONE_PORTRAIT` is the phone as it plays; `Rig.PHONE` stays the landscape size for the dormant compact layouts' specs. Snapshots: every shot at the new phone size (390 x 844 with insets), iPad and desktop with 0 audit findings; iPad and desktop pictures unchanged.
